  - **Message editors' drafts.** The text typed into an edit (`version_edit`, keyed by the version edited) or into "write your own version" (`scratchpad`, keyed by the slot) is a server draft like a summary's: `SaveMessageDraft` (thin over `SaveDraft`), `DiscardMessageDraft` (over `ClearDraft`, its text to the recovered texts), and `MessageDrafts`, a chat's drafts with text (`ListMessageDrafts` in the reader). `EditVersion`, `WriteVersion` and `AmendVersion` take the `draft` the text came from (id and session) and commit it in the same transaction, only if it holds exactly that text (`drafts.ts` `commit`).
  - **Message history** (`messageHistory.test.ts`). `VersionAmendments` reads all earlier saved texts of an owned, live, unpurged human-written version from `version_history`, newest first. `RestoreVersionAmendment` resolves an amendment belonging to that version and calls the existing `AmendVersion` with the viewed fork and expected version row count; it preserves the current text in history, copies when another fork shares the version, and publishes the updated slot. Generated-source edits still use `EditVersion`, creating a new version. `MessageDraftHistory` and `RestoreMessageDraft` use the common owner-checked draft store for version edits, scratchpads and queue edits. The shared message controller uses these APIs for all three targets; text typed during a history restore stays in the box and its next save and browser copy use the returned draft row version. Message and summary history controls read up to 1,001 states (the 1,000 retained patches plus their starting text), and show an empty history before the first draft save. A restore is another undoable history step, with the draft's id and row version checked; commit/discard still ends that session's history.
  - `SelectVersion` (in a fork, against the version the tab saw it show, §3.8), and `AmendVersion` for a finished user-written version (`NotAmendable` otherwise; DESIGN §3.1), in the fork named (`forkId`): in place, or when another fork shows it, as a copy in this fork only (§3.8), which the tab then reads again. `ListVersions` and `SetVersionFlags` take the fork too. A version's `rowVersion` is one more than the number of times it was amended, counted from `version_history`, since `versions` has no `row_version` column.
  - `StopGeneration` stops one generation and returns the version as saved. A Stop that loses the race with the natural finish, or comes twice, gets the finished version too; NotFound means it isn't the user's or is in the bin.
  - `DeleteMessage` and `DeleteVersion` (in a fork, `forkId`, else the fork through them) and `DeleteFork`: to the bin (§3.11). Generations under what was deleted are stopped.
- **`SubscribeChat`:** a stream of event batches, below.

**Windows of a path** (`ReadPath`, `db/reads.ts`). The client never loads a whole chat: it reads windows of up to 500 slots and pages from cursors.
- `Bottom` and `Older` read the window ending at a slot by chain segment, as `GetPath` does (§3.8): the cost is the window.
- `Newer` walks down from a slot the client already has, towards the leaf. A run of only children needs no check; at the first branch, the rest of the window is the leaf's ancestors, found in one walk up from the leaf, which costs its distance from the leaf once per page. Walking up once per branch instead cost 7 s for a page of 60 branch points 99,000 messages from the leaf (5 s at 70,000, 0.7 s at 10,000); one walk costs 150, 110 and 30 ms, and a page without a branch about 10 ms (the 100,000-message fixture, bundled workers, a loaded host). `pathWalks` counts the walks up, and a test checks that a page through 20 branches takes one. The slot isn't checked against the path (that would cost its distance from the leaf on every page): a slot of the chat that isn't on it walks down its own branch.
- A read names a fork (`forkId`; the current fork when null), whose tip is the path's leaf, or a leaf of its own (`leafSlotId`, as `useForks` scans a path). The window says which fork it read (`forkId`, null for a read by leaf).
- `Around` opens at a slot (deep links, DESIGN §10.1), about half the window above it. It checks that the slot is on the path, a walk from the leaf to it, once per open. A slot on another path opens the fork through it that was current most recently instead (`forkThrough`), or with none, the path through it following the newest reply at each step (through a message deleted on its own, never into a deleted branch); `forkId` and `leafSlotId` say which.
- Deleted messages are walked through but not returned; the cursors (`older`, `newer`) still move on.

**Branches** (DESIGN §3.2). Each path entry carries an optional `branchName` (migration `0039_branch_names`, stored on the slot that starts that alternate), plus `branchCount` and `branchIndex`: its slot and the siblings in its lane that a path can reach (live, or deleted on its own with replies left in place, what `Around` follows), and its place among them by creation. Reachability walks arbitrary chains of individually deleted placeholders by the parent index, stopping at a live descendant or a deleted subtree root. The subtree marker survives purge, so restoring a separately deleted descendant cannot expose that subtree. Live siblings need no descendant walk. Two counts per entry, by the parent's index (first messages by chat and depth), so a window costs the number of siblings more, not the chat's length. The indexes are named (`INDEXED BY`): without statistics SQLite took the lane's, scanning the whole lane per entry, which made a 60-slot window of the 100,000-message perf fixture take 5 s instead of 0.3 ms; `test/graph.test.ts` checks the plan. `SwitchBranch`, in the writer, takes the sibling at the index asked for and the fork through it (§3.8): the one current most recently, so the alternate's own line opens, at its tip. It moves nothing and makes nothing current; the client opens the fork it names. The composer stays with each fork: switching carries no text (DESIGN §6.2). `test/branches.test.ts`. A slot's `branchName` is the name the user gave (or took off) the fork it starts (`PathEntry.startsForkId`: the slot that fork appended first, at the message it was made at), else its own label (an ST import's, or a continuation's name from before forks), else that fork's name; `PathEntry.forkId` is the fork that appended it. `NameFork` names a fork (blank clears it), advances its first message's row version and publishes that `Slot`; a message that starts no fork (one that continues its parent's line) can't be named in unit 2. `ListBranches` lists reachable siblings with names and message previews in the same order as `SwitchBranch`. All are owner-scoped. The header menu shows names instead of Branch k, offers Name/Rename where a fork starts and Delete this branch for each shown fork, and uses the existing bin/Undo flow. Deletion switches to the preceding surviving sibling (the next for the first), fences older window reads, and clears the deleted slot/leaf from the URL while keeping its panel. If no sibling remains it follows the writer's surviving active path. This also works from a window in the middle of a long chat. `test/naming.test.ts`, the isolation matrix and `e2e/naming.spec.ts` cover naming, grouping, branch deletion and restoration at both widths.
- Which branch to land on below the sibling is the newest reply at each step, as for a deep link; `slots.last_visited_at` isn't kept, so a sub-branch visited last isn't remembered [P].
- A message deleted on its own at a fork counts as a branch while its replies stay; on its own path, its replies show without the switcher for that fork.

**Live updates** (`chat/ChatHub.ts`). Every committed change is published to the chat's subscribers, from any tab of the user:
- `Slot` (a message added, or its selection or versions changed, as one fork shows it: `shownForkId`; a tab showing another fork reads the slot again as its own fork shows it), `Version` (a generation ended, an amend, or a star or picker flag; with `forkId` when it's as one fork shows it, which tabs on another fork leave; an amend that gave the fork a copy also publishes that fork's `Slot`), `Chat` (the leaf or last message moved), `Reload` (something in the chat was deleted or restored), `Deleted` (the chat went to the bin; it comes after the `Version` events of the generations that stopped, and the subscription ends with it), `Cache` (the cache-warmth estimate, §15). The chat is checked once the subscriber is registered, so a chat deleted in between refuses the subscription rather than leaving it open. A client showing a deleted chat goes back to the chat list.
- Streamed text comes as `Delta { versionId, offset, text }`, and reasoning as `Reasoning` with the same fields, never as whole messages (CLIENT-FRAMEWORK §5). Their separate offsets let a client tell a repeat from a gap; batching coalesces each kind separately.
- Each subscriber gets a chat's events in batches at most every 16 ms. Consecutive deltas of a version are merged, and any other event is a barrier, so order holds.
- **No events are lost between loading and subscribing.** Each subscription starts with `Hello`, which carries the text and reasoning so far of every version generating in the chat, taken in the same synchronous step that registers the subscriber. A client subscribes first, then reads its windows, and applies events idempotently (by id, row version and offset). A second `Hello` means the socket reconnected: the client reads its windows again. This replaces the per-chat sequence number EFFECT.md §5 proposed: nothing needs replaying, since state comes from reads and live text from `Hello`.
- Publishing never blocks: events are queued per chat, and one timer sends the batches. Each subscriber may fall 512 batches behind (about eight seconds of a busy chat; a stalled socket or a throttled tab), then it starts over: its backlog is dropped and it gets a fresh `Hello`, which makes the client read its windows again, as after a reconnect.

**Generation** (`chat/Generations.ts`). One fiber per generating version, in the service's scope, any number at once and several under one slot.
- The **generator** (`chat/Generator.ts`) is an interface: given a chat position, a stream of text strings and separate reasoning chunks. The server's is the context assembler in front of the provider layer (`chat/ContextGenerator.ts`, §15). A server built without one (only tests) refuses `Generate` and `Regenerate` with `GenerationUnavailable` before anything is written.
- Text and reasoning accumulate separately on the main thread and are published as deltas. Every 2 seconds both are saved (`SaveGeneration`, status still `generating`), so a crash keeps most of it.
- **How a generation ends:** complete; `stopped` by the user, with its text; `interrupted` when the server stops (the scope closes and the fiber's exit handler saves it); `failed` with the generator's error class and message, and the text so far (DESIGN §3.1). Continue creates a new version with the old text and streams onto its end (DESIGN §7.5); the original stays in the picker.
- **Length cutoffs** (DESIGN §3.1; backlog U08): a provider `length` stop on a reply request is `ProviderError(length_cutoff)`, so the request, rollout and retained version fail with an explicit output-limit cause. Text and reasoning already received remain on that version, including when display streaming is off (the reply strategy flushes its buffer before propagating the failure). Failed versions stay out of ordinary context, including while selected. Continue uses their partial text through the existing prefill flow and creates a new version; the failed original remains. Other request purposes retain their existing length handling, including the summarizer’s truncated-output marker. The soft judge’s mark-only behavior is unchanged. `test/continueReasoning.test.ts` covers both streaming modes, exclusion at the mock provider and Continue; `e2e/backlogBindingsCutoff.spec.ts` covers the failure cause and Continue at 1440 and 390 in both themes.
- At startup, versions a crashed process left `generating` become `interrupted` (`InterruptGenerations`).
- **Nothing blocks the event loop.** Text arrives in chunks, publishing is a queue push, and saving is a message to the writer worker. `test/chats.test.ts` runs six streams at 300 tokens a second and checks the event-loop monitor's maximum stays under 100 ms on this shared host.

**Chats from cards, speakers and settings** (`@rp/shared/chatSetupApi.ts`, `api/chatSetup.ts`, `db/chatSetupWrites.ts`, `db/chatSetupReads.ts`, `db/cards.ts`; DESIGN §2.1, §2.2, §3.5).
- **Cards as chats see them.** A card is read from its upstream ref, or its oldest variant without one: the in-chat name and greetings from the document, the library label, and the persona folder (the `persona` tag). A chat's own `card.binding` isn't applied here; the context assembler resolves that. `ListCards` lists the live ones, `SetPersona` moves one in or out of the persona folder, and `GetCardForChat` returns its ordinary greetings (`first_mes`, then the alternates, empty ones left out), group-only greetings separately, and the fields its macros read.
- **Chats are made on the first turn, never before** (DESIGN §2.1, PLAN phase 2). Opening a card shows its greeting with nothing written (`/cards/$entityId/chat`), so greeting-only chats can't pile up, and `chats.status = 'pending'` isn't used. `StartChat` writes, in one transaction: the chat; `participant.join` for each card and the persona at the start, and `played_by_me.set` for the persona; each card's chosen greeting as its own message in card order, with every alternate greeting as a version of that message (origin `greeting`, `macro_mode = template`, `greeting_ref` with the card and its index), the one picked selected; then the first turn, the user's message (the client then asks for the reply, as after any send) or a reply with its direction. The title is the one asked for (a group's new chat), else the cast's names, or one name and the date, never just the first character's name.
- **A new chat of a group** (`/groups/$chatId/new`, `StartGroupChatPage` in `StartChatPage.tsx`, named by any of the group's chats) is the same pending chat with the group's characters (`ListGroupChats`): each member greets in order, with its card's greetings to pick from independently. Like ST (`getGroupChat` and `getFirstCharacterMessage` in `group-chats.js`), each starts at a random nonempty greeting and cards without greetings are skipped. The pending list scrolls while the composer remains visible. `StartChat.greeters` keeps each preview's selected index and frozen macros; without that payload, the server still greets with every card in order (random choices for a group unless its first greeting was explicitly requested, the requested greeting for a single card). **Playing as** picks the persona as for a card; **Reply from** picks who answers a human-written first message (the first character by default); Generate writes the selected **Writing as** speaker. The first send is `StartChat` with the pending cast (initially every member) and the group's full name as the title (the same unrestricted string as `CreateChat`), so the chat is one of the group's (`ChatGroup`: the same readable name and characters) and the tile and page count it (`e2e/chatSetup.spec.ts`).
- **Group-only greetings** (2026-10-30). The pending chat has Add character and Remove controls before the first send, including re-adding an original group member. Cast, persona and greeting picks update together against the latest state, so a delayed Add character read cannot restore an older selection. Adding a second card also exposes the group header and Reply from picker. `GetCardForChat` returns ordinary greetings and CCv3 `groupGreetings` separately, with empty entries omitted. The shared scoped `greetingSources` registry (`@rp/shared/greetings.ts`) registers `greetings.ordinary` and `greetings.group-only`; plugins can replace or disable either source. The preview and API macro freezing use `offeredGreetings`; the API passes the resolved option text, card indexes and frozen macros over the private writer RPC, so scoped source additions, overrides and disablement survive source and bundled worker boundaries without re-evaluating a separate registry. The worker-owned `StartChat` transaction checks ownership and stores that snapshot. Ordinary greetings stay available, and group-only greetings join them only with more than one distinct character card, excluding the selected persona. Each selected group-only greeting says “group greeting”. Removing a second character (or changing persona so only one character remains) resets an unavailable selection to the card's first available ordinary greeting and shows a visible note. The chosen greeting is stored as a normal template greeting version, with its frozen macros and original card index; all offered greetings become versions. Group-only greetings are never stored as available versions in a one-character new chat. ST group-chat import is unchanged. Unit/server tests cover availability, fallback, persona exclusion, duplicate ids, and source override/disable/restoration through both source and bundled workers; `e2e/groupGreetings.spec.ts` exercises adding/removing a character, a delayed card read, choosing the first reply speaker and storing a chosen group greeting at 1440 and 390 pixels in light and dark, plus re-adding an original group member.
- **Macros in display** (`features/chat/macros.ts`) go through `expand()` (§10), which decides from the stored macro mode. A greeting resolves against the cast at its own position (`GetCast` after its parent): `{{user}}` follows the persona picked until the first send, and a later change of who the user plays doesn't rewrite it. Its speaker card's fields (`{{description}}` and the like) come from the card as the preview had them. Its random and time macros are frozen as the preview drew them: `StartChat` stores each greeting's drawn values in `frozen_macros`, and display passes them back. The server freezes every greeting itself (`chat/macros.ts`), with the names the preview used: it keeps what the preview drew for the picked greeting where the macros could have drawn it, and draws everything else, so no greeting is left volatile and a card's greeting count is no limit. The bounds are one expansion's (at most 10,000 values and 16,000,000 characters, above what its work limit lets it draw), so whatever a preview drew is accepted, so a greeting reads the same tomorrow (DESIGN §5.6).
- **Single persona selection** (DESIGN §2.2, owner 2026-10-29). The `played_by_me.set.entities` and `playedByMe` arrays retain their public types, but the graph and ST-import writers persist only the last ID after ownership validation. The state fold also takes only the last ID, including in supplied initial state; cast assembly and prompt-name reads independently select one. `PlayedByMe` accepts an empty array to clear the selection. New-chat creation and the `Persona` control write zero or one ID; fork reads use the same fold, so selection stays independent by path and fork. Historical message speakers stay unchanged. No migration is needed for empty databases.

- **The cast** (`GetCast`) folds participants, presence, played-by-me, card bindings, role projection, the global books the chat turned off and its lorebook placement setting at the viewed position. Cards resolve as assembly does, including in-chat names. Model and preset are account settings: the compatibility fields in `Cast` read as null. The old `ChangeCast` model/preset variants change account settings after checking chat ownership and write no graph events. Other cast changes still write state at the displayed leaf and publish `State`. No explicit per-chat model or preset lock is implemented.
- **Text typed while the first send makes the chat** goes along to the new chat's composer (`composerCarry.ts`).
- **Message headers on phones** (`Message.tsx`) put the full wrapping speaker name on its own row, with time, number, streaming status and the visible Stop button below. Desktop headers retain their compact row.
- **Text typed in a chat not made yet** (`StartChatPage.tsx`) is kept in this browser's copies (`backup.ts`, kind `pendingChat`, under `pending-chat:<card>` per user, or `pending-group-chat:<characters>:<name>` per group, whose copies link back to `/groups/$chatId/new`), replaced as it's typed and dropped when the box is emptied; while the first send is out, the copy holds the sent text and what's typed since, as a failed send leaves them. Coming back to the card's pending chat puts the newest copy in the box, unless something's been typed already. A successful first send acknowledges only its submitted text. Any text typed meanwhile replaces the pending copy atomically with a `pendingChat` handoff carrying the saved `chatId` (`composerCarry.ts`), and navigation waits for that browser transaction. Browser recovery links that copy to the saved chat. Its opening composer replaces the handoff atomically with a normal `composer` copy naming the draft's id, row version and session, then replays that exact save; refusal or reload during either step keeps a recoverable copy. Both card and group flows use this handoff; there is no in-memory-only carry. Before that send, prose also has the private `user_field` server draft described in §3.10; creating the chat remains lazy.
- **Pending composers** (`StartChatPage.tsx`) use the same compact input row as the saved chat: **Generate** and a Send icon (`ui/Tooltip.tsx` `SquareIconButton`), retaining the accessible names **Generate X**, **Send as X** (or **Send** without a persona) and the send tooltip. Both share `ui/AutoGrowTextarea.tsx` and `composerKeys.ts`: the input grows with its text and width changes; The account’s Enter sends choice controls plain Enter; Shift+Enter inserts a newline and Ctrl/Cmd+Enter always submits. Generate takes the text as the first turn's direction only with guidance on (below); otherwise it leaves the text in the box. With guidance on, Send gives way to **Generate X** as the primary action, Enter submits it, and the text leaves the box as a sent message does (a failure puts it back); only text typed meanwhile goes along to the new chat's composer. Its label truncates to the available width for long speaker names, retaining the full accessible name. **Writing as** always shows the selected speaker and opens the same cast-only picker: the selected persona plus this card or the group's cards. **Playing as** independently picks `{{user}}` from the library. Both Send and Generate write the Writing-as speaker; guidance changes only the writer. `StartChat` stores that speaker in the fork's composer choice in its creation transaction, so the saved composer keeps it. First-turn creation and browser-copy handoff remain lazy. The speaker, guidance and action contributions are registered through `pendingComposerControls` from the chat feature, with keyboard invocation resolving the same registration as the buttons. `e2e/pendingChatHandoff.spec.ts` covers both widths and both pending routes, keyboard/touch submission, growth, tooltip and browser restoration.
- **The composer** (`Composer.tsx`): the always-visible speaker chip and up to three recent-speaker avatars, the growing input, History, **Write for <name>**, and Send, with the **Guidance** switch under the input. The speaker controls sit above the input on phones. Write for has a tooltip explaining that the model writes the selected speaker's turn, including a persona's. Send with text sends as that speaker, then asks the character selected in **Reply from**; with no text it asks for that character's reply alone (the accessible name and tooltip say “<name> replies”); with no one to reply (every character muted) Send posts the message and asks for nothing. Enter submits according to the account’s Enter sends preference (Automatic by default); Ctrl/Cmd+Enter always submits and Shift+Enter adds a line.
  - **Header space.** Chat portraits and title share the first row; branch and registered panel/action controls occupy a second row on desktop as well as phones, so group names retain room to read. The QA browser regression checks actual title clipping and horizontal overflow at 1440 and 390 px.
  - **Historical speakers.** `GetCast.cards` retains cards named by earlier joins, leaves, played sets and swaps on the viewed fork, including departed personas. Active participants and the played set remain separate. Message rows resolve the selected version's own speaker (or another version's / the first version's speaker for legacy versions without one) against those cards, keeping names and portraits after persona changes, impersonation and hand-written versions, including after reload. `e2e/qaAttribution.spec.ts` exercises persona switching through the picker, departed cast, written impersonation versions and portrait opening at 1440 and 390 px, in light and dark.
  - **Persona and speaker are separate** (DESIGN §2.2, owner 2026-10-27). Picking a speaker (the chip, a recent avatar, Cast → Write as) only sets the composer's speaker, from the chat's cast (owner 2026-10-28: to speak as another card, add it to the chat first); it never writes `played_by_me`, so `{{user}}` and the cached prefix stay as they are whoever the user writes as. Who `{{user}}` is changes only with the persona control (the Cast tab's "You:", the pending page's Playing as). The composer's default speaker stays the persona (`defaultSpeaker`). `test/assembler.test.ts` checks that sending as the persona, as a member, and generating as a member give byte-identical messages before the history breakpoint.
  - **Guidance** (`DisplayPrefs.guidance`, per account, default off; DESIGN §2.2). On, the box reads as guidance: the `guidance`/`guidance-surface` tokens, a "Guidance" label and the placeholder "Direction for the next message…"; Send gives way to **Write for <speaker>** as the primary action (a spark icon), and Enter (Ctrl/Cmd+Enter on touch) submits it with the text as its `direction`: guidance changes only who writes, never which character (owner 2026-10-28), so the model writes the selected speaker's message, a persona's (an impersonation) or a card's alike, never the Reply-from character's. Submitted guidance leaves the box as a send does: `Generate` names the composer draft it came from (`composer`), which commits it, and text typed while the request is out stays; the pending page clears it the same way. Each reply shows the direction it was written with under it (the `message.direction` slot). Off, Generate never takes the text, which stays in the box. The Continue button takes the box's text with guidance on, and none with it off; Alt+Enter always preserves and ignores it. A Generate onto a reply still being written is queued with its direction as before.
  - **Guidance on swipes** (`guidanceEdit.tsx`; DESIGN §2.2, owner 2026-10-29). A swipe (a message's Regenerate, the composer's regenerate icon, Retry with, Regenerate with) never takes the composer's text: the client sends no `direction`, and the server reuses the re-rolled version's, guidance switch on or off. The direction under a reply has **Edit**, and a model-written reply without one has **Add guidance** in its menu (`message.add-guidance`). Either opens the chat session's `GuidanceEditor` on that slot: the box shows the version's guidance (or nothing), typing changes the editor's text and never the composer draft, the guidance switch shows on and is disabled meanwhile without writing the preference, and a bar in the composer's `banner` placement reads "Editing guidance for <speaker>'s reply" with ✕. While it's open, Enter and the primary **New version** button make a new version of that reply with the box's text as its `direction` (empty for none), and the text stays; that reply's own swipe controls send the box's current text too, while any other reply's swipe reuses its own and Continue of another reply takes none. The composer answers swipes and Continue through `ChatSession.provideGuidance` (`composerGuidance`). ✕ closes the editor and the box shows the draft again; since the draft was never written meanwhile, a reload shows it too, and the edit itself doesn't survive a reload. The bar also closes when the reply leaves the loaded path (deleted, or out of a 500-message window), the view goes to another fork, or the chat changes (a new session). The draft's History button is hidden while editing. Editing and New version need only the loaded target and its fork; unrelated composer speaker/turn-order reads and attachment uploads do not block them. Enter and the button share the same readiness guard.
  - **Tests:** `e2e/rpLoop.spec.ts` (the realistic synthetic folder through a fake provider, at 1440 and 390 px, light and dark: the guidance look, Enter generating the selected speaker's turn with the direction, the box cleared, a swipe reusing the re-rolled version's guidance (not the box's text) with guidance on and off, the toggle across a reload, the inspector for a directed reply and an impersonation, the persona control on Personas and unchanged by picking a speaker, the muted member never offered to reply, the editor's conditions, generation types, badges and warnings, the one-time notice; a touch profile's Ctrl+Enter; the pending page's persona control and directed first reply), `e2e/composerSpeaker.spec.ts` (the speaker switcher lists only the cast and never adds a card), `e2e/guidanceEdit.spec.ts` (edit → submit → edit → submit → ✕ giving back the draft, reload both during and after editing, at 1440 and 390 px, light and dark; both widths also cover held speaker-choice reads, live swipes of the edited and another reply, empty guidance, Add guidance, deletion and chat navigation), `e2e/chatSetup.spec.ts`, `e2e/cast.spec.ts`; `test/chats.test.ts` (a swipe reuses the re-rolled version's direction unless given one; Continue carries only one given), `test/impersonationNotice.test.ts`; client `test/guidanceEdit.test.ts` (what a swipe and Continue are asked with, the editor, Edit, Add guidance, the bar).
  - **The impersonation notice** (DESIGN §2.2): after Write for a card the user plays, the composer asks `ImpersonationNotice`. For an account preset with no enabled prompt conditioned `as_user` (the imported `impersonationPrompt` counts; the built-in preset has one), the server records the preset in `user_prefs` key `notices` (`MarkNoticeShown`, one writer transaction, answering whether this call recorded it) and the composer says once, per preset per account [owner question 12], that its rules against writing for `{{user}}` may need "not when writing as `{{user}}`", with a link to the preset. The separate “Regenerate the latest reply” icon still makes a version at the tip. Empty/loading placeholders stay short, and the textarea refits after placeholder changes, so a loaded phone composer returns to one line.
  - **Width** (`Composer.tsx`, a container query): the speaker controls sit left of the input, and above it when the composer's container is narrower than 640 px (phones, and desktop chat columns narrowed by resizing a sidebar).
  - **Reply from** (`ReplyFrom.tsx`, `cast.ts`) shows the present characters' faces and names, excluding cards the user plays and, when there are alternatives, the composer's own speaker. One tap picks the next reply; its status names the character and says why. The default is round-robin in cast order after the most recent character's turn, wrapping and skipping muted and played cards; without a previous character turn it starts with the first eligible member. `composerTurn.ts` reads backwards from the branch tip in 60-message windows until it finds that turn, independently of the scrolled message window. Its cached history follows the session's path revision: Slot and Version events, Reload and reconnects invalidate it even when the tip stays unchanged or the changed turn is outside the loaded window. Streamed token deltas do not invalidate it; sending waits for the current history read. A pick lasts for one reply, survives reload until used, and offers Back to turn order. Consumption names its original draft and card, and clears only a matching pick, so an in-flight send cannot reset a different pick or another branch. With fewer than two eligible members the extra row is omitted. Cast → Speak now remains available.
  - **Persisted choices** (`speaker.ts`, `@rp/shared/composerApi.ts`): migration `0036_composer_choice` adds nullable speaker and reply-from card ids to the draft row, with owner triggers, foreign keys and purge cleanup. `GetComposerChoice` reads by draft id; `SetComposerChoice` updates only named fields in the writer transaction without changing the text's row version/history. The API publishes `ChatEvent.ComposerChoice` to all tabs. Choices travel with a moving draft; branch copies and reopening restore them. The client rereads on reconnect, serializes rapid picks and rereads after writes to catch a newer tab's event. Send is disabled until the current draft's choices and turn order are loaded and its choice is saved; typing depends only on the text draft being ready, so a slow choice read never blocks writing. With no saved choice, the speaker defaults to the first played card.
  - **Speaker search** (`SpeakerSwitcher.tsx`, `ui/Popover.tsx`, `ui/Tabs.tsx`): the chat's cast only, the persona and the cards in the chat (owner 2026-10-28), with no tabs; the recent-speaker avatars beside the chip are the cast's too. Search is focused on open, filters by name or library label without case/accent sensitivity, and puts recent speakers then the cast first for an empty query. Up/Down, Home/End and Enter navigate and pick; Escape closes. TanStack Virtual renders only visible rows plus the active option, so hundreds of cards remain reachable with keyboard and scrolling. The search (and the persona control's tabs) stay above the scrolling list on phones. Picking never changes the cast or the played set. With `persona`, the same switcher is the persona control, from the library: Members / Personas / All cards, opening on Personas every time, and a card not in the chat says "joins the chat". In-chat names and avatars take precedence over library values. Pointer activation of any tab, including the remembered active tab, returns focus to search through the stock Tabs callback; keyboard navigation retains focus on the tabs.
  - **Tests:** `e2e/composerSpeaker.spec.ts` covers round-robin replies, a saved one-reply pick, empty Send, writing a persona's turn, reload and live cross-tab speaker sharing, search and keyboard control, a one-card chat, and a phone with 240 additional cards, a fully readable named phone action, and a deep link whose last character turn is beyond the loaded window, and typing while a saved-choice request is held. `test/composerChoice.test.ts` covers draft movement/copy, independent branch resets and conditional consumption; `test/isolation.test.ts` covers both choice RPCs. Deletion/restoration of earlier turns inside and outside the loaded window, the resulting Send/empty Send speaker, and remembered-tab pointer focus with keyboard tab navigation are covered at both widths. Client `test/session.test.ts` guards path invalidation without token or read-replay invalidation. Client `test/speaker.test.ts` covers writes crossing branch switches and concurrent tab events.
- **The Cast tab** (`CastTab.tsx`, DESIGN §2.1, §3.5, §4; ST's group and character panels): at the top, **You:** the persona control [owner question 9]: picking a card sets `played_by_me = [card]` from the leaf on, joining it first if it isn't in the chat, and a persona-folder card played until then leaves the chat (it isn't one of the characters): the persona's definitions swap, as in ST, and the history is untouched, its earlier messages keeping its name in the request (§15 "`roles.projection`", owner 2026-10-28). It's one `ChangeCast` `Persona` change: the server reads the cast at the leaf and writes the join, `played_by_me.set` and leaves with `AddStateEvents`, one transaction, so a failure leaves the persona as it was (`test/chatSetup.test.ts`). Then everyone in the chat at the leaf shown, each with their avatar, display name, version, "used by N chats", "your persona" and "muted" (dimmed), and a **Muted** switch (`Presence`, inverted; none on a card the user plays, which the model never writes, and the count at the top ("N characters") leaves those cards out). **Muting** (DESIGN §3.5) is the existing `presence.set` event [owner question 8]: a muted character is never picked to reply automatically (Reply from's default and round-robin, the default speaker the assembler picks when a version has none), but Speak now, Write as and an explicit Reply from pick still make it speak (Reply from shows muted faces struck through, `replyFaces`, never as next in turn), and its card and notes stay in the context. The header counts "N muted". The UI says nothing of presence. Each row has **Speak now** (the model writes their turn now: `Generate` for them without a direction; the row says "writing…" while it does), **Write as** (the composer's speaker, and the cursor in the composer), **Show card** (the definition this chat uses, read-only: description, personality, scenario, first message, example dialogue, creator's notes, long ones folded with "Show more", the empty ones named in one line), **Edit**, **Fork for this chat**, **Use another version**, and a menu with "Use as my persona" (as the persona control) and "Remove from chat" (`Leave`). **Add character** is a searchable list of the user's cards not in the chat, persona-folder cards marked, and joins the one picked. One line at the top says changes apply from the latest message on, on this branch. `ListChatCards` reads, per card, at the leaf shown: its single library ref, "used by N chats" for it (§14.2), whether the chat pins a `revision` or uses the `default` head, and the definition the next request reads, resolved by the assembler's rules (`cardInChat`: a pinned revision, else the upstream head). A card that isn't the user's live card (another user's, or binned) is `NotFound`, as a missing one; the tab asks only for live cards. It reads again at a new leaf and after any library change. Where it reads an earlier revision, the definition shown says so.
  - **Card editing and forks** (DESIGN §4, `CardForkDialogs.tsx`): the Cast rows and each active card's own chat-panel tab share **Edit**, **Fork for this chat** and **Use another version**, alongside the usage count. The card tab reads its action metadata in the viewed fork at its leaf, as the Cast tab does. **Full editor** opens this card's library editor over the chat's column, in the chat's editor workspace (§14.6, preset overlays), where changes affect every chat using it; without the workspace (a plugin removed it) it navigates to the editor page as before. **Fork for this chat** opens a dialog with the display name, version (focused) and in-character name, plus a choice to move unsaved editor drafts (name drafts stay on the source). The display name and version must differ from every other live or archived card's pair; the client blocks a clash and the writer checks again atomically. The dialog captures its fork and position when opened. **Fork and edit** calls `ForkCardForChat` and opens the new card in the editor. The copy is an ordinary library card. **Use another version** shows the entire family as an indented tree with versions, usage counts and disabled binned/purged members; picking one calls the existing `ChangeCast` `Swap`. This also switches back to the parent. The old Only in this chat / Everywhere chooser, card override RPC and override metadata are removed.
- **The side panel** (`ChatPanels.tsx`, as ST's panels sit beside its chat): two rows of navigation (`ui/PanelTabs.tsx`), each its own tab list over the same panels, so a character named "Model" is never one of the tools. **Tools** (`Chat panel`): icon over label, in the registry's order (the most used first: **This chat** (`ThisChatTab.tsx`), Model, Prompt preset, **Notes**, **Lorebooks** (`LorebooksTab.tsx`), **Summaries**, Snippets, **Portrait** (`ui/Portrait.tsx`, §14.5a), Display, History edits), never wrapping: the row measures each tab and shows the ones that fit, the rest under **More tools**, the tab open always in the row. **Characters**: **Cast** (`CastTab.tsx`: present, played by me, removed, added), then a chip per character with its portrait and name (the persona last, marked "you"), the strip scrolling sideways with the open one in view. Each character's tab (`CharacterTab.tsx`) edits its card in place, below. The panel is on stock controls (`ui/PanelTabs.tsx`, `ui/Sheet.tsx`). From 1024 px wide it's a resizable column (416 px by default, clamped to available space; §14.5a) to the right of the messages, which stay readable, scrollable and sendable beside it; narrower, it's a full-height sheet over the chat with its title and a Close button. The open tab is the URL's `?panel=` (`portrait`, `cast`, `chat`, `lorebooks`, `notes`, `summaries`), so a reload or a copied link keeps it; the chat's own navigations (a branch switch, a summary's range) keep it too. This browser remembers, for each user, the tab open (`rp.chatPanel.<user id>`, removed on close) and reopens it on a wide screen when the URL names none, as after a link to a message, and writes it back into the URL; a phone opens the sheet only when asked, at the last tab used (`rp.chatPanelTab.<user id>`). Another account signing in on the same browser reads its own keys. The header's `PanelToggles` has one button per tab while the panel is closed and one "Hide panel" while it's open on a wide screen; a phone has a single "Chat panel" button. A tab mounts when first shown and stays mounted, hidden, while the panel is open, so switching tabs never ends what one holds (a summary's edit, a note's fields); This chat and Lorebooks read their lists to choose from again each time they're shown. The Notes tab's code is fetched as the panel opens, and if it can't be fetched the tab says so with Try again instead of failing the chat. Every change in it is a state event at the leaf shown (`ChangeCast`), so from there on, on that branch; the context assembler (§15) reads it.
- **A character's tab** (`CharacterTab.tsx`, as ST's character panel): the card's portrait and name, its chat actions (**Full editor**, **Fork for this chat**, **Use another version**, the `chatCardActions` slot), one line saying what a save reaches ("Saving changes Mira in 3 chats, this one included. To change it here only, fork it for this chat.", "… no other chat uses it", or, for a chat bound to a fixed revision, that saving changes the card and not what the chat uses), Save with the editor's status line, then Description, First message, Personality, Scenario, Example messages, System prompt, Post-history instructions and Creator's notes as text boxes: the editor's `TextField`s on the card's `EditorStore` from the chat's editor workspace, the same store the full editor over the chat uses, so drafts, field Undo/History/Revert, the leave guard and Save with its diff (`SaveDialog`, given the chat and fork for the rename offer) are the editor's (§14.6). Each long field has **Open <field> large**: a dialog over the chat with the same draft (ST's maximize), never a navigation. Greetings, tags, the lorebook, notes and the rest are in the full editor, which a line under the fields links to. Its **More** menu has a new chat with the card and the card's notes.
- **This chat** shows each thing the next reply is sent with, with its value and where it comes from. The model: `GetNextContext`'s and `Cache`'s `model` (`EffectiveModel`: connection and model ids, the connection's name and the model's key as they are now, whether it's hidden, and whether it comes from the chat's binding or the user's default, as the assembler's `model` source chose it, `chosenModel` in `assemblySources.ts`), so a default changed in another tab, a rename or a deletion shows before a send uses it: "Set in this chat", "Your default model" (linking to Settings → Chats), none with a link to add a connection, or a note that it's gone. A hidden model is still used and named, with a line saying it's hidden in Connections; the picker (which changes it for the chat) offers only visible models. The preset: `GetNextContext`'s and `Cache`'s `preset` (`EffectivePreset`: ref, name, and the scope-chain level it came from, `role`, `chat`, `connection`, `user` or `builtin`, as `presetFrom` in `assemblySources.ts` resolved it), named as such ("SillyTavern's default preset" for the built-in one, never a placeholder), with the budget's context size and reply reserve and a link to open it in the editor; a picker changes it for the chat, showing the preset in effect. When the chat has its own model or preset, **Use the default** under it takes it off (`DefaultModel`, `DefaultPreset`), naming what it goes back to: the user's default model, or for the preset the connection's override, else the user's default, else ST's (from `GetGenerationDefaults`, read with connections, all models including hidden ones, and preset labels each time the tab is shown and on every next-context `Cache` notification, including ones whose effective settings are unchanged under chat bindings; older in-flight reads and reads from an inactive panel cannot overwrite newer guidance). Hidden defaults are named with a separate hidden indicator; deleted defaults are described as gone. Hidden models remain excluded from the choice list; the panel then names the default and says "Your default model" or "Your default preset". Then summaries on or off (turning them on opens `EnableLcmDialog` as a dialog) and role projection with a line on what it does. There's no card layout choice: the preset's markers place the cards (§15 "Card definitions").
- **Lorebooks** (DESIGN §1.3, §5.0) has one section per kind, under the **Only below the history** switch (on unless the chat turned it off; `LoreBelowHistory`) and its "?" popover (`ui/Popover.tsx`) saying why (entries that come and go above the history invalidate the cache for everything after them) and how (positions are overridden; every entry goes after the history and is re-evaluated each turn); off, it says entries above the history stay until its start next moves. **Characters' books:** each member card's own book under its own name (`character_book.name`, else "<card>'s lorebook") with "<card>'s own lorebook" and its entry count (`CastCard.lorebookName`, `lorebookEntries`, read with the card in `db/cards.ts`), its per-chat switch (`CardLorebook`; off, it says its entries aren't sent and why an import turns it off) and a link to the card. **Added to this chat:** the attached books (`lorebook.attach`) with a switch (off detaches it; a book switched off stays listed while the tab is open, to switch back), Open, **Use in every chat**, and **Add lorebook**, a search over the library's lorebooks not attached yet. **Global:** every global book, once, also one attached here (it isn't listed under the added books too), each with a switch for this chat only (`GlobalLorebook`; it changes only global use). A book attached as well has a second, independent **Added to this chat: <name>** switch in that same row (`Lorebook`); switching either use off or on never changes the other. Either active use can send the book, and a detached attachment switch remains while the tab is open. Each row also offers **Not global**. **Limit:** a card bound to an attached world file (`lorebook.binding`) has its own book replaced by that file in assembly, which this tab doesn't show; the cast doesn't carry the bindings.
- **Settings → Chats** (`ChatDefaults.tsx`, `/settings/chats`) holds what applies to all of the user's chats (`GenerationDefaults`): the default model and preset, the refusal judge, the fallback summarizer and the default summarization instruction (with an explicit Save). Each control writes only its own setting (`ChangeGenerationDefaults`: the fields named, merged into the stored `generation.lcm` in one writer transaction), one write after another, so a change here never puts back what another tab changed since the page read them; the answer is the defaults as now stored. The instruction typed and not saved is copied into this browser (`backup.ts`, kind `setting`, filed as "Settings → Chats · Summarization instruction"): the field shows it again after leaving the page or a reload, saying it isn't saved yet, Browser recovery lists it, and Save (or typing it back to the saved text) drops the copy. There's no server draft for it (the `drafts` table's target kinds don't include user settings), so Retry leaves it and logout waits for it as for any copy. The panel links there.
- **Tests.** `e2e/chatCardPanel.spec.ts` (eight characters with long names, among them "Model" and "History", and one: the tools in one row with More tools, the cast strip apart; a character's fields edited in place, opened large, saved with the diff; no bin notice for an imported CRLF text, a plain one after a large deletion, dismissed; the full editor over the chat and back to the same URL; the editor page's header; at 1440 and 390 in both themes), `e2e/chatPanelSafety.spec.ts` (an unsaved instruction across navigation and reload, a summary's edit across a tab switch with a save in flight and across a failed save, a tab switch and a reload, the model following another tab's default and a rename, a hidden model named, a chat's own model and preset taken off back to the defaults, one setting's write leaving another tab's default, the own lorebook's name and count, the remembered tab per user), `e2e/chatSetup.spec.ts` (the Cast and This chat tabs, the tab kept in the URL across reloads, closing), `e2e/chatLore.spec.ts` (the model and its source; attaching a lorebook from the search, sending with the panel open, detaching), `e2e/chatNotes.spec.ts` (Notes beside the chat and as a phone's sheet), `e2e/lcm.spec.ts` and `e2e/lcmEnable.spec.ts` (Summaries, the rail switch, the summaries switch, Settings → Chats), through `e2e/support/panel.ts`, which opens a tab at either width.
- **Notes** (`ChatNotes.tsx`, DESIGN §4.1; loaded the first time it's opened, since it carries the note editor): a group for the chat's own notes, then one per cast card by display name, the cards the user plays included (a persona note is a card's note), as `ListChatNotes` reads them at the leaf shown: each note's upstream ref and head (a linked slot's `doc.content` is its shared note's text resolved in the viewed fork), `onByDefault`, `linked` (the shared note's ref, title, whether it's in the bin and how many slots use it) and whether it's on there (`noteSwitches` over the events at that slot, a card's note with `on_by_default` on unless switched off, `@rp/shared/state.ts`). Each note is a collapsed row (`NoteRow` in `features/editor/notes.tsx`): its switch, its name (or "Untitled" and its first line), its position in a few words, a chip for its condition ("Aria's turn", "While Bram isn't muted"), a link icon when it shows a shared note, "linked note is in the bin", "Private", and "author's note" on the chat's author's note (`ChatNote.authorNote`, the assembler's `isAuthorNote`); the position select names a card's "with the definitions" as "in the card's part", and a note placed just before or after the chat's author's note says what that means, including ST's default note in a chat without one; clicking it opens its editor inline, one at a time. The switch turns a note on or off with `ChangeCast` (`Note`: `note.activate`, `note.deactivate`), from the leaf shown on, on that branch. An opened note's row shows the registered `chat.note-actions` slot (`presentation.tsx`) above its editor; its `note.author-note` contribution (`AuthorNoteAction.tsx`) offers the chat's own notes **Use as author's note**, or **Stop using as author's note** on the one that is, with a line saying what goes around it. Both are `ChangeCast` (`AuthorNote`: `author_note.set` with the note, or with null for none), from the leaf shown on, on that branch. Card notes get no action: the listing and the assembler only take a chat's own note as its author's note, so naming a card's note leaves the chat without one. `test/chatSetup.test.ts` covers choosing, replacing, clearing from a later message (the earlier one keeps its choice) and a card note. "Add note" in each group makes a new note or, through "Pick a shared note…" (`SharedNotePicker`, filtered by name with `ListSharedNotes`), one linked to a shared note: the chat's own with `CreateChatNote` (an upstream note entity owned by the chat, `newChatNote`), a card's with `CreateCardNote` on the card's upstream ref; either way it's turned on at the leaf shown (DESIGN §4.1). Editing a chat-owned note is from the displayed leaf on, on this branch [P: DESIGN §3.4]. `OpenEditor` resolves `note.binding` there; `CommitEditorDrafts` writes a revision parented by that binding and the new binding event in one transaction, leaving the library head and sibling branches intact. `ListChatNotes` resolves that same binding. Card-owned notes remain library edits. The Notes tab refreshes on chat `State` events as well as library changes. Editing uses the editor (§14.6): a store per note, drafts per field, Save as a revision; a note is deleted to the bin with the usual toast (`DeleteNote`). The Character panel of a card in the chat has a "Notes (N)" line that opens this tab scrolled to the card's group. The list reads again after any library change (its reactivity key is `library`, which saves, deletes and restores invalidate); a change made in another tab shows the next time it's opened.
- **Base UI under the CSP.** Base UI's Select adds an inline `<style>` to hide its scrollbar, which `style-src 'self'` refuses. `ui/CspProvider.tsx` turns those off (`disableStyleElements`) and `styles.css` carries the rule; `e2e/csp.spec.ts` opens a select to catch a regression.

**Waiting for a cache write.** Generations still run concurrently across chats, branches and swipes. The provider gate (§6) holds only followers sharing the same cold reusable endpoint and effective cache-routing identity. Different one-turn directions after a reusable block breakpoint share its gate; changing those directions does not make a fully warm history cold. Growing history coalesces until its new endpoint is covered; a warm earlier prefix alone does not release it. `WaitingCache` events and `Hello.live.waitingCache` keep that status visible through reconnects; the reply body says “Waiting for the cache…” and Stop works while held. This wait is separate from a message queued onto a generating parent: the cache follower already has its own version and context.

**The send queue** (owner, 2026-10-12, DESIGN §2.2; `queued_messages`, migration 0012). A message sent, or a reply asked for, onto a message whose selected version is still generating waits on the server, so it survives closing the tab, under that slot, not a version. It shows as pending under the streaming reply and can be edited or withdrawn until it posts, both with the row version the user saw, so a tab that missed another's edit gets `StaleWrite` and shows the conflict instead of overwriting or deleting it. Withdrawn text goes back to the composer, after anything already typed there, and is also kept as recovered text before the queue row is removed, so closing the caller cannot lose it.
- **Durable queue edits** (`QueuedMessages.tsx`, registered as `composer.queue`; backlog U03). Message text and queued generation directions use the message editor's durable store and browser copies (`MessageDraftTarget.Queue`, `queue_edit` draft keyed by chat and queue id). The draft records the queue row version it started from; another tab's update requires an explicit second Save. Save changes content or direction and commits only the named draft holding exactly that text and session in the same writer transaction. Earlier text reads/restores every retained state of the draft's server history (the shared controller uses `MessageDraftHistory` and `RestoreMessageDraft`; `QueueDraftHistory` and `RestoreQueueDraft` remain available), including the starting state before its at most 1,000 changes; cancelling sends it and destructive history to recovered texts. Refreshes and another tab reopen the editor. Posting or withdrawing never removes an unsent edit: its chat-owned draft remains visible as an unsent edit, including after reload, with Copy to composer and Cancel. Late saves still reach that draft after the queue row disappears. A chat purge removes its queue drafts. `test/chatSetup.test.ts`, the isolation matrix and `e2e/composerBacklog.spec.ts` cover messages, directions, history, commits, late saves and orphan recovery; `e2e/queue.spec.ts` covers the two-tab queue conflict. Queued image previews use the registered `chat.queued-previews` slot alongside this editor; withdrawing restores the message's images as well as its text through the composer contribution. `e2e/queue.spec.ts` checks edit, reload and withdrawal with image attachments at desktop and phone widths.
- **Stop belongs to a version** (backlog U07). No composer Stop contribution is registered. Every generating version retains its own Stop button, including when selected through its version picker; changing the selected swipe never retargets a composer action.

- **Queue events leave in commit order:** each write that moves the queue publishes it before anything else it does waits, so a reply posted from a queue that ends at once can't have its newer queue overtaken by the older one.
- **Posting.** When a generation of the slot ends, or the user selects one of its finished versions (`SelectAndDrain`), and the version the queue's fork selects isn't generating, the first entry posts after the selected version, in that write's transaction: a message, then a reply to it by the speaker picked when it was sent (`reply_speaker_entity_id`, as the client picks one for an immediate send), or the reply asked for. It posts in the fork it was sent in (`queued_messages.fork_id`), which was chatted in when it was sent, so if the user has gone to another fork meanwhile, the chat stays where the user is: moving them back would hide what they sent there. Should that fork have gone on from the reply, or gone to the bin, the post makes a fork of its own there. Two forks can wait on one reply (a fork made at it while it was being written): each fork's entries post in that fork, each fork's first with a reply of its own (`QueueOutcome.more` beside `next`), in the order the forks queued first. The fork's composer stays its own throughout. The new reply starts generating, and the rest of the queue waits for it. A message sent with every character muted (`SendMessage` with `reply: false`, stored as `no_reply`, migration 0048) posts with no reply, as an immediate send does, and the entries after it post on from it under the same rule. So it posts after whichever version ends up selected: complete, stopped (right after the stopped version), or failed (which stays out of the context).
- **Generate onto a generating reply** is queued too, as an entry with no text, under the same rule: whatever is aimed at a reply still being written waits for it. Chosen over refusing it (the user would have to wait and retry) and over starting it at once (it would be generated without the reply it follows).
- **The reply goes to the bin** (or its chat does): the deletion stops its generation, and that generation's end recovers the queued message text and directions before removing the entries. Restoring or purging before the generation finishes runs the same recovery before clearing its queue. Subscribers get `Queue` with what's left.
- **A restart** with messages queued behind a reply that becomes `interrupted`: at startup they post in order after it, without replies, and queued replies are removed after their directions are saved as recovered text. The server doesn't start paid generations with nobody there; the user presses Reply. A reply interrupted by the server stopping leaves its queue to that startup.
- `test/queue.test.ts` covers each of these with a generator that ends only when the test says so; `e2e/chatSetup.spec.ts` covers posting after a finished reply, stop-then-post and withdrawing in the browser.

**The fake generator** (`dev/fakeGenerator.ts`, `dev/syntheticText.ts`) streams synthetic Markdown (prose, dialogue, lists, quotes, an occasional table), seeded by the version id, at `FAKE_GENERATOR_TPS` tokens a second, about `FAKE_GENERATOR_TOKENS` (400) per generation, in chunks of what's due every 10 ms or more. It's for tests, the development server and the performance harness, and only those can wire it: the production entry point `main.ts` has no generator whatever the environment says (`test/chats.test.ts`), and `src/dev/` isn't in the Docker image (`.dockerignore`). The development entry point, `src/dev/main.ts`, reads `FAKE_GENERATOR_TPS` and refuses to start with `NODE_ENV=production` (`test/config.test.ts`). The browser tests start it only for files that ask for the fake generator.

**The client** (`packages/client/src/features/chat/`), on stock components (CLIENT-FRAMEWORK §8):
- **URLs:** `/chats` lists chats, newest first, with delete, and makes a chat without characters. `/chats/$chatId` opens a chat at the bottom of its current fork; `?fork=` shows another fork, `?slot=` opens at a message and marks it. The URL names a fork only while it isn't the current one: once a send there makes it current, the URL drops it. Each message's menu has "Copy link" (that URL, with `fork` on a fork other than the current one) and "Branch from here" (`CreateFork` at it: the chat opens on the new fork, which is current, and Back returns to the fork it came from).
- **The header** (`ChatHeader.tsx`): the cast's faces (`AvatarStack`; the participants the user doesn't play) with their names beside on a wide screen and a count on a phone, the chat's readable name (`chatTitle.ts`: the card or group name and the day ST started it, never ST's file name; it's also the tab's title), the branch shown, and the chat's own buttons, which `ChatPage.tsx` passes in (`actions`). On a phone it's one line: the name truncates. Under it, a chat that has outgrown its context with summaries off has a one-line status bar with **Enable summaries**; a phone shows only "Summaries are off.", the reason in the button's title and description (`ChatPage.tsx`). **The branch shown:** where the path branches is read from the leaf up (`forks.ts`: `ReadPath` 500 at a time, at most 2,000 messages, once per branch shown; what's loaded since replaces it at its depth, and session naming metadata from Slot events updates off-window names with row-version fencing), so the header says "Branch 2 of 3", or its saved name, for the latest place the path branches, from anywhere in the chat. Named branches stay in this scan when their last sibling is deleted: the header shows the name with branch actions, without switch wording or a single-choice radio list. Rename, clear name, delete and jump remain available; clearing the sole branch's name returns it to an ordinary unnamed path. Its menu lists each place with its branches (switching through `SwitchBranch`, as the message's own control does), "Go to message #n", and, on a fork that isn't the chat's current one, "Back to the latest branch".
- **One `ChatSession` per open chat** (`session.ts`) holds the loaded window of the path, kept current by the subscription. It subscribes first and opens the window on the first `Hello`; another `Hello` (a reconnect) or a `Reload` reads the loaded range again. Writes apply the server's reply at once (the event that follows changes nothing); selecting a version shows it before the reply and puts the slot back if refused, and a `StaleWrite` reads the slot again. The server may answer a read from before an event that reached the client while it was in flight; so `Slot`, `Version` and `Chat` events received while reads are in flight are applied again once each lands, and applying one twice, or after a newer state, changes nothing (row versions, and a finished version never goes back to generating).
- **The chat view loads with the first chat opened** (a lazy route): it carries the Markdown renderer, 170 KB gzipped of the client's 415.
- **The list** (`ChatPage.tsx`) is TanStack Virtual in chat mode (`anchorTo: "end"`), keyed by slot id with measured heights: prepending older history and growing rows below keep what's on screen in place.
- **Following the bottom:** at the end of a window that reaches the leaf, the list holds there while the last messages grow or arrive. The user scrolling up (wheel, touch, keys, grabbing the scrollbar, a middle-button autoscroll, or any scroll away from the last 48 px) lets go at once, and TanStack Virtual's own following goes off with it: when it wrote the scroll position as a smooth scroll began, the scroll stopped in its first pixels and the list never let go. Reaching the end again holds on once the user has stopped for 300 ms. Known gap: a scroll up with none of these signs, like find in page, is pulled back until it's 48 px from the end; telling it from content shrinking by the scroll position alone wasn't reliable.
- **Paging:** it pages older and newer from the server's cursors when the first or last rendered message nears the loaded edge, keeps at most 500 slots, and drops the far end, read again when needed. Rows have `contain: layout paint style`.
- **A token updates views of its version.** Each generating version's text is an atom of its own (`liveText`), read by its message's body and any open picker preview or comparison of it, and kept alive while it streams whether shown or not. Deltas are collected and applied once per animation frame; any other event first applies the deltas before it. Message rows are memoized and re-render only when their slot changes.
- **Message rendering** (`ui/Markdown.tsx`, `ui/StMarkdown.tsx`) dispatches through the subscribed `messageRenderers` registry. `registerDisplay` registers the lazy ST formatter as `markdown`; plugins can replace or disable it, wrap its effective component to extend formatting, or add a higher-priority renderer. Disabling all renderers shows plain text. This applies to messages, reasoning, greetings, summary text and version previews. The formatter uses Streamdown 2.7's memoized blocks and renders React elements through the unchanged rehype-sanitize and rehype-harden pipeline. Raw HTML stays literal text (rehype-raw is omitted and aliased to a stub); message images cannot load, and links open in a new tab without a referrer. A fenced code block is one frame (`styles.css`, `[data-streamdown="code-block"]`): the raised surface with a border, so it shows on the user's raised messages too, its language as a small muted label above the code; Streamdown's inner body and the `pre` draw no box of their own (before, an outer box held the label and a second framed box held the code). `e2e/messageFormatting.spec.ts` checks one painted or bordered element per block, at 1440 and 390 px, light and dark.
- **ST 1.19 formatting** (`ui/stFormatting.ts`; DESIGN §10.2, built 2026-10-29) uses ST's six double-quote pairs and nearest-closing-delimiter regex, with no match across a newline or inside code. AST quote nodes preserve their original glyphs; italics inside quotes inherit the quote colour. As with ST’s default `encode_tags=false`, straight quotes inside literal `<...>` tags are shielded from dialogue matching; the tags remain text. Both asterisks and underscores give italics, double/triple underscores give underline, and double asterisks remain bold. The underline AST uses sanitizer-approved `ins`, then a React component renders `u`; no HTML permission was added. Soft line breaks become `br`; blank lines still separate paragraphs. ST's `fixMarkdown(text, true)` runs on every message, completed or streaming: it removes adjacent horizontal whitespace from paired marks, then closes odd asterisks and straight quotes per line. Its original behavior is retained, including not closing underscores/curly quotes, touching code whitespace, and the quote check winning when both checks match one line. This changes display only; stored, copied, edited and prompt text stays original. The former remend display pass and direct dependency are removed. Reference links still receive only their needed definitions across blocks (`ui/referenceDefinitions.ts`, at most 32 KB copied per message, never into an open code fence). `markdown.test.ts` covers the formatting, protections and effective renderer replacement/wrapping/addition/disable/restore; `messageColors.test.ts` covers visible settings controls. `e2e/messageFormatting.spec.ts` checks rendering, inherited quote colour, colour persistence/reset, unchanged stored text and production CSP at 1440/390 in both themes.

- **Copying a selection** (`ui/sourcePositions.ts`, `ui/messageCopy.tsx`; built 2026-10-30). Actual message and reasoning prose opts in through `MessageBody`; editors, composer, previews and other text keep native copy. The registered `message.source-copy` entry in `messageCopyHandlers` can be replaced, wrapped, disabled or superseded by a higher-order handler. The ST renderer preserves parser positions through quote/underline/soft-break wrapping, carries UTF-16 source ranges through `fixMarkdown` and CRLF normalization, and attaches trusted DOM metadata after sanitization. Per-block plugin options keep Streamdown's processor cache from sharing offsets between blocks/messages; unchanged blocks retain their memoized parser pipeline while streaming. Contiguous text uses a compact offset; escapes, entities and display repairs carry individual ranges. Display-only closings, list numbering, code-frame labels and blocked-image caption labels have no stored text. Image hardening keeps its existing policy/output and retains only the original image position and alt text for mapping. Code maps the body independently of its frame, retaining logical line breaks between the rendered line elements; empty-line filler glyphs contribute no extra break in either clipboard flavour. Selecting an element's first/last rendered character includes its stored opening/closing markup; partial selections copy exactly the stored slice between their endpoints, without balancing marks. Several selected bodies contribute slices in DOM order separated by a blank line. Selections starting or ending outside message bodies retain the browser’s selected outside text and layout breaks around the mapped slices; temporary native-text reads restore the selection and its direction. Keyboard Copy and native menu/phone Copy use the copy event; native selection drags use the same effective registered handler through dragstart, without cancelling the drag or overriding an unrelated link/image drag. The handler writes both `text/plain` (stored slices) and `text/html` (selected rendered fragments with their formatting, without provenance attributes). Rich fragments retain code-line newlines and suppress native quote pseudo-glyphs without relying on the app stylesheet. Streaming bodies use their current live text, and reasoning uses its own stored/live text. Whole-message menu Copy text still copies the complete stored content. `sourceText` lets the renderer align transformed display text to storage with a bounded diff: replacements refer to their original range and display-only insertions have no source. Replacement renderers supply the same `data-source-map` contract or replace the copy handler. `test/sourceCopy.test.ts` covers every built-in text formatting kind, partial edges, reference/repeated blocks, repairs, literal HTML, escapes/entities, CRLF, emoji and alt captions; `e2e/messageCopy.spec.ts` grants Chromium clipboard permissions and exercises whole/partial/across-message and mixed outside selections, keyboard and browser Copy commands, native drag transfers, rich HTML, repairs, code (including selected blank-line breaks), native editing, streaming and reasoning at desktop/phone widths.
- **Message colours** are semantic theme tokens in `styles.css`: main text, italics, underline and quote. Dark defaults are ST's `#dcdcd2`, `#919191`, `#bce7cf`, `#e18a24`; light defaults are `#303039`, `#666666`, `#286044`, `#925000`, chosen for AA contrast against the message background. The Display panel exposes four labelled colour inputs and individual Reset buttons (`MessageColors.tsx`). Nullable hex-colour patches (`mainTextColor`, `italicsTextColor`, `underlineTextColor`, `quoteTextColor`) use the existing account-owned display preferences; null follows the current theme. The registered `app.tools` contribution `display.message-colors` applies these tokens throughout the authenticated app, including portal previews, and clears overrides when unmounted. Theme changes update default colour swatches.
- **A message** (`Message.tsx`): the speaker's avatar and name (the card's, the persona's for the user's turns; an edit or a written version keeps the message's speaker, and an older one without a speaker shows another version's, or the first version's, deleted or not), and its side as the context reads it (`sideOf`: a card the user plays is the user's, another of the cast the model's, else `PathEntry.side`), which decides its presentation, its time (short, the full date in a tooltip), its number on the path (`#n`, counted from 1 as the inspector and the summaries count), and its state: generating, stopped, interrupted, failed (with its cause in the body), refused, edited. The Stop of a generating version is in its header, always shown.
  - **Versions** are at the end of the message, as in ST, where a swipe that changes the text's length leaves them on the last message: `‹ 3/7 ›` on every message with more than one, and on model-written turns on either side, whatever their depth. The counter opens `VersionPicker.tsx`: a scrollable desktop popover or full-height phone sheet with numbered two-line previews, writer/model and time, star/hide buttons, a starred filter and a Hidden section. Hidden versions stay selectable there but leave the arrows' cycle; hiding a version does not hide its message from context. Compare selects two versions for both texts (side by side on desktop, stacked on phones) or the editor's `ValueDiff`. Past the last visible version, › generates another when the slot has model-written text; a horizontal swipe on touch does the same. A swipe is a touch drag of more than 60 px, at least twice as wide as tall, that starts on the message (`swipeCanStart` in `Message.tsx`): never on a control (button, link, input, textarea, select, summary, menu item), in an editor (a contenteditable such as CodeMirror's, any `role=textbox`, a label), or while the message is being edited; a `pointercancel` (the browser took the drag to scroll) drops it, and a pointerup with a text selection is no swipe (parity finding 2). The version arrows and picker are unaffected. `e2e/messageGestures.spec.ts` covers each case at 390 px. Picker previews and both comparison views subscribe to each version’s live text. Comparisons retain version IDs and resolve current content, status and model from the updated list, so they follow streaming through completion. Session version listeners update open lists and comparisons even when an unselected version changes in another tab.
  - **Actions** are icon buttons with tooltips (`ui/Tooltip.tsx`), shown on hover or focus on a wide screen (always on a touch screen without hover), and on a narrow one after a tap on the message, below its text, with why it isn't sent (when it isn't) just above them, so scrolling the actions into view keeps the reason's start in view: Edit (amend for the user's own finished text, a new version otherwise), Regenerate on model-written turns on either side, and Delete version (Delete message when it has only one, DESIGN §6.2). The rest are in its "More actions" menu (`ui/Menu.tsx`): Write your own version (`WriteVersion`), Generate a version on human-written turns, Regenerate with… (or Generate a version with…) using the same model choices as Retry with…, Branch from here, Copy text (the text shown, streamed so far while it generates), Copy link, Context it was generated from (the request inspector, §15) and Delete message with all its versions. The composer has Send as and Generate (above; "Send" and "Reply" when the user plays no card) and Regenerate; Stop belongs to each generating version (DESIGN §3.1). Typing re-renders nothing else. Nothing waits for a reply: a send during one is queued (below).
  - **Editing** (Edit, Write your own version) opens an editor in the message whose text is a server draft (`messageDraft.ts`, the drafts above), held by the chat's session rather than the row, so a message scrolled out of the virtualized list keeps it: saved 400 ms after typing and when the page is hidden or the chat closed, read back with `MessageDrafts` when the chat opens, so a reload opens each editor again, on its version or message, with its text. Save names the draft to commit with the version, and once the version is saved drops the browser copy of exactly the text it submitted: text typed while the version was being saved stays in the editor, which stays open, with its copy, its pending save and its base (the draft it was typed on, whose ended session the server reopens with it). An edit that made a new version leaves that editor on the version edited, reached by its arrows. Cancel discards it to the recovered texts, and while the server doesn't have the text yet it refuses and keeps the editor open. As for summaries, every change also leaves a copy in this browser (§3.10 invariant 4, `backup.ts` kind `message`, the `SaveMessageDraft` payload as typed, filed under `messages:<chat>` with the editor as its field), replacing only this editor's copy not on its way to the server; a save's success deletes the copy it sent and re-files what was typed since on the new draft, and a failure keeps it. Opening the chat replays its copies, oldest first, once the chat's drafts are read, opening each editor again with the text the server took, or with what it still doesn't have and the error. So text typed while the server is unreachable survives a closed tab. The editor keeps its base apart from the draft the server last answered with: text shown from a copy the server refused keeps that copy's draft id, row version and session through retries and more typing, and the base moves only when the server answers a save with exactly the text sent, so the retry goes on top of text saved elsewhere meanwhile instead of being typed over it. Replays and saves run one at a time, in order. A replay (or the first save of a refused replay's text) that goes over text saved elsewhere shows the composer's notice (`ReplayNotice`: both times and the other text) until acknowledged, through typing; “Use the other text” restores the displaced text explicitly, keeping the browser text in the draft history. “Earlier draft text” lists every retained state (up to the common 1,000-state cap), including after reload. Escape closes its dialog without cancelling the message edit, including while focus is still on the editor’s history trigger before modal focus moves; `e2e/messageHistory.spec.ts` exercises that focus interval explicitly. Restores run with saves in order; text typed during a restore stays in the box with its browser copy.
