The registered `message.draft-history` and `summary.draft-history` contributions use `message.editor.tools` and `summary.editor.tools` slots. The registered `message.amendments` action opens saved amendment history for human-written versions, with full earlier text and an optimistic restore in the fork captured when the dialog opened. `e2e/messageHistory.spec.ts` exercises both editors and saved amendment restores at 1440 and 390 pixels in light and dark, including Escape from the history dialog without cancelling the message edit. `e2e/chatViewDrafts.spec.ts` holds a Write and an Edit's commit while more is typed, fails a replay once before a retry over another device's text, and replays over another device's text.
- **The branch switcher:** a message with alternates shows `‹ ⑂ 2/3 ›` (named "Branches: 2 of 3"), in its header, apart from its versions; the chat's header has the same switch for every place the path branches (above). A switch opens the fork through the alternate (above) at the message switched to, which the URL then names (`?fork=` unless it's the current fork, and `?slot=`); where the chat opens doesn't change until something is sent there. A new alternate arriving from another tab raises the count on the messages shown; a message another fork appended at the same tip (`PathEntry.forkId`) doesn't join the window.
- **Composer mode and phone layout** (`Composer.tsx`, `ui/viewPrefs.ts`): account display choices apply optimistically to every consumer in the tab's atom registry. Preference writes run in click order. Each preference read records the last write acknowledged before its request starts (`ui/preferenceWrites.ts`); its result retires those saved patches even if another browser changed the account again. Older reads cannot retire newer patches, and a refused patch rolls back with the initiating control's error. Enter and the visible Guidance switch use the same local mode, including while its server write waits. At viewport widths below 640 px, the composer keeps its speaker, Guidance switch and Options trigger in one row. Options exposes the registered settings, reply choices and help plus earlier-text history in an anchored, scrollable popup, leaving the conversation in place. Recent speakers remain in the speaker picker. The phone cache estimate keeps its badge and countdown on one line, with the explanation and countdown switch under Cache details. Desktop placements are unchanged. The extra `help` placement is a composer contribution, as are settings, speaker, guidance, reply and action controls. `e2e/composerQa.spec.ts` holds mode writes across submissions in both directions and asserts at least 422 px of conversation at 390×844 with five characters, three-line guidance and a warm cache (after dismissing the first-use impersonation notice); both themes also exercise the exposed controls. The suite also checks a newer account choice arriving before readback, refusal/rollback and saving again; `test/preferenceWrites.test.ts` covers read/write ordering without wall-clock assumptions.
- **The composer's text is a server draft** (DESIGN §6.2; `composerDraft.ts`, `@rp/shared/composerApi.ts`): one per fork, the fork shown. `OpenComposer` (by fork; checks the chat and fork are the caller's and live), `SaveComposer` (600 ms after the last key), `ComposerHistory` and `RestoreComposer` (by the draft's id) act on the draft store of §3.10. Server calls run one at a time, in order.
  - **The text belongs to the draft it was typed into** (§3.10), kept apart from the fork the view asks for. Every save names that draft. Going to another fork opens its composer first, with the box read-only while its text loads: if it's the same draft (the view named the current fork it had), the text stays; otherwise the text is saved on its own draft, and the new fork's shows. The fork's tip moving (a message appended, or one deleted or restored) reads its draft again with the box open, for another tab's text. Going through several forks quickly shows only the last; each step saves on its own draft.
  - **Another tab's newer text** reaching this tab (the draft read again after a move, a send's reply, a save the server had already made): text not changed here takes it; text changed here is saved on top, the other text one step back in the history. The box's base changes only with its text (§3.10).
  - **A send** takes the text and its draft at the click, and the box empties: what's typed from then on is the next message. The text is saved on its draft, then sent in that draft's fork, at its tip, naming the draft, its session and the text (`composer`), so the server commits it in the send's transaction only if it still holds that text. If the view went to another fork meanwhile, it isn't sent, and stays in its own fork's composer. Once answered: a committed draft takes the next message; a draft another tab typed on meanwhile shows that tab's text, unless something was typed here since, which then goes on top of it. A send that didn't go puts its text back in the box, ahead of anything typed since.
  - **Recovered text:** text typed in this browser while offline in a session ended elsewhere, or another tab's text a send's commit took out of the history, is in the bin's recovered texts, and the box says so, with a link, until the page goes.
  - **Starting a branch** ("Branch from here") copies the composer into the fork made (`ChatSession.branchFrom`, `OpenComposer` with `copyFrom`). `composerWorkspace.ts` keeps the draft, its serialized save/send queue, busy state and operation errors with the chat session, across fork-scoped surface remounts. A remount therefore retains the source draft needed by `copyFrom`, and a held send rejected after switching forks remains visible as an error while its text stays in its original composer. `e2e/composer.spec.ts` covers both races against the real server; neither test’s contract was weakened.
  - **Leaving** follows the editors (§14.6): a hidden tab saves at once; closing or reloading with text not on the server asks first, and `pagehide` sends it with `keepalive` as `composers` in `POST /library/drafts` (§14.5), which `SaveComposer` saves, so it never recreates a moved or sent draft. While a send is out, that's the send's text if the server hasn't confirmed it, and what's typed since as `next`.
  - **This browser's copy** uses the editors' pending-save records (`backup.ts`, under `composer:<chat>`): each has an independent id and its original draft id, row version, session, text and `next` flag. RPC and keepalive send that same payload and acknowledge that copy's id; typing during a save leaves the newer copy intact. Typing replaces only this tab's unsent copy of the same draft; going to another draft leaves a copy that couldn't be saved in the browser, where it comes back as that draft's composer opens. Opening a composer replays every pending composer save unchanged, ordered by time with the send's text before its next message, and keeps every copy that fails, whatever the reason; one of the opened draft's shows in the box on its own base. Another draft's copies wait until they're 30 s old. The writer's operation receipts prevent an acknowledged save whose browser copy survived from resurrecting sent text or creating recovery. A replay over text saved elsewhere shows a warning with this browser copy's time and the writer's displaced-text time, a preview, Keep this browser's text, and Use the other text. The choice is an explicit restore, which cannot coalesce with typing and is itself undoable. Restore RPCs accept a picked `content` as well as a history position, bounded by the draft text cap and guarded by the draft id and row version, so the choice still works after history retention. The editors' per-user expiry and logout rules apply.
  - **History** (the History button): the draft's states, newest first; picking one restores it, itself undoable. It's restored only over the text the box showed at the pick, with nothing unsaved: if the box changed before it could go (calls before it were still out), it isn't. Text typed while it's on its way stays, saved on top of the restored state.
- **Deleting** shows a toast (`ui/Toast.tsx`, Base UI) with Undo and "Open the bin" (DESIGN §6.2), on messages, versions and chats, naming the chat by its readable name ("Deleted: Version of a message in “The Lantern Inn, started Apr 3, 2026”").
- **Context membership:** the server publishes the next request’s history start in `Cache`, even before a first generation or without a TTL. Messages before that start have muted text, a dashed border and an accessible “Not in the context” icon labelled "Out of context". Deliberately hidden messages have a ghost labelled “Hidden from context” ("Hidden"), dimmed, without the dropped-message style. Each mark says why in its tooltip, and on a phone below the header once the message is tapped: where the history sent starts and the preset's budget that put it there (its name, maximum and target, from `NextContext.budget`), or that the user hid it and how to send it again (`PathEntry.hiddenByUser`), or that it was hidden in ST (an imported version), or that an empty message has nothing to send.
- **Hiding a message** (ST's ghost toggle; `e2e/hideMessage.spec.ts`): its "More actions" menu has **Hide from the model**, or **Show to the model again** on a hidden one. `SetMessageHidden` writes `SetSlotHidden` (§3.8) and publishes the slot; like an edit it starts no generation (DESIGN §8). The slot's event has `CacheWarmth` estimate again, which names a hidden message before the last breakpoint ("Message N was hidden."); stepped history treats a hide as any reduction before its start, and summaries covering the message go stale and fresh again by visibility, as for an ST-hidden run (§16, Freshness). `test/hideMessage.test.ts` checks the next request without and with it through a mock provider. A row before the first message sent says the same at the cut ("Messages above this aren’t sent to the model: the preset … allows …"). Boundaries are scoped to the path leaf, so an old event cannot mark another branch. Inactive paths use `GetNextContext(chatId, leafSlotId, forkId)` when opened and when a `Cache` event invalidates their estimate. Cache estimates carry their fork id, and an event for another fork sharing the same tip triggers a read of the viewed fork. The client checks the displayed fork, leaf and read generation before applying a result, so two tabs can show different branches without borrowing each other’s boundary. If next assembly fails, the server falls back to the nearest recorded start on that path.
- **A generated version** links to its context ("Context it was generated from", in its menu: the request inspector, §15), and shows the notes its context recorded, collapsed ("2 notes on its context"). Routine history truncation is filtered from this list, including older rollouts; it remains in the request inspector. **The cache-warmth indicator** sits above the composer (§15).
- **Notifications** (DESIGN §10.1; `features/notices.tsx`): `SubscribeNotices` streams the caller's notices: `ReplyFinished` when a generation ends complete or failed (`chat/Notices.ts`, published by `Generations`, per user, a bounded backlog of 256; overflow replaces pending notices with `UserDraftSync` and the newest event), not when stopped or interrupted. A reply in a chat this tab isn't showing, or while the tab is hidden, counts as unseen until that chat is open and visible: a badge on the chat in the list, the count in front of the tab's title, and a favicon with a dot (two static SVGs, since `img-src 'self'` forbids a drawn one). Counts belong to the tab and go with a reload. **Optional completion sound** (U14; `features/completionSound.ts`, `completionSoundFeature.tsx`) registers as `notifications.completion-sound` in `noticeChannels` (`noticeContributions.tsx`): settings, interaction activation and unseen-reply playback resolve the effective entry, so replacing or disabling it changes all three without affecting badges or system notifications. The settings host subscribes to registry snapshots. Sound is off by default and saved per browser in Settings › Notifications, independently of system notifications. Enabling it, or interacting after a reload, activates Web Audio; an unseen complete or failed reply plays one short tone only while audio is running. A suspended or unavailable device skips the tone without queuing playback. Notice identities (version and terminal status) are deduplicated for the tab lifetime before badges, sound and system notifications, including retries of the subscription. Unit tests cover activation, suspension, opt-out, replay, channel replacement and disablement; browser tests exercise sound through real unseen completions, reload and touch activation. System notifications are a per-browser choice in Settings › Notifications: the page calls `Notification.requestPermission` only when the user turns them on, and the switch shows on only once the browser allows it. The registered Settings child uses the relative path `notifications`, so its navigation link opens `/settings/notifications`. `e2e/chatExtras.spec.ts` clicks that Settings link at 1440 and 390 pixels before exercising permission choices.
- **An unfinished ST import** (§13) shows as a badge, from the title's marker (`importState` in `stImportApi.ts`), not as part of the title.
- Card portraits use the authenticated media route (§14.5); initials are the fallback. **New chat** opens a pending chat with that card and saves a new peer chat on first send, rather than another root of the current chat.
- `e2e/chat.spec.ts` covers streaming, versions, stopping, editing, links, branches, deleting, another user, and the screens at phone and desktop width in both themes; `e2e/composer.spec.ts` the composer's drafts (reload, another tab, send, history, closing, a copy brought back) and its races, each with a request held on its way while the user goes on (typing during a send, a branch switched during one, another tab typing on, quick branch changes, a restore, starting a branch); `e2e/chatExtras.spec.ts` the branch switcher, Undo, the bin, recovered text, notifications, and asking for permission only when told to; `e2e/chatNotes.spec.ts` a note made in a chat reaching the request a fake provider receives through real generation (the production server, no fake generator), and leaving it once turned off, and a card's note edited from the chat as the card's; `e2e/inspector.spec.ts` the request inspector and the cache indicator; `e2e/cast.spec.ts` the Cast tab (absent, speak now, show card, add, write as, remove) and Edit opening the card used everywhere. `e2e/cardForkForChat.spec.ts` covers the Cast and card-panel actions, the fork dialog and its clash check, the new editor, branching three messages back with the fork retained, an existing sibling retaining the parent, and switching back through the family tree, at 1440 and 390 pixels in light and dark. `test/cardForkForChat.test.ts` checks the pinned source revision, note switches and version attachments, fork-local queue and composer updates, original message attribution, forking after a swapped-in card is purged, and full transaction rollback after a forced failure; `test/isolation.test.ts` probes the new RPC across users.

**The performance harness** (`packages/client/perf/`, `npm run test:perf`; CLIENT-FRAMEWORK §7). The built client and the real server with bundled workers and the fake generator at 300 tokens a second per stream, on Chromium through Playwright, one worker.
- **Fixtures** (`fixtures.ts`): synthetic chats of 100,000 messages (every 25th reply, every 50th message, with four versions, one failed; an HTML message every 997; a three-message side branch every 5,000) and two of 300. The server migrates a fresh data directory and creates the chats; the messages are then written straight into the file in one transaction (seconds, where 100,000 RPCs would take minutes), cached per fixture version in the system temp directory and copied for each run.
- **Scenarios:** open at the bottom and 70,000 messages up; scroll the full length to the root, prepending about 1,650 pages of older history; six streams of 20 seconds, measuring renders, following at the bottom, typing in the composer, drift while scrolled up and prepending while they still run; swiping through versions; switching chats mid-stream and back; one reply streaming on past 4,000 tokens; three versions streaming at once under one slot, one shown; a bursty stream (300 tokens at once every second) and a slow one (4 tokens a second), each on a server of its own with the generator paced that way (`FAKE_GENERATOR_BURST_MS`, the development entry point only).
- **Measured:** long animation frames, frame intervals, Event Timing input durations, where the first loaded message is after older history lands above it (a message gone from the screen counts as an unbounded error), the distance from the end while the last reply grows, the JS heap after garbage collection at the start, every 400 pages and the end of the full scroll, renders of streaming bodies on screen per update to one of them, other bodies and rows re-rendered, and list renders (`features/chat/perf.ts` counts them, built in only with `VITE_PERF=1`, as `npm run test:perf` builds; elsewhere the counters, `mountedBodies` and `window.__rpPerf` are dead code and left out), delta gaps, the round trip of a trivial RPC while the streams run (the server's event loop carries every stream), and main-thread script time (CDP's `ScriptDuration`) while the long reply streams.
- **Budgets** (`budgets.ts`) fail the run. They're set from runs on this shared 48-core host at load averages of 30 to 90, with headroom for that load. One run at load 33 (2026-10-12):

| Scenario | Measured | Budget |
|---|---|---|
| Open at the bottom / at a deep message | 0.9 s / 1.0 s (3.7 s / 6.1 s at load 58) | 8 s each; 8 frames over 100 ms |
| Full scroll, 1,666 older pages | anchoring error 0 px; heap 7.2 → 11.3, 12.1, 12.4, 12.8 → 12.9 MB; frame p95 33 ms; 4 frames over 100 ms | 2 px; growth 40 MB, total 150 MB; 70 ms; 40 |
| Six streams | frame p95 16.8 ms; 0 frames over 100 ms; input p95 32 ms (88 to 112 at load 40 to 70); 1 render of a streaming body on screen per update, 0 rows re-rendered; 0 px from the end while at the bottom; drift and prepend error 0 px; no gaps; RPC round trip p99 25 ms | p95 50 ms; 3; 250 ms; 1; 0; 120 px; 2 px; 0; 100 ms |
| Swipe through versions | input p95 72 ms (200 at load 49); 0 frames over 100 ms | 250 ms; 3 |
| Switch chats mid-stream | away 0.5 s, back 0.2 s; the stream kept growing, no gaps; 1 frame over 100 ms | 8 s; 8 |
| A long reply, 4,065 → 5,429 words | script 147 ms a second (128 with the incremental split, so it went); 1 body render per update; 0 px from the end; frame p95 16.7 ms; 0 frames over 100 ms | 400 ms; 1; 120 px; 50 ms; 3 |
| Three swipes streaming under one slot | 510 updates, 170 of them to the one shown, 1 body render each, 0 rows; the one before, swiped to, had kept its text and grew; no gaps; frame p95 16.7 ms | 1; 0; 0; 50 ms |
| Bursty, 300 tokens a second at once | 6 updates in 5 s; 1 body render each; 0 px from the end; worst frame 125 ms at load 44, none over 50 at load 22 | 4 to 7; 1; 120 px; 3 over 100 ms |
| Slow, 4 tokens a second | 23 deltas, 23 updates: none held back; 0 px from the end; no long frames | equal; 120 px; 0 |

- **2026-10-13 run:** all 11 scenarios passed at load 10–18. Open at the bottom/deep slot: 727/893 ms. Full scroll: 1,665 pages, 0 px anchoring error, heap 8.2 → 13.9 MB, frame p95 16.8 ms. Six streams: frame p95 16.8 ms, input p95 32 ms, RPC round trip p99 9.4 ms, one body render per update and no row renders while typing; no drift or gaps.
- **What it found:** a list that stopped following a growing reply, then (with a wider threshold) one that wouldn't let go when the user scrolled up mid-stream; paging that read older history on every open; and races in its own measurements, found by mutating the client in review.
- **Not covered yet:** WebKit, CPU throttling for phone-class devices, real phones (CLIENT-FRAMEWORK §7), and CI: the full run takes about four minutes plus the build.

**Open decisions this leaves open:** "start over" with a card (DESIGN §2.1, [O]) isn't built.

---

### Message images (U15, U16)

Message input has an ordered `images` list of `{mediaId, altText?}` (at most 32, alt text at most 2,000 characters). `POST /chat/image` accepts a bounded 16 MiB octet-stream upload, detects PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP from its bytes, and returns `{mediaId}`. It uses the import routes' authenticated guard, upload permits, body deadlines, content-addressed atomic file writes and sweep holds. `RecordMessageImage` records the asset and the acting user's reference in one writer transaction. `GET /library/media?id=…` is the existing owner-scoped original/thumbnail route; knowing another user's media id grants neither a read nor an attachment.

`SendMessage` and `StartChat.first.Send` attach the list to the created version. The writer validates each image against the chat owner's media references before inserting its ordered `version_attachments` rows. Image-only messages enter context. `ReadPath`, version lists, slot reads and version events include the references. An `EditVersion` without `images` copies its source's list; an explicit list replaces it, and `[]` clears it on the new version. `WriteVersion` and regeneration keep the selected version's images by default; Continue copies its source. An `AmendVersion` without `images` preserves attachments, including the copy made when another fork shows the version; with `images` it replaces the set (an amend even when the text is unchanged). `version_history.images_json` snapshots the complete earlier set on every amendment, including text-only changes, through the text trigger or an explicit entry for an image-only change; a fork's copy copies that history and gets the new set while the version other forks show keeps its own. `VersionAmendments` returns each entry's earlier `images`. Restoring an amendment restores both its text and its images, through the same fork-local amend path; restoring is itself undoable. Selection and forks retain version ownership; ordinary deletion/restore retains attachments, and permanent purges remove them with the version's content. Existing history edits and ST suffix copies also copy these rows.

Queued sends store their list in `queued_messages.images_json` (baseline and equivalence fixture updated), including image-only messages. Normal completion and restart recovery post the same list. Text edits of a queued message keep it.

The registered assembler stages read images from the selected versions, pin their references with history and carry them through the final messages' source pieces. The snapshot format's assembler version is **38** (image controls, token estimates and image-aware cache identity, §15). Image-only history renders `[Image]` in the prompt so text filters and post-processing retain its position. The registered `reply` execution strategy receives an owner-scoped `resolveImages` callback. It loads only the last `maxImages` occurrences (four when unknown) with asynchronous file I/O immediately before provider mapping; snapshots never contain base64 bytes. Missing images fail explicitly. A model without vision fails with `images_unsupported`, with no caption model or provider call. Preview issues also report unsupported images and the last-N limit.

**Taking an image out (fimg).** The history read marks each image the fork took out (`HistoryImage.excluded`, from the `image_exclude` override; pinned with history, so a rebuild replays it). `requestStage` leaves those out of the request messages through `messageImages` (`assembler/images.ts`), so the vision refusal, the `images_limited` issue, reply and summary hydration and the provider boundary's last-N cap count only what's sent. The registered `images` stage, after `request`, records the path's images the request leaves out with their rule in `assembly.imagesLeftOut` and the snapshot (`imagesLeftOut`); an image taken out has rule `user`, "Taken out of the context by you". The other built-in rules, checked in this order, are `hidden` (its message isn't sent), `history` (its message was read but isn't in the request: not summarized yet, or over the budget), `vision` ("<model key> can't see images"; the reply still refuses), and `limit` ("Only the last N images are sent to this model", from `newestImages`, the same helper reply and summary hydration use to choose what they load). Messages the assembly never read are given as depth ranges: `summary` ("Covered by a summary", up to the LCM cursor) and `history` (older than the history read). A plugin's stage registered after `images` can push its own `<plugin>/<rule>` entries, or replace the stage. `GetNextContext` (and the `Cache` event) returns the next request's `images`; the client keeps it with the view's next-context estimate, so the reasons are what the next assembly does, never a second copy of the rules. The inspector lists each such image under Not sent ("Image 3 of message 2: Taken out of the context by you"). In the client, `message.images` shows a corner toggle on each message image (`ToggleIconButton` in `ui/Tooltip.tsx`): on hover or keyboard focus, and always on touch screens; an image taken out keeps it visible and pressed and is dimmed (`opacity-40 grayscale`), with a tooltip saying it isn't sent. An image a rule leaves out is dimmed the same way and its toggle is always shown, pressed and disabled (still focusable and hoverable); the image's tooltip, the toggle's label and the view button's accessible name give the rule's message. While the editor is open its attachment controls replace these previews and toggles. `test/imageExclusion.test.ts` covers fork independence, the next context's reasons (user, limit, vision, hidden), the sent request's images and limit, the inspector and putting it back, plus the actual summary cursor’s covered range. Assembler tests cover budget omission and replacing/disabling the registered image policy.


Chat Completions receives inline data URLs; Anthropic, Responses and Gemini receive SDK image blocks. The input APIs support images on user turns, so an image-bearing assistant/system history turn is projected to a user turn with its existing speaker text; inspector mapping uses that same projection. The common provider boundary still enforces its final payload cap after transforms. Image loading and sending belong to the registered reply policy and assembler stages, so a plugin can replace or disable those policies. The registered image composer, draft uploads, message viewer and summary range transport are built (U16/U22). Image token estimates use provider counts, documented formulas or ledger calibration (§6, §15); provider spending admission detects images in the final payload and reserves conservatively (§6), saturating unknown-context reservations at the largest safe ledger integer instead of overflowing it.

**Direct input and viewing (U16).** `features/chat/images.tsx` registers the effective `chat.composer`, a `chat.images` surface provider, `message.images` preview and `queued.images` preview. Disposing the composer override restores the base text composer; each other contribution can be overridden or disabled through its registry. The composer has a visible paperclip button (Attach images, `AttachButton`) in its action row beside Send, one tap to the picker, whose tooltip says that pasting an image or dropping it anywhere in the chat works too; `AttachmentInput.action` places it and `AttachmentInput.editor` (below the composer) renders only when something is attached or an upload failed, so an empty composer adds no rows. It shows removable thumbnails (`AttachmentList`), accepts image-only sends, and keeps images apart from guidance. Message and queued thumbnails open the owner-scoped original in the shared, keyboard-dismissible dialog. Draft, message and viewer images use `ui/ContentImage.tsx`, which inherits the root blur/hide rules and supplies a hidden-image placeholder. With attachments, the composer shows the effective model's vision capability and last-N limit before Send; `GetNextContext.model` exposes optional `vision` and `maxImages` for this purpose. Text-only refusal and request-preview issues remain U15's server behavior.

**Editing a message's images (fimg).** The message editor (Edit of a generated version makes a new version, Edit of the user's own amends it, and Write your own version) shows the set the version will own: its source's, with removable thumbnails and the same `AttachButton` and `AttachmentList` as the composer; picker, paste and drop on the editor add there, never to the composer. `message.editor-images` contributes to the `message.editor.tools` slot, so it is replaceable/disableable independently. `MessageDraft.images` is persisted with the text by `SaveMessageDraft`, including an image-only edit or an explicit empty set: `drafts.images_json` and the draft history's before/after image snapshots share the text's session, row version and conflict handling. Reloading or opening the account on another device reopens the set; browser recovery copies and the HTTP backup carry it too. Draft-history restore restores the matching text and images. A matching version save or Cancel ends the draft on every device; a stale save with matching text but different images leaves the newer draft active. Image changes made during a save stay in the editor with their recovery copy; Save waits for uploads and is disabled while an upload is pending. The preview's thumbnails/toggles are hidden while its editor is open. Saved amendment history names each earlier image count and restores the full set. `test/editImages.test.ts`, client `test/editorHistory.test.ts` and `e2e/chatImages.spec.ts` cover persistence, history, ownership, fork copies, commit races, uploads and another browser finishing the edit; screenshots cover both widths/themes and 390 px uses real touch input for toggles.

**Attachment drafts (U16).** `composer_images` (baseline and equivalence fixture) stores uploaded references against the fork's composer draft, with an immutable row identity and active/sent/removed states. `GetComposerImages` reads active rows plus requested receipts; `SetComposerImage` adds a reference or records removal. Both enforce the draft's chat and owner, and an added image must be readable by that owner. Writes are single worker transactions, with a maximum of 32 active images. Tombstones make offline replay idempotent: neither a sent image nor a removed one can become active again. `SendMessage.imageDraftIds`, paired with `composer`, consumes exactly those active rows in the same transaction as the post or queued send. A stale send cannot consume them twice, and a refused send rolls the consumption back.

`imageDraft.ts` stores separate browser IndexedDB rows scoped by account, chat and fork. The whole selected batch is persisted before any upload; failure keeps every selected blob for replay on reload and shows an error. A local pending position preserves selection order before server positions exist, including across reloads; later additions retry pending uploads in that order. Local blob thumbnails are allowed by the image CSP while bytes await upload. Upload acknowledgement replaces the blob with the server reference, then saves that reference on the composer before Send becomes available. The server row position keeps attachment selection order independent of browser UUID keys. Opening the composer merges the server draft with the browser's rows and honors sent/removed receipts; uploaded drafts therefore follow the account across browsers/devices. Bytes awaiting upload remain in the selecting browser. Sending captures only that send's rows and marks them locally until acknowledgement; images attached meanwhile remain for the next message. A failed send restores its captured rows. Without a server receipt after reload, an unanswered send stays in the draft with a note to check the chat before sending again. Queued withdrawal restores both text and images under fresh draft identities, with a browser copy before the server write. `e2e/chatImages.spec.ts` covers picker, paste/drop, removal, refresh, failed-upload batch replay and pending thumbnails under CSP, image-only sending, an attachment added during an unanswered send, recovery after a lost send, separate-browser draft reads/removal, model capability warnings, full-size viewing and text-only sending when browser attachment storage is unavailable. It captures the draft and viewer at 1440 and 390 pixels in light/dark themes.


`test/messageImages.test.ts` uses synthetic PNGs and real HTTP/RPC/worker paths for uploads, deduplication, owner isolation and rollback, image-only sends, edits and fork amends, selections, history replacement/undo, deletion/restore, queued posting, snapshot replay after a later attachment edit, last-N selection, explicit text-only refusal, and actual bytes through all four provider APIs. The client preserves image-only context eligibility when applying live version events (`session.ts`); `test/session.test.ts` covers visible, hidden, failed and attachment-cleared versions, so an image-only turn is never labelled empty merely because its text is empty.

## 13. Importing an ST data folder

**Persona selection.** Speaker mapping prefers the message’s persona avatar, then its unambiguous name, then the locked header persona as fallback. All personas who spoke join the cast, preserving earlier messages’ speakers; only the persona mapped from the last user-side message is selected. A last user-side message with no mapped speaker clears the selection, rather than reviving an earlier persona. Without a mapped persona the existing no-selection fallback remains (`{{user}}` = `User`). This also applies to group imports. Card/preset/lorebook exports contain no chat persona set and preserve literal macros. Synthetic import and assembly regressions cover two personas, repeated speakers, multipart files, group imports and pinned names (`personaImport.test.ts`, `assembler.test.ts`).

**Card addition dates.** The format worker captures ST's top-level `create_date` before removing local root metadata. It accepts ISO dates, Unix millisecond numbers, compact/space-separated ST timestamps and English month/AM-PM timestamps. Numeric timestamps may also be digit-only strings; ST humanized timestamps are interpreted as UTC, independent of the server timezone. This travels separately from the card document (`ParsedFile.stCreatedAt`), preserving CCv3's author `data.creation_date` independently. Folder import passes this date, or the file handle's `ctimeMs` for missing/invalid metadata, truncated to SQLite's integer milliseconds. Only a new entity uses it; re-import leaves Created untouched. A direct single-file upload always uses the current time. Browser folder uploads use ST metadata when available and import time otherwise, since browsers do not expose filesystem ctime.

A SillyTavern data folder into the library and chats (DESIGN §1.4, §8.1; DATA-MODEL §14). Read from ST's public source at commit `06bde939` (`public/global.d.ts`, `scripts/chats.js`, `bookmarks.js`, `personas.js`, `authors-note.js`, `world-info.js`, `utils.js`, `extensions/memory`). Shared: `@rp/shared/stFolder.ts` (which paths are read) and `stImportApi.ts` (the RPCs and the report). Server: `formats/stChat.ts` and `formats/stData.ts` (the parsers), `importer/stParse.ts` (their format-worker side), `importer/StImports.ts` (the import on the main thread: sessions, library files, and each chat through its record), `importer/stMapping.ts` (what a chat file means: its card, speakers and state), `importer/stReport.ts`, `db/stImportWrites.ts` and `db/stImportReads.ts`.

**How the folder gets in.** The app usually runs on the user's own computer, where the ST folder is, so the import page takes the folder's path (DESIGN §1.4) and the backend reads it (`importer/stFolderDisk.ts`, `api/stImport.ts`). A folder browser under the path field (`features/library/FolderBrowser.tsx`, `ListFolder`) starts in `IMPORT_START_DIR` (the home folder when unset; a Docker deployment sets it to where ST's data is mounted), which the path field also shows until the user types or opens a folder, and lists subfolders (hidden ones left out, at most 1,000), marking those that look like ST's from the names in them: its install (`server.js` and `data`), its data folder (`default-user`), or a user's folder (`characters` and `chats`). Opening a folder fills the field; it never fills it on its own, so typing isn't overwritten. A browser on another machine can pick the folder instead (`webkitdirectory`), and its files are uploaded.
- **By path:** `ScanStFolder` lists the files under the path that the import reads or lists (the same `classifyStPath` tails `findStRoots` uses), each with its size and, for a chat, the parent it names (`main_chat`), read from its first 64 KiB. It walks at most 6 levels below the path (enough for `<install>/data/<handle>/chats/<card>/<chat>.jsonl`), at most 500,000 entries, skips `node_modules` and `.git`, and doesn't follow symbolic links inside. `ImportStFolderFile` then reads one file, or a chat's part from byte `start` (at most 8 MiB, cut at a line end), and hands the bytes to the same `StImports.file` the upload uses. Each request carries the file's size and modification time from the scan, and a file that changed since is refused (ST may be writing to a chat during the import), so parts never mix two versions of a file. The root must be absolute, and the file's path relative to the user's folder must classify, so `..` and files the import doesn't read are refused. The frontend drives both sources with one loop (`features/library/stUpload.ts`): order, deferred branches, orphans, link choices.
- **Admin only:** the three RPCs refuse anyone but an admin (isolation matrix, §4). On an instance with several users, anyone else could read any user's files by naming them.
- **By upload:** the client sends each file, one HTTP request each (`POST /st-import/file`), through the library upload's limits (§11.4): the same body reader, stall and trickle rules, memory permits and one transfer per user.
- **What's sent.** `classifyStPath` names the files read: `settings.json`, `User Avatars/*` images, `characters/*.png|json|charx`, `worlds/*.json`, `OpenAI Settings/*.json`, `groups/*.json`, `chats/<folder>/*.jsonl` and `group chats/*.jsonl`. Nothing else (backups, thumbnails, `secrets.json`, extensions) is sent, and the server classifies every path again and refuses the rest with `400`, as it does any path with an empty, `.` or `..` segment, a backslash, a colon or a control character. Text-completion presets and instruct, context, system-prompt and reasoning templates are listed by path in `StartStImport` and counted in the report as unsupported (DESIGN §1.2), never uploaded.
- **ST's multi-user layout** (`data/<handle>/`): `findStRoots` finds each user folder in what was picked (the ST install, its `data/`, or one user's folder), and the user picks one handle, which goes into their own account. **Not built:** the admin-only option of one user per handle.
- **Order** (`ST_FILE_ORDER`): settings, personas, cards, lorebooks, presets, groups, chats, group chats, so a chat finds what it links to.
- **Chats come in parts** of at most 8 MiB (`ST_CHAT_PART_BYTES`), cut at line ends and sent in order (`part`, and `last` on the last one): a chat can be far larger than any card, and a part bounds what one request holds in memory. A line longer than a part can't be sent. A part out of order, or after the server lost the chat's earlier parts (a restart), is answered `restart`, and the client sends the chat again from its start, which the matching below makes safe.

**Summarize settings.** `settings.json` retains `extension_settings.memory.prompt` and `promptWords` alongside the injection position, depth, role, template and enabled state (ST 1.19.0’s public `extensions/memory/index.js`). `StImportSummarySettings` saves these mapped settings under the importing user’s `import.st.memory` and fills `generation.lcm.tail` only if no instruction is set. The import lease fences old runs; re-importing preserves an instruction the user changed. No provider request starts on import. A newly imported chat stores a copy of the account's summarization default with summaries off (§16 *Defaults*); importing into an existing chat leaves its settings alone.

**Selected chat-completion preset.** `settings.json` also reads `oai_settings.preset_settings_openai`. When that named file from `OpenAI Settings/` is successfully imported (including an unchanged file), `StImportDefaultPreset` adopts its ref as the user's `generation.preset` only if the stored setting is absent or JSON null. The writer checks the import lease and preset ownership and makes the conditional write in one transaction; an existing choice, even one whose ref is no longer live, is preserved. The report names the preset ST used and says whether it became the default or the existing default was kept. Until its file is imported, the report warns that the selected preset file has not been imported from the folder. Other presets in the folder never become the default automatically.

**Unchanged persona notes.** When a library file is unchanged, `GetCardImportNote` resolves its existing first imported card note in a reader worker, using the same identity lookup as `ImportFile`. The persona-to-note map is populated before chat mapping even on re-import, so newly added chats with ST author-note frequency zero switch the persona note off. This reads the existing note without importing it again or changing local edits. `test/stPersonaReimport.test.ts` permanently covers the two-import inventory reproduction with synthetic files.

**The import** (`StImports`): one per user at a time, held in memory on the main thread until an hour after its last request or the user's next `StartStImport`. It holds what links chats to cards (each card's name, the first 4 KB of its greetings and its world; personas; groups; lorebook names), what earlier imports recorded, and the report, never a file. What it holds about the folder, and the summaries of the chat being read, is capped at 16M characters (about 50 MB of heap); a file that would add more is refused, and the report says to import the rest in another run. Every file is parsed in a format worker (§11.3) and stored through the writer; the main thread never parses a file or a document. `GetStImportReport` reads the report. Another user's import is `NotFound`, and a file sent to it is `404` before its body is read. Its RPCs and the route are in the isolation matrix (§4): cross-user probes, and admin-only for the three that read the disk.

**Parsing** (`formats/stChat.ts`, `formats/stData.ts`), pure and under the shared caps (§8):
- **A chat part** is read line by line, each line its own JSON document through `parseJson`, under the shared depth, container, value and prototype-key caps. A line that isn't a message is skipped and counted, as ST skips it. The first line is the header when it has `chat_metadata` (or `user_name`) and no `mes`; old group chats have none. Messages cross to the main thread as JSON text in batches of at most 100 messages or 1 MiB, each with the speakers it names, which the main thread passes to the writer unread with only those speakers' cards. A part has at most 1,000 speakers; messages past that have none, and the report says how many. Before the cap, a file naming a new speaker on every line made each batch carry and check every speaker so far, quadratic in the file (a review measured 17.6 s for 20,000 lines, and about two hours of writer time for one 8 MiB part).
- **Caps:** a part 8 MiB; 1,000 swipes per message; 1,000 variables, each value at most 1 MB (the macro engine's cap, §10); names 200 characters; greetings compared on their first 4 KB. `settings.json` 32 MiB (it holds every extension's settings), a group file 1 MiB, 10,000 personas, 1,000 members per group. Each cap has a crafted file in `test/formats/stChat.test.ts` or `test/stImport.test.ts`.
- **Dates** as ST reads them (`parseTimestamp`): epoch milliseconds, digits, ISO 8601, `June 19, 2023 2:20pm` (read as UTC: ST uses the browser's time zone, which the server can't know), and ST's humanized forms. A message without one, or with a number past what a date holds (a timestamp column takes only whole numbers it stores exactly), takes the time of the one before it, so a re-import gives it the same time.
- **Only what's mapped is read** from `settings.json`: personas, `world_info_settings.world_info` (`globalSelect`, whose books become the user's global lorebooks, §15 "Lorebook activation"; `charLore`), the default author's note, the characters' own author's notes (`note.chara`) and `note.allowWIScan`, the Summarize extension's settings and whether it's disabled, the selected chat-completion preset name above, and `power_user.default_persona` for chats without a user turn. The rest, connection settings included, is dropped unread.

**The mapping** (DATA-MODEL §14):
- **Messages:** one slot per message. `swipes[]` are its versions, `swipe_id` selected; `mes`, which ST shows and sends, is selected even when it isn't among the swipes (as an extra version). Each version's time is its `swipe_info` date or the message's `send_date`. `is_user` decides the writer (`human`, else `model`); every version is `origin: imported`, `macro_mode: verbatim`, `status: complete`. `extra.reasoning` (and each swipe's) is the version's `reasoning`. `is_system`, ST's one hidden flag (`hideChatMessageRange` sets it), is `context_excluded`. Attachments aren't imported; the report counts them. `extra.api` and `model` aren't kept: they name ST's connections, not ours.
- **Speakers:** a user message is the persona its `force_avatar` names (ST's `/thumbnail?type=persona&file=…`, or `User Avatars/…`), else the one persona with its name when unambiguous, else the chat's locked persona (`chat_metadata.persona`). A model message is the card its `original_avatar` or `force_avatar` names, else the chat's card in a single chat, or the one member with its name in a group. Narrator messages (`extra.type: narrator`) have none.
- **Chat state**, as state events with origin `import` at the chat's start: `participant.join` for the card (or the group's members) and the personas that spoke, `presence.set false` for muted members, `played_by_me.set` for only the persona mapped from the last user-side message (last occurrence, including a return to an earlier persona and across file parts); with no user-side message, the chat's locked persona or then `power_user.default_persona` is selected and joined to the cast when its avatar was imported; with neither available, no persona is selected and `{{user}}` reads `User`. The remaining events are `lorebook.attach` for the chat's world, the card's (`extensions.world`) and ST's extra books for the character (`charLore`), `lorebook.binding` to identify each card's primary ref, `lorebook.embedded` off for each card whose own `character_book` ST never sent, and `var.set` for each variable. ST's global lorebooks (`globalSelect`) are imported but not attached. A lorebook not in the folder is reported once per import, not attached.
- **A card's own lorebook** (DESIGN §1.3). ST 1.19 never sends a card's embedded `character_book`: `getCharacterLore` loads only the lorebook file `extensions.world` names (`loadWorldInfo`), and "Import Card Lore" (the dialog `checkEmbeddedWorld` shows once per card) is what makes that file from the card's book and links it. So a chat with a card that has a book but whose `extensions.world` file isn't in the folder gets `lorebook.embedded` off, as ST had it; a re-import that finds the file turns it back on. That `extensions.world` name is the book's name in the ST of whoever exported the card (ST writes it on save), so it isn't reported missing. Applies like the rest of a chat's state: at a new chat's start, and for a chat imported earlier only with new messages.
- **The author's note** becomes a chat-owned note (`kind: author_note`) with `author_note.set`, and `note.activate` only if ST sent it: its text isn't empty and its frequency isn't 0 (DESIGN §4.1). No frequency is kept: a note goes every turn. ST's default note applies to a chat without one of its own, as in ST. Empty chat notes and default settings without text are kept too, off: their position, depth and role still place what rides on the author's note (owner question 6: it still goes where the note sits). Only missing chat and default settings leave no imported note. In-chat it's placed at its depth and role; after or before the main prompt it's a `section` placement with its imported role [P]. With `allowWIScan` it gets `scan_lore`.
- **A character's own author's note** (`note.chara`, matched to a card by its avatar file name without the extension) becomes, when the card file is imported and the note has text, a private card note titled "Character author's note" (`kind: character_author_note`, `StImportCharacterNote`, one per card, merged on a re-import) with `on_by_default` as ST's `useChara`, at the top of the author's note for ST's "before" and "replace" (owner, 2026-10-28: "replace" never replaces anything; the chat's author's note is still sent) and at its bottom for "after", and `scan_lore` with `allowWIScan`. ST applies it only in one-to-one chats, so an imported group chat switches it off (`note.deactivate` once; `ChatState.characterNotesOff` remembers it). A one-to-one chat whose non-empty author's note ST would have replaced gets an info line (`st.character_note_replaced`). The report counts them (`character_note`).
- **Personas** become cards in the persona folder (tagged `persona` when created; a re-import leaves tags alone) with their original avatar file, named and described from `settings.json`. A persona placed anywhere but the prompt's persona description (at depth with its role, at the top or bottom of the author's note, or not sent) gets an **ordinary** note on its card titled "Persona description", on in every chat, with the text at that placement (`author_note_top`, `author_note_bottom`, `depth`, `none`), and the card's description is left empty: one copy, nothing hidden (DESIGN §4.1, owner 2026-10-28). At the author's note's top or bottom it joins each chat's own author's note, at that note's depth and role, immediately before or after it, so every imported chat gets ST's position from its own note without a copy of the text or a per-chat setting (card notes have no per-chat position: a card note's placement is in its library revision, and only a chat's own notes are pinned per chat, by `note.binding`). ST 1.19.0 (`setFloatingPrompt`, `addPersonaDescriptionExtensionPrompt`) still sends the persona with an author's note that's empty but due, and none of it with frequency 0: a chat whose author's note had frequency 0 gets `note.deactivate` for the persona's note (`FolderContext.personaNotes`, from the persona's import in the same run) and for the card's Character Author's Note, which ST adds the same way (both in `characterNotesOff`, once). A re-import whose persona file is unchanged doesn't know the note, so a chat new to that run keeps it on. An avatar `settings.json` doesn't list is a persona named after its file, as ST shows it. A persona's hash covers its image and its settings, so a changed description updates it.
- **Cards, lorebooks, presets** go through `ImportFile` (§11.1) like uploads, with the path in the folder as `source_path`. A file must be what its folder holds: a preset in `worlds/` fails.
- **Groups:** a group chat belongs to the group whose `chats` (or `chat_id`) names it, and is titled `<group>: <chat>`. One no group lists is imported with its speakers matched by avatar.

**Linking a chat to its card** (single chats): the card whose file the chat's folder is named after (ST's own link), else the one card with the folder's name (a renamed card file), else the one card whose avatar the messages name, else the one card named as the chat's character speaks (a card downloaded again under a new file name, `name (1)`, keeps its old chats' folder), else the one card whose greeting the chat's first message is, with `{{char}}` filled in and `{{user}}` matching any name. More than one candidate, or none, puts the chat in the report's `links` with its candidates and why (no card has the folder's file name or the speaker's name; ST's `undefined` folder, where it saves a chat made with no character selected), and it isn't imported until the user picks a card (or none); the client then sends it again with `card=<entity>`. The writer checks that card like any id it's given, so another user's is refused.

**Branches and checkpoints** (`chat_metadata.main_chat`, ST's `bookmarks.js`) merge into their parent's chat, with one durable fork per file record (fork independence unit 9). A new main file uses the chat's first fork; a merged file gets its own fork, inheriting its recorded parent's bounded lineage, even when both files end at the same message. Fork creation for import does not switch the chat's current fork. Matching shares slots and versions by text, time and writer; a different selected swipe writes a `select` override with origin `import` in the file's fork. Hidden-flag differences can still start independent slots. Messages without a match append under the last match. State and note events written at finish belong to that file's fork, so shared-tip checkpoints keep separate state. Re-import checks non-import events on that fork's path through its bounded lineage (including dormant positional choices, excluding suppressed events). A local choice blocks imported changes to the same setting or target, including opposite switches; unrelated settings and choices made only in another fork do not block updates. An imported note locally edited, switched, rebound or deleted retains its identity and binding when ST changes or removes it; an upstream note edited in the library is likewise retained. The file record still remembers the latest ST state, so replay is stable. Parents are sent before children; unresolved parents defer, then import as separate chats when explicitly orphaned.

**A fork's name from ST** (`stForkName` in `importer/stMapping.ts`) names the branch a merged branch or checkpoint file makes. Automatic names are limited to ST's generated forms. Current ST generates `<parent> - Branch #<n>` or `<parent> - Checkpoint #<n>`, after stripping that same kind's existing suffix and legacy prefix from the actual parent name ([ST 1.19 `bookmarks.js`, lines 88–110](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/7e8663cd9c184a550b37238218bdd32c6efc68e9/public/scripts/bookmarks.js#L88-L110), [209–217](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/7e8663cd9c184a550b37238218bdd32c6efc68e9/public/scripts/bookmarks.js#L209-L217)). The importer passes `main_chat` as that context; an unrelated prefix, an unknown parent, or a modern `Bookmark #` suffix does not count as automatic. Legacy automatic names require the date: `Branch #<message> - <date>` ([ST 1.12, line 149](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/1.12.0/public/scripts/bookmarks.js#L149)), `Checkpoint #<n> - <date>` ([lines 58–76](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/1.12.0/public/scripts/bookmarks.js#L58-L76)), or `Bookmark #<n> - <date>` ([ST 1.10, lines 59–77](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/1.10.0/public/scripts/bookmarks.js#L59-L77)). Bare `Branch #42`, `Checkpoint #42`, `Bookmark #42` and timestamps remain user names. Current ST saves nonempty checkpoint input verbatim ([lines 277–292](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/7e8663cd9c184a550b37238218bdd32c6efc68e9/public/scripts/bookmarks.js#L277-L292)); a user choosing exactly an automatic filename is inherently indistinguishable. Other names are cleaned as a name typed here is, with an ST date suffix removed, and cut to the 100 UTF-16 units `NameBranch` takes without splitting a code point.

**Names belong to forks.** `StImportFinishChat` places a merged file's custom name on `forks.name`, including checkpoints with no messages of their own. Automatic filenames supply no label. The record's `forkId` keeps the name attached across shared-prefix swipe changes, writer changes, truncation, parent movement and reimports; no naming collision can force a suffix copy. `name_user` protects both a local rename and an explicit clear. A record already named by import keeps its label. A new source path is a separate file and fork, even when its bytes match another file; the old fork keeps its local name. Slot naming columns are frozen by `slots_branch_name_frozen` and serve only as legacy switch-point labels. Records lacking `forkId` resolve their recorded end to a fork when claimed, or create one if no fork ends there; `stImportFork.ts` remains solely to resume old interrupted suffix copies. There is no migration of deployed user data in this baseline-only build.

**Re-running is idempotent and resumable.**
- **Library files** are matched by `source_path`: the same hash is `unchanged`, another hash is a re-import into the same upstream ref (§3.9), merged with the user's edits. A file with the hash of an upstream ref the user already has, from a manual upload say, is that ref.
- **Chat files** are recorded in owned `jobs` rows (`kind: st-import-chat`): source, chat, fork, notes, mapped state and end. Re-import matches the recorded path, preserving local selections, amendments, hiding and deletions as that fork shows them. Only this file's fork advances to its new end, including from the nearest live ancestor after tail deletions; local continuations keep their tip and composer. If an unmatched ST message would replace a suffix carrying local selections, edits, hides, deletions or positional state, or a message the user inserted where the replaced suffix starts (below its parent, or above the first message when the first is replaced) or inside it, that fork likewise keeps its current path and tip (an insertion counts while it is placed in the fork, deleted or not, as a deletion counts); the record retains ST's replacement path for matching later imports. The check uses indexed lineage overrides, amendment history and visible event provenance rather than copying the old suffix. A message the user inserted is never a candidate for a file message (matching reads children with `inserted = 0`), so a re-import can neither move a fork onto one nor overwrite it; an append-only re-import goes below the file's last message, after any insertion there. Other forks at the same tip never follow an import's extension. Unchanged replay neither moves a tip nor changes its name. An unfinished import retains its fork, last matched position and prior path across batches and claims. A deleted fork makes the record binned, just as a deleted chat does; importing does not restore either. `forkVersionsReimport.test.ts` covers insertions before, at, inside and after a rewritten suffix, above a rewritten first message, and a deleted insertion restored in its original gap after re-import; a shortened file also retains live and deleted insertions in its removed tail.
- **Old ST messages:** the file's recorded slot at the same depth and with the same writer wins even when ST overwrote every swipe or an identical sibling exists. New text becomes an imported version on the existing slot. Its selection changes only in the recorded fork, through `writeSelect`, and shortening records a history reduction only there. Local effective selection, replacement, hide and deletion overrides in that fork, and amendment history, preserve the user's choice. Choices in other forks do not block import. Shared-prefix changes retain every suffix slot, version history, attachment and event without copying. Swipes remain available to every fork's picker (owner Q1); selection is independent. Purged placeholders match only by their recorded position, timestamp and writer, and never receive erased text. Changed versions or selections report updated; replay reports unchanged. Existing message drafts keep their original source, optimistic conflict handling and recovery (§3.10).
- **The import never removes chat content** [P] (a design change of 2026-10-12, after reviews kept finding what an undo had to protect). The record is written with the chat's first message, as `processing`. A new chat is visible from then on, titled `<title> (import unfinished)` until its import finishes (the client shows the marker as a badge, §12), with its leaf following its messages as they come in (from unset, or where the import last put it, so a leaf the user moved stays). An import that stops halfway leaves the chat as it was: a new import run reports it (`st.unfinished`) and finishes it from the record, or the user deletes it like any chat, into the recycle bin; the bin and its purge are the only place content is removed. A new chat is finished as new, and a branch from where the stopped run went its own way (`firstAdded`, kept in the record), with its parent's state as the state it changes. A chat that fails or whose request is interrupted gives back the budget its summaries held.
- **One run writes a file at a time** [P]. The record holds a lease, the import run's id: a run claims it (`StImportClaim`, which also returns the record as it is now, so a run never acts on a view of it another run or a finished import made stale), and every batch and the finish check it (`ImportLeaseLost` otherwise). Finishing clears it, so no earlier run writes a finished file. Starting an import makes it the user's current run (`StImportBegin`, a `jobs` row of kind `st-import-run`), and only the current run may claim a record, start a chat or write one: a run's request still on its way after the user started another writes nothing, and the leases of runs that are over count as expired.
- Anything in the recycle bin (a chat, a library entry) isn't imported again; a chat deleted for good is. Messages in the bin match, so a re-import doesn't bring back what was deleted here. A message deleted for good has empty versions: its placeholder matches only on this file's recorded prior path, at the same depth with the retained timestamp and writer. Its erased text and versions stay erased; new messages continue below it. Purged versions never match by their empty text. Whole deleted branches, including purged subtree roots, remain hidden.

**The report** (`StImportReport`): counts per kind (added, updated, unchanged, skipped, failed), messages, summaries, greeting-only chats skipped (a chat of exactly one message, not the user's: the chat ST makes by opening a card, DESIGN §1.4. The chat's first message is held back until a second one comes or the file ends, so the outcome doesn't depend on where the parts fall, and the rule is the same when an earlier import made the chat. A file of no messages is skipped too. A chat of two or more messages with none of the user's goes in [P]. Earlier versions held messages in `DATA_DIR/st-staging`; at startup that folder is removed once, unless it's a link, which is left alone), unsupported files, what the user must act on (at most 500), the link choices and hidden-run offers (at most 2,000 each), and how many didn't fit. Its messages quote files through `shownText`, and are rendered as text.

The report holds only what a user must act on (DESIGN §1.2, owner 2026-10-28): `Report.issue` drops infos, and of a file's own report only its errors are kept (its warnings say what the import chose, which the entry shows). What's left: files that failed or weren't updated, the import's cap (`st.too_much`), an unfinished earlier import (`st.unfinished`), edits that conflict (`st.conflicts`), a file whose earlier import is in the recycle bin (`st.in_bin`, a warning saying to restore it or delete it for good), a lorebook a chat names that wasn't in the folder (`st.world_missing`), one ST applies to every chat that wasn't (`st.global_world_missing`, measured on each read from `globalSelect` and the books imported), ST's selected preset missing (`st.preset_missing`) or not adopted because the user had chosen a default (`st.preset_kept`). The other notes the importer had made (the summary's placement and history, a character's note ST replaced, a chat without a user turn, a fork with no message of its own, messages shown differently, the global books marked, a chat no group lists, the preset adopted) are infos, so not listed: each is shown where it applies (the note, the chat, the lorebook's switch, the default preset) or changes nothing the user does. The counts, messages, summaries, greeting-only chats and unsupported files stay in `StImportReport` for the tests; the client doesn't show them.

**Hidden runs** (DESIGN §1.4): a run of at least 10 hidden messages from message 0 [P: the threshold, since the decision says only "long"] is offered for un-hiding, once, for a chat the import made. `UnhideImportedRun` clears `context_excluded` on the path from the chat's first message to the run's last.

**The client** (`features/library/stImport.tsx`, `stUpload.ts`), reached from the import page ("Import a SillyTavern folder", `/import/st`): the folder picker (`FileDrop` with `directory`, which takes no drops: a dropped folder has no paths), the ST user to import when the folder has several, what will be sent, then progress by file and bytes. A chat is read from disk a part at a time, never whole. It reads each chat's first line to send parents before their branches; branches still deferred are sent again after the rest, then on their own. The report shows, by kind, what was added, updated or failed (not what was unchanged or skipped), the chats to link (each with its candidates, by label and card file, or any library card when there are none, and "No card"), the hidden runs with Un-hide and whether a summary covers them, and "Needs your attention" with the report's issues. `e2e/stImport.spec.ts` drives it with a synthetic folder in ST's multi-user layout, at phone and desktop width.

**Imported summaries** (DESIGN §8.1; [P] throughout, as the owner's details are open):
- **Where ST keeps one:** the Summarize extension (`extensions/memory`) saves each summary on a message (`extra.memory`); the current one is the latest on any message but the last (`getLatestMemoryFromChat` skips the last). ST sends it through `extension_settings.memory.template`, `{{summary}}` replaced, at its `position` (-1 none, 0 after the main prompt, 1 in-chat at `depth` with `role`, 2 before the main prompt), unless the extension is disabled. The `{{summary}}` macro reads it too. In ST 1.19.0 both depend on the extension being loaded: `setMemoryContext` sets the extension prompt at the configured position (`public/scripts/extensions/memory/index.js` l.964–965), the macro is registered only when the module loads (l.1111–1130), and a disabled extension's script is never loaded (`public/scripts/extensions.js` l.626–628), so with the extension disabled ST sends the summary nowhere, and with position None only through `{{summary}}`.
- **Where it goes:** a chat-owned note with `kind: imported_summary`, titled "Summary (from ST)" (notes imported before keep their title), holding the current summary, ST's template and its placement, with the earlier summaries as its earlier revisions. It is switched on exactly when ST was sending it: the extension enabled, and either its position not None or an enabled prompt of the ST preset selected at import (`oai_settings.preset_settings_openai`, read when that preset file is imported, before the chats) holding `{{summary}}` (`summaryMacroPrompts`). Its placement is ST's position while the extension was enabled, else none, so a summary sent only through the macro isn't also injected. From then on only its switch decides whether it is sent (DESIGN §8 [P]). Each revision records the message ST saved its text on (`source_slot_id`, the earliest of a run of the same text), so the assembler can send the one a path has (§15). The template, frequency and coverage are on the current revision only (each keeps its placement, which a note needs), and the history keeps the latest 200 summaries within 256K characters, so storing a chat's summary is one short write however long its template (a 128 KiB template with 1,000 summaries took 69 ms under load; when every revision carried the template, a review measured 13 MB written from a 141 KB upload, holding the writer 689 ms). Notes already have placement, state events and history, so no migration was needed; a field on the chat was the alternative. A summary kept in the author's note stays in the author's note. The assembler places it as ST did, and `{{summary}}` reads it (§15 "Imported summaries").
- **Covering the hidden run:** the summary covers the run when the message it's on is at or after the run's last message, since ST summarizes the chat up to the message it saves the summary on. The note then records `covers` (the run's first and last slot and its length), and the offer says so (`summaryCovers`). A summary from before the run's end is reported as not covering it. **Import writes no L1 summary row:** enabling LCM is when the user chooses between adopting the summary as that range's L1 and redoing LCM (DESIGN §8.1); the adoption is `EnableLcm` (§16, *Enabling and backfill*), which reads `covers`.
- A summary on the last message, which ST ignores, is left unused, as ST leaves it.

**Measured** (2026-10-12, `test/stImportScale.test.ts`, opt-in with `ST_IMPORT_SCALE=1`; source workers, load average 15 to 20): a synthetic 50,000-message chat (80.7 MiB; a third of the model's messages with three swipes; about 1 KB each) imports in 20.8 s, and again, unchanged, in 3.3 s. The writer took it in 500 transactions of 100 messages: 448 over 25 ms, median 29 ms, 95th percentile 68 ms, longest 192 ms (with 250 per batch: median 69 ms, longest 389 ms). The test process (the server, its workers and the test's own copy of the file) peaked at 1.06 GB RSS and 218 MB of main-thread heap, and the main thread's event loop was never more than 17 ms late (25 ms in an earlier run).

**Tested:** `test/stImport.test.ts` imports a synthetic folder (`test/support/stFolder.ts`) through the real server as the client does (`test/support/stUpload.ts`): cards, personas, a lorebook, a preset, a group, a main chat with a branch, a greeting-only chat, a renamed card, a chat linked by its greeting, (`test/stImportLinks.test.ts`) a card downloaded again under a new file name linked by its speaker's name, ST's `undefined` folder, and a card's own lorebook not reported missing, an ambiguous link resolved by a choice, a hidden run with several summaries (one on the last message), a summary without a hidden run, a summary kept in the author's note, and unsupported files. It checks the graph, speakers, state, notes and report. A second run of the whole folder in 600-byte parts changes nothing; a chat sent in parts matches one sent whole, and takes ST's later messages on a re-import; hostile paths are refused before the body is read. `test/stImportReimport.test.ts` imports ten synthetic messages, then reproduces ST 1.19’s `public/script.js` writes: a new selected swipe on old message 4 (zero-based), an in-place edit overwriting its selected swipe, both changes plus two appended messages, and an edit after a local continuation. It asserts the same slot ids and parents, preserved and selected versions, version metadata, no duplicate suffix, leaf and composer, updated reports and unchanged replay. Further cases preserve local selections, edits, hides and deletions (including purge), and prefer the recorded path over an identical sibling’s text. Its exhaustive graph snapshots use the existing fixture worker with a read-only connection, closing it and waiting for worker exit before the helper completes; a main-thread SQLite guard rejects application-database connections while allowing the startup instance lock. `test/stImportForkNames.test.ts` and `test/stImportForkNamesReview.test.ts` check automatic filename recognition and user names resembling it. `test/stImportNamePlacement.test.ts` checks fork-owned names and clears across Unicode and nested files, shared-prefix swipe and writer changes, replay and identical-byte files under distinct source paths; shared-tip checkpoints get separate named forks. `test/stImportSharedSelection.test.ts` checks fork selection overrides, unchanged shared slot IDs and suffix size, local amendment history and events, parent isolation and replay, including an interrupted 205-message import. The bin tests verify re-import creates no copies and preserves purge and restore. `test/forkVersionsReimport.test.ts` checks that re-import addresses the recorded fork even while a checkpoint is current, retaining each fork's amendment and attached events on the shared suffix; and that a re-import stops at an insertion right above the rewritten message, inside the rewritten suffix and above a rewritten first message, goes on as before with an insertion before the change (which stays in place), and never matches an appended ST message to an inserted one with the same text, writer and time. `test/stImportLocalState.test.ts` covers local variable choices, unrelated and other-fork updates, edited/switched/deleted notes, a writer change retaining a locally edited suffix, and shared-tip note editor routing with cross-chat fork refusal. `e2e/stReimportDrafts.spec.ts` covers saved and in-flight message drafts at desktop and phone widths, reload, a second re-import while version reads are held, Save’s original source and Cancel’s recovered text.

**Known limits:**
- A line of a chat file can't be longer than a part (8 MiB).
- A re-import changes a chat's state only from new messages on; without any, nothing moves.
- Speakers and links are matched only against the cards in the folder imported.
- The writer decodes each batch before its transaction, but still in the writer thread.

**Registered parts of the ST import** (PLUGINS §8). The folder import has two registries of §6's kind, apart from the library importers (§11.2), whose result cannot express chat parts. **Folder handlers** (`StImports.handlers`, `importer/StImports.ts`) dispatch a file by its kind (`settings`, `persona_avatar`, `card`, `world`, `preset`, `group`, `chat`, `group_chat`). The built-ins register at layer start through the same API plugins use. A disabled handler fails with a specific message and a `st.handler_disabled` report issue. The chat handlers retain the part, lease and recycle-bin handling. A handler reads through typed format operations (`Formats.st`) and stores through the writer's typed operations. The path classification, sessions, holds and ownership checks stay fixed.

**ST parser sources** (`StParserRegistry`, `importer/stParserRegistry.ts`) are exposed alongside `Formats` on the server. Entries name a parser id (`chat-part`, `settings`, `group`, `library-file`, `persona`), a trusted module URL and an export with the corresponding `StParsers` signature (`importer/stParse.ts`). The built-ins register the same source descriptors, using the bundled `rp:st-parsers` module. A plugin uses an absolute module URL; it may wrap the exported `builtinParsers`. These references come only from trusted server registrations, never uploaded contents. Each `Formats.st` job captures the effective source when its Effect executes and transfers it with its bytes through the typed RPC; the module is loaded only in the format worker. This works in source and bundle mode, across the pool and after a crash. `disable` sends an explicit absent source and returns a `st.parser_disabled` report. Closing a registration scope restores the previous source for new jobs; already admitted jobs retain their selection. ESM modules remain cached: changed code needs a new module URL. This is worker execution plumbing; plugin installation and consent UI are not built. `test/stFolderSeam.test.ts` covers the real workers and HTTP upload path.

## 14. Editing the library

The card and preset editors (DESIGN §4, §6.2): every field edits through a draft on the server, saving commits a revision, and nothing is ever lost. Contract in `@rp/shared/editorApi.ts` (merged into `AppApi`), the shared logic in `@rp/shared/editing.ts`, handlers in `packages/server/src/api/editor.ts`, storage in `db/editorWrites.ts` and `db/editorReads.ts`.

**Main is permanent** (owner, 2026-10-28). A preset always has a `main` prompt and an order entry for it. The editor permits switching it off and moving it, but never deleting it, including an imported Main marked as a custom prompt. `withMain` and `presetOrder` in `@rp/shared/presetOrder.ts` put a missing Main first, switched off, keeping any existing prompt text; if the prompt itself is absent they supply an empty Main. ST imports, our document import validation, editor reads of old revisions, order writes, and revision storage/restores apply this rule. Old revisions remain unchanged in history. Reordering and resetting retain the slot. Notes before and after a switched-off Main still occupy its empty slot, including when Main is placed in-chat. The assembler no longer skips those notes for an absent Main; no fidelity-gate matcher existed specifically for that case. Regression tests cover import/reimport, document validation, editor order writes, restore storage, note placement, and the custom Main's browser controls.

**Card fork surfaces** (card forks unit 5; `features/cardForks/`). The library row, card page and editor offer Fork, Forks, Compare with parent (or Compare with family for a root), Mark as a fork of… and Unlink. Fork starts with the parent's saved display name, version and in-character name; version is focused and the display name/version collision check blocks creation until one differs. It copies the card and its notes through the existing `ForkCard`, optionally moving drafts after they reach the server, and opens the independent card's editor. Non-card variants retain their previous dialog and following semantics.

The editor and card page have a Forks section; the Forks action and the home grid's fork-count/fork badges open the same family list as an indented tree. Each member shows its display name, version, last use and chat count. Usage comes from the shared shell query, counting visible fork views as chats and dating a card by its own latest speaking activity, consistently with the card page and home; family metadata supplies lineage and deleted states. Live and archived members link to their editor, the current card is marked, and binned/purged members remain visible with state badges. Provenance says “Forked from” with the parent's name and state, and “Made for” with a link to the named chat when applicable. Comparisons are read-only field diffs of the two saved heads (drafts excluded), using the existing revision diff UI, with a selector for any other family member. A binned member offers a restore explanation, and a purged member says its content is unavailable, instead of attempting to open its editor. Taking text over is ordinary editing. The parent picker searches live and archived cards by display name, version and in-character name (`LibraryRef.modelName`, read from the saved head in the database worker), ranks matching display names first and surfaces writer refusals (including a cycle).
