Mark and Unlink only call `SetCardParent`; they write no content revisions. `LibraryRef.hasImport` reports whether an upstream has an import line; the library's Update from file action is shown on cards only when that is true, never on a newly made fork.

**Card contribution seam** (`features/cardForks/surfaces.tsx`). `cardContributions` uses the same reversible, reactive registry as chat contributions. `CardSurface` derives its rendered entries from the captured registry snapshot, so React Compiler observes replacements, additions, disablement and disposal after mounting; removing an action also unmounts its dialog. Hosts supply the entity and optionally an editor store, and request actions, provenance, family or badge placement through `CardSurface`. The fork feature registers each command separately (`card-forks.fork`, `.family`, `.compare`, `.parent`, `.unlink`) and each non-action surface separately (`.family-surface`, `.provenance-surface`, `.badge-surface`), with lazy bodies. Plugins can override, disable or add one contribution; hosts contain no fork-specific actions. This is the new minimal seam for card actions and panels where the chat registries did not apply. Browser tests (`e2e/cardForkSurfaces.spec.ts`) import the realistic synthetic ST folder, make forks through the UI and cover the dialogs, multi-generation tree, arbitrary family comparison, linking/unlinking, cycle refusal, provenance, deleted members and badges at 1440/390 in both themes. The registry test covers replacement, disabling and restoration. The mounted Vite/React Compiler regression in `e2e/chatContributions.spec.ts` checks all four card placements across the card page, editor, library and home grid, including open-dialog removal. Card-fork browser regressions also check archived-parent search by in-character name, family counts against shell fork views and saved-head comparisons excluding drafts.

**Creating a prompt preset** (U11; `CreatePreset`, `features/editor/NewPreset.tsx`): the editor registers New card and New prompt preset in the `library.actions` slot. A named preset is created as an owned library upstream through the existing writer operation, with the built-in ordered prompt document, Main and Post-History Instructions forbidding card overrides, and regex unapproved. It opens immediately in the existing preset workspace, with the same drafts, history, save and export paths as an imported preset. It contains no model settings. Server and browser regressions cover creation, safe defaults, editing and reload.

**Revisioned text comparisons** (U17, 2026-10-28; DESIGN §6.2, UX-REWORK “Editing and saving”). Message and summary editors use the existing lazy `CodeEditor`, with change gutters and the existing server drafts and browser recovery. The registered `message.text-editor` and `summary.text-editor` contributions supply the editor; `message.editor.tools` and `summary.editor.tools` add a live, full-text “Compare with saved text” view. An edit compares against its source message version (even when a different swipe is selected); a new written version compares against empty text. Summary drafts fix their comparison and save base on the first keystroke, before autosave, and retain their named revision when another tab selects a different one. The editor reads that revision's immutable text; a failed comparison read offers Retry saved text without closing or discarding the draft. Saving and discarding still use the existing draft lifecycle.

Library history now uses the paginated revision browser for notes as well as cards and presets: selected revision against its first parent (empty for the first revision), or against the current working document. Restoring makes a new revision; card name checks apply only to cards. All library Save comparisons use each changed field's draft revision base and the full-text merge view. The message version picker's Changes view, saved amendment history (earlier text → current saved version), and expandable summary revision comparisons (selected earlier revision → saved summary) also use full-text merge views. Expandable comparisons, including each library-history field, mount their merge editors only while open. These reuse `RevisionDiff`: side by side at desktop widths, unified removed/added text below 640 px, read only, with complete documents available through scrolling. `e2e/revisionText.spec.ts` covers note/preset Save, history and restore with long text at 1440/390 in light/dark; `e2e/messageHistory.spec.ts` covers restored message/summary drafts, change gutters, current comparisons, saved amendments and summary revision comparisons at the same sizes and themes. Existing draft safety tests use the code editor's full-document assertion helper. Review regressions in those browser suites check comparison-read retry with subsequent editing and Save, and allocation only for open library fields; `test/editorHistory.test.ts` covers a revision arriving before the first summary autosave.

### 14.1 Drafts per field

**Every field the user edits is a draft** (§3.10), keyed by the ref and the field's path in the document (`entity_field`). A text field's draft holds its text; any other field's (a number, a switch, the prompt order, a new prompt, a list of tags) holds its JSON, with the path prefixed `=`; the literal `undefined` explicitly omits an optional field. So each field has its own undo history on the server, the order of 100 prompts included, and a reload or another device finds every unsaved change.
- **Paths name list elements by identity** where they have one (`["prompts", "identifier=main", "content"]`, as merge conflicts do, §3.9), so reordering never moves a draft to another prompt. Lists without identities (`alternate_greetings`) use indexes; an index into a list with ids (`modules`, `prompts`, `order`, `regex`, a lorebook's entries, or any list whose elements all have one) is refused, since after a reorder it would name another element.
- **Only the canonical spelling of a path is a field** (`parseField`): at most 8 segments, indexes whole and not negative, no `__proto__`, `constructor` or `prototype`. `SaveEditorDraft` refuses anything else (`EditRefused`). A value with such a key at any depth is refused when the drafts are applied.
- **The working document** (`applyDrafts`) is the head with the drafts applied: values first, shallowest first, then texts, so a text in a prompt a value draft added lands in it. In a list, `null` at an element removes it, and an identity no element has adds one at the end. The client builds it for the diff and the search; the writer builds it again to commit.
- **Protected fields** never change through a draft, each having its own action or none: `schema_version`; a card's `avatar_media_id` and `asset_media_ids`; a preset's `regex_approved`; a note's `owner`.
- A draft records the revision it started from (`baseRevisionId` when a session starts). Saving the same text against a new base now moves the base (`drafts.ts`), which is how the client keeps a draft after a conflict.

### 14.2 Saving

**`CommitEditorDrafts`** takes the ref's row version and the drafts to save, each with its row version, and in one writer transaction:
1. checks the ref and every draft are as the client saw them (`StaleWrite` otherwise: another tab typed on);
2. sets aside the drafts in something that's gone (a prompt or module deleted since, here or in what it follows): they end as discarded, their text going to the recovered texts, and the reply names them (`setAside`) so the client can say so. This comes first, so such a draft is never a conflict;
3. checks the others for conflicts: a draft whose base isn't the head conflicts where the saved version changed that field since its base, unless both now say the same. A base is read from the ref's entity, or from the card it was forked from for a draft moved with a fork (§3.9). Nothing is saved, and `EditConflict` names each field with the saved value and the draft's;
4. applies the other drafts and validates the result against the kind's schema (`validateDoc`), which would also drop keys the schema doesn't know. `InvalidDocument` lists the problems with their paths, at most 20. A draft the schema would drop (text at a key it doesn't know) is refused the same way, and stays: a save never ends a draft whose text it didn't keep (`droppedDrafts`);
5. writes the revision (origin `user`, with the user's message), unless nothing changed, and passes the change on to the refs that follow (§3.9);
6. ends the drafts as committed, sending text from before a destructive change to recovered texts (§3.10).

**Every revision is valid for its kind** (card, preset, note, lorebook; a preset overlay has no schema yet). Each write path checks what it was given and says why it refuses: commits, resolved updates and merges here, imports in the format worker (§11.2). `insertRevision` checks every revision but an import's again before storing it, and a document that fails is a defect, never a stored revision. Tests build their documents from `test/support/docs.ts` for that reason. A note is checked on every path, an import's too: the card importer's note (in `parsedFile`, as the writer will complete it), the writer's own (`insertRevision`), and the folder import's chat notes (`stImportWrites.ts`). A note's depth is at most ST's limit, 10,000 (`MAX_NOTE_DEPTH`): a card's Character's Note deeper than that is placed at the limit, and one that isn't a whole number from 0 at ST's default 4, each with a warning in the report (`noteDepth`).

Building and validating the document happens in the writer, because the drafts it applies are read in that same transaction; it's JSON and schema work, no I/O, as `EditRef`'s hashing already was. A 50 KB description and a 100-prompt preset save through it in the tests.

**Other writes,** each a revision on the head and passed on to followers:
- `RestoreRevision`: an earlier revision of the same entity as a new revision, `Restored the revision of <time> UTC`, `origin_ref` naming it. Nothing in between is lost (DATA-MODEL §15, invariant 1). Another schema version is refused.
- `SetRegexApproval`: below.
- `/library/avatar`: below.
- `ForkVariant` (the writer's `ForkWithDrafts`): a non-card variant following the ref, in one action, optionally moving the ref's unsaved drafts to it, so "make this a variant" works after the edits.
- `ForkCard` and `SetCardParent`: card forks, cards of their own (§3.9). The card editor’s Fork dialog calls `ForkCard` with display name, in-character name, version and the draft choice. `SetCardParent` remains API-only.
- `RenameRef`: the library label and subtitle (DESIGN §4: the in-chat name is the document's `name`, a field like any other). Labels are trimmed, 1 to 200 characters with no control characters.
- `CreateCard`: a card from scratch (`newCardDoc`), an upstream with every field empty but its name.
- Notes: `CreateCardNote` makes a note entity owned by the card (`newCardNote`: at the end of the tail as `system`, off in chats), `ListCardNotes` lists a card's notes by their upstream refs, and `DeleteNote` sends a card's or a chat's note, or a shared one, to the bin. A chat's own notes are made from the chat (§12). A note is edited like any ref. Both creates take an optional `link`, which must be a live shared note of the user's; the slot then takes its title.
- **Shared named notes** (DESIGN §4.1; `db/sharedNotes.ts`): note entities with `owner: { shared: "global" }` (global snippets) (`newSharedNote`), so they have revisions, drafts, the bin and the editor like any note, with no new kind or table. A slot (a card's or a chat's note) with `link` shows a global shared note's library head (scoped snippets resolve below); its own `content` is ignored while linked, and it keeps its own position, role, condition, scan switch and on/off state. `link` is protected from drafts (`PROTECTED` in `editing.ts`): it changes only by `CreateSharedNote { title, content, fromNoteRefId?, chatNote? }` (with `fromNoteRefId`, a new shared note from that slot's saved title and text, and the slot linked to it, in one transaction; a linked slot or a shared note is refused) and `UnlinkNote { noteRefId, chatNote? }` (the shared note's last revision, in the bin or not, as the slot's content, and no link). A chat's note at `chatNote` is written as `CommitEditorDrafts` writes it: a fork-local revision and its `note.binding` (`chatNoteEdit`). `ChatNotePosition` carries an optional `forkId` through editor reads, commits and shared-note actions; the Notes tab supplies its viewed fork and remounts its editor when that fork changes. `ListChatNotes` passes the same fork to `ChatNoteEntries` and `noteAt`, so two forks sharing a tip resolve separate note revisions even while another fork is current. `ListSharedNotes { query?, refId?, entityId? }` lists the live shared notes, filtered by title (case-folded in JavaScript, since SQLite's `LIKE` folds ASCII only), each with an excerpt and how many slots link to it (a slot counts once: its library head links, or a chat bound a revision of it that does).
- **Snippets** (DESIGN §3.1, §4.1; UX-REWORK's 2026-10-27 confirmation; `@rp/shared/snippetsApi.ts`, `db/snippets.ts`, `db/snippetWrites.ts`, `features/snippets/`): the same shared note entities, refs, revisions and bin, with no second text store. `CreateSnippet` creates a shared note owned by the viewed chat, or global outside a chat. `ListSnippets` searches titles and contents; existing global shared notes are already snippets. `SaveSnippet` checks the exact revision, keeps the text's continuously active draft (a `snippet` draft key of the ref and fork, empty fork for globals, with the usual patch history), and materializes a revision in the same writer transaction. Global text moves its upstream head and reaches linked slots; scoped text writes a `snippet_revision` fork override. Only forks with a visible binding list a scoped snippet. The overlay's frozen lineage bounds and cap compaction carry it into future forks from any point, keeping existing forks independent. `MakeSnippetGlobal` makes the viewed revision the global head in one transaction; this explicit scope change exposes that text everywhere. Writes, reads, scopes and bindings check ownership; the baseline's snippet-binding triggers check entity, revision and chat ownership. Purging a revision collects its snippet bindings, so later lineage compaction never copies a reference to purged text.
- The **Snippets panel** is a registered `chatPanels` contribution, search and a selectable list above the code-editor box, a name, Insert and Make global. Text saves continuously; no Save button. The workspace outlives its tab, serializes saves with their acknowledged revision, keeps in-flight edits, and leaves conflicting text editable with a Save as new snippet recovery action. Browser copies are per account/ref/fork, removed only after acknowledgment; recovered copies are labelled. Same-origin library invalidations update other open tabs, and focus refreshes the list. A registered app tool supplies a sheet outside chats; a registered chat surface provider supplies the viewed chat/fork and opens its right-panel tab (a sheet on phones). Beside the selected snippet's Insert and Make global, a registered `snippets.tools` slot (`features/snippets/slots.ts`) holds per-snippet actions. `snippets.history` adds **Undo** and **Earlier text**, backed by the snippet draft's server history, so they work after a reload: `SnippetHistory` (reader) lists the draft's states newest first; `RestoreSnippet` (writer, one transaction) checks the expected revision, records the chosen state as a restore in the draft's history (so the replaced text stays listed) and materializes it as a revision exactly as `SaveSnippet` does: the global head, or the viewed fork's `snippet_revision` override. Undo restores the state before the current one; pending typing is saved first. A new fork's scoped snippet starts its own draft seeded with the resolved text before the first edit, so that edit can be undone without sharing its parent's history. Existing shared notes get the same initial text before their first snippet edit. Making a scoped snippet global carries the viewed draft history with it. If the library editor or revision restore changed the shared text, history shows that revision as the current state, and the next snippet edit or restore first retains it as a separate draft state. Restore keeps any text or name typed while the request is in flight, saving it on the acknowledged restored revision. `test/snippets.test.ts` covers listing, the stale-revision refusal and restore; `e2e/snippetRecovery.spec.ts` covers Undo and Earlier text after reloads at desktop and phone widths in both themes. `snippets.delete` adds **Delete**: pending text is saved, and a failed or still-pending save prevents deletion and preserves the browser copy; fields stay read-only while deletion runs so typing cannot race the bin write; then the existing `DeleteNote` moves the note to the bin, and the usual deleted toast (`useDeletedToast`, whose optional `onRestored` lets the workspace read its list again after Undo) offers Undo and the bin. Deleting asks first only when other notes show the snippet (`ListSharedNotes`' `usedBy`, with the viewed chat for a scoped one), saying how many and that they send nothing until it's restored or they're unlinked; an unused snippet goes straight to the bin, like other notes. The same spec covers both paths.
- **Text transfers** use the generic `textActions` registry and its registered application host (`textActions.tsx`, `appTools`); plugins can override/disable Send, Insert and Open in Snippets. Selecting text in any ordinary text field, code editor or rendered message/swipe offers **Send to snippet** on the context menu or Ctrl/Command+Shift+S: append to the open snippet, or create one. **Insert** uses the snippet's selected part, or its whole text, at the last field's cursor, composer included. Inputs use their native selection and input events; the UI code editor registers a target that dispatches a normal undoable CodeMirror transaction, including across its shadow root. Snippet controls never replace the remembered destination. Password and secret controls are excluded. Note pickers in a chat include the scoped snippets visible in its viewed fork; linked text and assembly resolve the same binding, and a snippet scoped to another chat sends nothing. Linked chat notes open the corresponding snippet through an action contribution; a scoped snippet's library page directs editing to its chat panel. A Send operation completes its create or append-and-save before the next queued transfer or scope change, so rapid sends share one new snippet and remain in the originating scope. Unlink resolves the shared text in the viewed fork before copying it into the slot.

- Updates from what a ref follows (§3.9): `PreviewUpdate`, `ApplyUpdate` (when no help is needed) and `ResolveUpdate`. A resolved document comes from the client, so the writer (`ResolveMerge`) takes it only while an update is pending, checks it against the kind's schema, refuses media and cards that aren't the user's as `NotFound` (`avatar_media_id`, `asset_media_ids`, a note's `owner.card`), takes each protected field from one side or the other (`protectedChanges`), and grants nothing (§14.3).
- **An automatic merge never makes an invalid document** (`planMerge` in `db/library.ts`). Two valid edits can merge into an invalid one: a variant deletes a prompt the upstream then adds to its order. The fields the schema refuses become conflicts, the ref stays as it was with "update available", and the resolver shows them. This costs a schema check of the merged document per merge, in the writer.

**Reads:** `OpenEditor` returns, in one snapshot, the ref, its head document, how many chats use it, every draft of its fields (ended ones too: their row versions are what the next save starts from) and, for non-cards, the entity's upstream and variants (empty for cards). `ListRevisions` and `GetRevision` (of the ref's own entity, and for a fork the revisions of the card it was forked from, which a moved draft may have started from) are the history. `CardFamily` is a card's family tree (§3.9). `EditorDraftHistory`, `RestoreEditorDraft` and `DiscardEditorDraft` are §3.10's operations, for `entity_field` drafts only.

**"Used by N chats"** (DESIGN §4): the user's live chats a change to the ref reaches. That's those whose state names it (`card.binding`, `preset.binding`, `lorebook.attach`), those with a chat override following it, and, for an entity's upstream, those its card joined (`participant.join`), which is what chats use without a binding. A chat counts even if the card left it since: too many is safer than too few. One query (`usedByChats` in `db/library.ts`) serves the editor and the Cast tab.

### 14.3 What changes never grant

A preset's scripts run only once `regex_approved` is true (§9), and only `SetRegexApproval` changes it, with its own revision ("Allowed the regex scripts to run"). The user's own edits in the editor keep it, a script's included. Anything else keeps it only for the scripts it was given for (`keepsRegexApproval`, comparing them as canonical JSON): a merged import or variant change that brings different scripts (`applyMerge`, `ResolveMerge`), and a restored revision, come out not approved. Without that, a re-import of a file with a new script merged into an approved preset arrived approved, since a merge keeps the side that changed the flag. A fast-forward is no exception: a ref without edits of its own would move to exactly the revision it follows, approval included, so where that revision has an approval or permission the ref doesn't, the ref gets a merge revision with it kept off instead (`applyMerge`).

The agent's permission to edit a note (`agent_editable`) works the same way (`withoutGrantedAgentEdits`): the user turns it on in the editor, and a merge, a resolved update or a restored revision keeps it off wherever the head has it off.

**What a note names is the user's own** (`checkReferences` in `libraryWrites.ts`), checked on every write path that takes a note document (`CreateCardNote`, `CreateChatNote`, `CommitEditorDrafts`, `ResolveMerge`): its owner card and its condition's card must be cards of the user's, and its `link` a shared note of the user's (a shared note itself links to nothing); anything else is `NotFound`. The isolation matrix probes each.

### 14.4 Documents

- **Notes** (`@rp/shared/docs/note.ts`, schema version 1; every key below is optional, so older revisions decode) have a placement: in-chat at a depth with a role; at the tail with a role; in a section with an optional role (system when absent), the start of the context, the start or end of the tail, before or after the main prompt, the card's own (`card`, with its definition) or the top or bottom of the author's note (§13); or none, read only by a macro. An owner: a card, a chat or `{ shared: "global" }` or `{ shared: chatId }` (a global or chat-scoped shared named note/snippet). `condition`: `{ when: "always" }`, `{ when: "turn", card }` or `{ when: "not_muted", card }`, absent meaning always. `scan_lore` (absent off, ST's default), `on_by_default` (a card's note on in every chat with the card unless switched off there), `link` (the shared note a slot shows), `module_id` (the card module or exported note it came from). `interval`, ST's frequency, is still decoded for older imports, never written and ignored (DESIGN §4.1: no insertion frequency). Export writes ST's Character's Note from a depth note, and from a tail note at depth 0, never from the others, which ST's note has no place for. The fields the editor doesn't edit (a chat note's `kind`, `template`, `covers`) stay as they are: a draft changes only its own path, and the schema keeps them.
- **`agent_editable`** on notes (DESIGN §4.1a, proposed) is only stored: nothing reads it until agents exist. Import never reads it and export never writes it, as with `regex_approved`: a file never grants anything (DESIGN §11). Card fields don't have the flag yet.

### 14.5 Avatars and images

- **`POST /library/avatar?ref=<card ref>&rowVersion=<n>`** (`importer/routes.ts`): the image as the body, `application/octet-stream`, at most 16 MiB, with the import's limits on slow bodies, its permits and one transfer per user (§11.4). The ref is checked before the body is read. The type comes from the bytes (`importer/images.ts`, the same classifier the importers use, a PNG needing its whole signature): PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP, else `415`. The file goes to media storage as an import's do (§11.1), held from the sweep until `SetCardAvatar` records it, and the card gets a revision, "Changed the avatar". A stale row version is `409`.
- **`POST /library/drafts`**: editor drafts a closing page sends with `fetch(…, { keepalive: true })`, JSON, at most 500 and 4 MiB; each saved as `SaveEditorDraft` would, naming the draft it was typed into, the writer deciding a conflict with another tab's save (§3.10). Another user's ref is `404`. A chat's composer goes in `composers`, saved as `SaveComposer` would (§12); a summary's edit in `summaries` (`SummaryDraftSave`), saved as `SaveSummaryDraft` would, and a message editor's text in `messages` (`MessageDraftSave`), saved as `SaveMessageDraft` would, both of which Browser recovery's Retry sends.
- **`GET /library/media?id=<media id>`**: a stored file the user holds a reference to (`GetMediaFile`), only if it's one of those four image types, with `nosniff`. Anything else, another user's file included, is `404`. The same bytes uploaded by two users are one file both reference, so both can read it. Originals and thumbnails now use `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable` and a strong ETag; `If-None-Match` (including weak validators and lists) returns an empty `304` after authentication, ownership and file-existence checks. **Privacy change:** the old `private, no-store` was a privacy default. The browser's own disk cache now holds image bytes, including across logout; shared caches still may not store them. Image concealment still applies to cached images. Media bytes are immutable: `recordMedia` inserts by SHA-256 without updating an existing asset, and `SetCardAvatar` records changed bytes as a new media id before revising the card.

### 14.5a Portrait display and images in public

`ui/Avatar.tsx` crops only the displayed image with `object-cover`; every stock avatar requests a thumbnail at its measured CSS width × `devicePixelRatio`, rounded up to 96, 192, 384 or 768 pixels (`ui/mediaImage.ts`, shared snapping in `@rp/shared/media.ts`). ResizeObserver covers tiles, group collage cells, header faces, composer, message portraits, Cast, card pages and editor previews, including resized panels; window resize also rechecks display density. No guessed initial URL downloads an unnecessary smaller image. Avatar images use `loading="lazy"` and `decoding="async"`. All stock avatars use one rectangular renderer (`ui/Avatar.tsx`), with 2:3 proportions at each fixed size; all cropped portraits share `ui/Portrait.tsx`'s 2:3 frame through `Avatar`, including home tiles, chat rows, individual header cast faces, speaker pickers, cast chips, group faces and reply-from. Chat messages default to ST 1.19's rectangular size: 60 × 90 px, 10 px corners (50 px base, width factor 1.2, height factor 1.8, corner factor 5). Image settings offer message portrait sizes 48 × 72, 60 × 90, 72 × 108 and 84 × 126, saved per account through display preferences. The old browser avatar-style choice (`rp.avatarStyle`) is no longer read. Full-image viewing retains the image's natural aspect ratio. Header faces, cast, composer and library tiles keep their own compact sizes. A portrait's initials use the same box when it has no image or images are hidden.

Message portraits and Cast portraits open the **Portrait** tab beside the chat, or the full-screen chat sheet on phones. `ui/Portrait.tsx` renders an aspect-preserving thumbnail with `object-contain`, sized to the measured panel or phone sheet, its card's name, and a button (or a click on the image) for a full-size, scrollable sheet. Only that full-size sheet requests the original URL without `w`; both images decode asynchronously. Personas use the same mechanism. The URL carries `panel=portrait&portrait=<entity id>` so refresh and shared links restore the selection; a tab opened without a selection explains where to click. Other tabs stay mounted while switching. Full-size opening is disabled while images are concealed; an already open full-size image follows the global CSS too.

`ui/imagePrefs.ts` applies `data-images` to the root **before React renders**. Show, Blur and Hide are stored per browser (`rp.images`, `rp.images.conceal`), shared across accounts on that device, and follow other tabs' storage events. The app shell and sheets have one-click toggles (Show ↔ last chosen concealment, initially Blur), plus image settings for all three modes and account-owned portrait size. **Alt+Shift+B** works throughout the app, text fields included; repeating the key does not toggle repeatedly. Storage being unavailable leaves the choices in memory. Blurred images stay blurred on hover/tap. Root CSS covers img, video, canvas, picture and SVG image; hidden stock surfaces show neutral initials. `imageSurfaces.test.ts` rejects URL backgrounds and raw image surfaces outside the stock avatar/viewer. The editor uses the stock avatar too. Markdown image URLs remain blocked (§5); import screens currently render no images. PNG card import removes metadata card chunks but preserves image chunks/pixels/dimensions; persona and avatar uploads preserve their bytes. Serving an original reads stored bytes without resizing; thumbnail requests use the format workers (§14.5b).

**Enter sends** (`DisplayPrefs.enterSends`, `composerKeys.ts`). Settings → Display and the chat’s Display panel offer Automatic (default: desktop Enter sends, coarse-pointer Enter adds a line), Always and Never (Enter adds a line; Ctrl/Cmd+Enter sends). Both saved and pending composers share the predicate and show the active submit/newline keys in their hints. Submission waits for account preferences to load, including while editing a reply’s guidance. Alt+Enter remains reserved for Continue, and composition never submits. Existing `SetDisplayPrefs`/`GetDisplayPrefs` worker operations carry the field: the writer merges a patch into the account’s display row in one transaction. Older rows read Automatic without resetting other choices.

**Account viewer preferences.** `ui/viewPrefs.ts` shares `GetDisplayPrefs` queries partitioned by account. `DisplayPrefs` includes streamed reasoning (`expanded` by default), message portrait size (`default`), timeline mode (`originals`), coverage rail (`false`), the composer's guidance toggle (`false`, §12), display macro/regex switches (`false`) and the four nullable message-colour overrides (above). `SetDisplayPrefs` accepts a patch; `db/displayPrefs.ts` merges it with the stored value in one synchronous writer transaction before upserting `user_prefs`, key `display`. Existing reasoning-only rows gain the new defaults when read. Independent controls and concurrent patches retain the other choices. Saves invalidate the shared query; errors remain visible on the control. Portrait size and summary mode follow the account across reloads, chats and browsers. Image concealment remains device-local and is applied before render.

**Desktop layout sizes.** `ui/ResizeHandle.tsx` is shared by the left chat-list sidebar and the right chat panel. A visible edge grip supports pointer capture (cancel/lost capture ends a drag), arrow keys (10 px, Shift 40 px), double-click and Home to reset. Defaults: left 320 px, right 416 px. Left bounds: 220–420 px, reserving 700 px for the remaining layout; right bounds: 260–640 px, reserving 426 px for the chat. A ResizeObserver clamps display width when available space shrinks, without replacing the remembered wider choice. Keys `rp.panelWidth.<user id>.sidebar/chat` partition widths per user in this browser, alongside the existing remembered panel tab. Phones keep sheets with no resize handles. The panel tabs wrap onto another row when needed so every full label stays visible. Sheets focus Close on opening; image-control tooltips sit below their triggers and all stock tooltips ignore pointer events so they cannot block controls. Browser tests cover original dimensions, all portrait entry points, size steps and rendered aspect ratios, concealment/no exposed reload frames, cross-tab changes, resizing, reload, reset and both viewport modes (`e2e/portraits.spec.ts`). `e2e/portraitLayout.spec.ts` imports the realistic 380-message group and checks sheet dismissal after privacy changes, full tab labels at both widths, and composer input/action bounds at maximum sidebar widths and after reload.

### 14.5b Thumbnail workers and measured image loading

`GET /library/media?id=<media id>&w=<positive pixel width>` rounds up to **96, 192, 384, 768**, capped at 768; invalid widths return `400`. The existing format pool (§11.3, `FORMAT_WORKERS`, default 1) runs `Thumbnail` alongside its other jobs, one job per worker, with a 30-second execution deadline. Native sharp 0.35.5 uses one libvips thread per worker and no process image cache; decoding is limited to 40 million pixels. The main thread performs only authentication, owner-scoped reader RPCs and asynchronous disk I/O. A map of pending cached Effects coalesces simultaneous requests for each immutable file/width. Already admitted native work continues if an HTTP caller disconnects, so its replacement waits for the same job.

`importer/thumbnail.ts` auto-orients, keeps aspect ratio, never upscales, and encodes WebP quality 80 into `media/<prefix>/<sha256>.thumb-v1-<width>.webp`, next to the original. A private temporary file is atomically renamed into place; later jobs and server restarts reuse it. The derivative version is part of the path and ETag and must change if encoding rules change. Header-only metadata inspection has no pixel limit; actual decoding retains the 40-million-pixel limit. Images above that bound, or whose resize exceeds sharp's 20-second timeout, serve their original bytes, MIME type and ETag even on sized URLs. A private `.original-v1` marker remembers this fallback across widths and restarts, avoiding repeated doomed work. The media sweep keeps derivatives and fallback markers while their original is recorded, and sweeps unreferenced derivatives and interrupted temporary files. Corrupt or missing files return `404` without taking down the worker.

**Animation policy:** animated GIF/WebP and APNG requests serve the original bytes and original MIME type, preserving animation rather than flattening it. GIF/WebP detection uses page metadata; PNG chunk boundaries are checked for APNG's `acTL`. The `.original-v1` fallback marker avoids repeating metadata work; existing `.animated-v1` markers are also honored. Animation therefore retains its original download cost.

**Native deployment:** sharp stays external to the formats bundle. Its pinned npm optional dependencies include linux-x64 glibc and darwin-arm64 sharp/libvips packages in the lockfile. Docker's production `npm ci --omit=dev` and final `node_modules` copy include the native binaries; building needs only npm packages, with no system libvips or separate download. The production image was built and an offline container generated a 192×96 WebP through the actual bundled format worker. Tests that put bundles outside the repository link their external sharp dependency into the temporary bundle directory.

**Measurement (2026-10-15):** Chromium/Playwright, 1440×900 and 390×844 at DPR 1, built client, real HTTP server and worker storage on port 5995. `test/support/stLargeImages.ts` adds 150 deterministic uncompressed RGB PNG cards (100 at about 3 MiB, 50 at about 6 MiB) to `writeRealisticStFolder`'s 12 cards, 3 personas and long group chats. Pixels are generated; no private or downloaded images are used. There are 151 distinct original image files after deduplication. Browser caches start empty for each cold run; the first post-change desktop run also has no disk thumbnails. Warm means a literal `page.reload` in the same browser context, with the scroll position reset before timing. The measurement scrolls every viewport and waits until every image has loaded, so totals include the entire library, not just images near the fold. Scroll pacing (100 ms per step, 13 desktop / 33 phone steps), UI loading, waits and the final 200 ms settle are included in elapsed time. CDP `encodedDataLength` counts transferred image bytes including HTTP headers; zero-byte disk/memory cache reads are excluded from network request counts.

| Viewport / run | Before image bytes | After image bytes | Before / after network requests | Before / after all-images time |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 1440×900 cold, unthrottled | 629,525,221 | 659,538 | 151 / 153 | 4.273 s / 5.546 s |
| 1440×900 warm, unthrottled | 629,525,221 | 0 | 151 / 0 | 3.642 s / 2.662 s |
| 390×844 cold, unthrottled | 629,525,221 | 657,535 | 151 / 152 | 5.322 s / 4.944 s |
| 390×844 warm, unthrottled | 629,525,221 | 0 | 151 / 0 | 5.558 s / 4.839 s |

The first visible viewport finished in 1.372 / 1.243 s before/after on desktop, and 1.166 / 0.905 s on phone. At that point the browser had initiated 46 / 48 desktop image requests and 12 / 13 phone image requests; only nearby tiles were requested. The extra cold derivative requests are shared originals at different rendered sizes (including the desktop sidebar), not duplicate generation. Entire-library cold bytes fall about **99.9%**. Unthrottled loopback can transfer hundreds of MB very quickly, so cold native generation is slower than that baseline on desktop; a literal warm reload transfers no image bytes. Browser image events still exist on warm reload (153 desktop / 152 phone) but cause no HTTP image transfers.

The Fast 4G-like CDP run uses 150 ms latency, 9 Mibit/s download and 1.5 Mibit/s upload. Browser caches are empty for cold loads; existing disk thumbnails are reused. Literal warm reloads stay in the same context. This includes throttled application JavaScript and the same library traversal, not only image transfer.

| Viewport / run | Image bytes | HTTP image requests | All images loaded | First visible viewport |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 1440×900 cold | 659,538 | 153 | 6.550 s | 2.490 s |
| 1440×900 warm | 0 | 0 | 3.143 s | 1.306 s |
| 390×844 cold | 657,535 | 152 | 7.091 s | 2.480 s |
| 390×844 warm | 0 | 0 | 5.303 s | 1.254 s |

An additional original-build throttled whole-library traversal was stopped after more than 12 minutes without completing; it is not used as a completed timing or byte measurement, and its cause was not diagnosed. The completed unthrottled before/after runs and completed changed-build throttled runs are the reported comparisons.

Server tests cover snapping, WebP, ratio, no upscale, originals, ETags/304, ownership/authentication, corrupt pixels, animated GIF/WebP/APNG, concurrent cold requests and disk reuse (`test/thumbnails.test.ts`), alongside media sweep and format worker tests. `e2e/images.spec.ts` imports the large fixture and checks lazy thumbnail requests and rendered-width × DPR 2 at both sizes, then opens the original 1024×2048 portrait. Existing portrait and privacy/layout browser tests also pass with the thumbnail preview.

### 14.6 The editors in the client

`packages/client/src/features/editor/`, at `/library/$refId` (loaded with the first entry opened), on stock components. The library lists **Edit** on cards and presets and has **New card**.
- **The page says which state you're in** (DESIGN §6.2), in a header without boxes: the name (a kind badge and variant/chat badge for kinds other than cards), the card's provenance, its version, and one muted line on what saving reaches ("Used by 3 chats: saving changes it in all of them. To change it in some only, fork it.", "make a variant" for other kinds; a chat's own version names its chat with a link back and says the original's later changes still reach it). Under it a sticky bar: Save, Search, History and **More** (a popover kept mounted so the dialogs its actions open stay open: the card's `actions` contributions, Fork, Forks, Compare, Mark as a fork of…, Unlink; Make a variant and Rename for lorebooks and notes; presets keep Make a variant and Rename in the bar), with "Everything is saved", "Keeping your changes…" or "N fields have changes, kept as drafts until you save" at its end. The card family section shows only for a card with forks. An update from what the entry follows shows as a banner.
- **The store** (`store.ts`) holds the snapshot and a draft per field: typing changes the field at once and saves it 600 ms after the last key, one save per field at a time, naming the draft's id, row version and session as last seen; if another tab saved the field meanwhile, or another draft is at the field now (the one named moved to a variant), the server puts this on top and keeps theirs (§3.10). A field's base changes only with its text: reading the drafts again (after a commit, say) leaves a field with unsaved text on the base it was typed on. Every store on the page guards itself (`useLeaveGuard`), the card's notes included: leaving the editor saves what's left, closing or reloading the tab with a change not yet on the server asks first (`beforeunload`), and the page sends those changes as it goes (`pagehide`, to `/library/drafts`, with `keepalive`). A send counts only once the server answers, so a failed one goes again the next time. Browsers cap such a request at 64 KB, and a larger one isn't sent. Every change also leaves a pending-save record in this browser (`backup.ts`, IndexedDB), shared with composers: an independent id, target and field, original draft id, row version, session, revision base and request payload. A tab may replace only its own copy of the field that has not been sent; requests already out keep separate copies, and one that fails goes only if a newer copy typed from it replaces it. Acknowledgement deletes exactly that id. Opening the entry, a note included, replays all its copies with their original bases, so the writer preserves displaced text, and shows a notice. A displaced copy also leaves a conflict in that field's client state, shown beside its controls with both times, a preview, and an undoable switch through the same explicit restore as the composer. An entry that can't be opened (in the bin, say) keeps its copies until it can. A stale active field keeps its revision base across a commit; committing it goes through the conflict picker.
  - **Browser retention.** Copies are plain text in the browser profile, partitioned by user, and only that account's copies are exposed. Unacknowledged copies have no automatic age or capacity expiry. Retry and logout flushes accumulate `x-drafts-recovered`; recovery retries retain the explanation and recovered-text link through further retries and reopening the dialog. Logout retains this notice if it fails, and carries it in the login URL when it succeeds, including after a retry or reload. The login notice directs the user back to the same account; it contains no recovered text or account identity. Logout flushes each exact pending save before ending the session; failed saves keep the session open and offer Browser recovery (inspect/copy out, retry saving, or confirmed deletion of a selected copy). Another account's login leaves earlier copies untouched. Browser recovery remains accessible from the header for expired sessions, missing targets and other failed saves. Every record requires the complete original payload; intermediate plaintext editor and JSON-in-content composer formats are not supported. Removing a module, prompt or script discards its descendant drafts, and Revert or the conflict picker's take-theirs response carries recovery metadata so the editor keeps its source link to recovered texts.
- **Components read the store only through hooks over `useSyncExternalStore`** (`useText`, `useValue`, `useDirty`, `useEditorState`), each subscribed to its own field's channel. Typing in a 50 KB description re-renders that field, and the page only when a field starts or stops having changes. A component that read the store directly showed stale values: the React Compiler memoizes what a stable object's methods return. For the same reason no closure in render uses `x!.y` on state that can be null: the compiler reads a closure's dependencies while rendering.
- **Each text field** has Undo (one step back in its server history), History (every state of the session, restorable) and Revert (the draft discarded to recovered texts) while it has changes. Each is made only over the text the field showed when it was picked, with nothing unsaved; if the field changed before it could go, it isn't. Text typed while it's on its way stays, saved on top. Numbers keep what's typed until it's a number. Alternate greetings and tags are each one value draft.
- **Saving** flushes everything, shows each changed field (`changedFields`: objects key by key, lists with identities element by element, else whole). All library editors use read-only CodeMirror merge views: side by side at desktop widths, unified below 640 px, with the complete text scrollable. Each field compares with the immutable revision its draft was typed on, including a stale draft after another tab saves; revision documents are cached separately from draft state. Saving takes an optional message and commits, naming each draft by its id and the row version saved, so the revision holds exactly the text shown; nothing is committed while a change isn't on the server. An `EditConflict` turns the dialog into the per-field picker: keep mine, take the saved version, or edit, then save again. Text the commit sent to the recovered texts (from before a large deletion, or another tab's text a save went over) is counted in the reply (`recovered`). The store adds up every save's count and, where the save names its field, the field (`recoveredFields`); `RecoveredNotice` then says, plainly, "What Personality had before you replaced most of it is kept in the bin, under Recovered text, in case you want it back" (or, from a revision's save, which doesn't name fields, "What you replaced or deleted…"), with **Open the bin** and **Dismiss** (`dismissRecovered`). A large deletion is measured without line endings (`isDestructive` in `@rp/shared/drafts.ts`): a text box turns an imported file's CRLF into LF at the first key typed, which used to count as deleting the whole field and showed this notice for an ordinary edit.
- **History** lists revisions with their origin and message and restores as a new revision. For every library kind, any revision (including the head) can be selected and compared with its first parent (empty document for the initial revision) or with the current working draft/head. Only differing fields are listed, each collapsible, using the same responsive read-only merge views. Earlier revisions load in pages of 50 through an owner/entity-scoped `before` cursor on `ListRevisions`/the reader worker's `RefHistory`.
- **Fork** creates an independent card with three names and optionally takes its unsaved drafts (§3.9). Cards have no variant navigation or variant badge. For other kinds, **Make a variant** names it and takes the unsaved changes along by default (`moveDrafts`), then opens it. Variants of the entry are listed as links.
- **The update resolver** previews the merge: without conflicts it's one click; with them, each conflict shows both sides (and how each changed the base) with keep mine, take theirs (the default is mine) or edit, and the resolved document goes to `ResolveUpdate`.
- **The card editor**: avatar (upload; shown through `/library/media`), Display name (`name`), Name the model sees (`nickname`, with the empty fallback explained), Version (shown as subtitle, `character_version`), the definition fields, greetings, the card's own prompts, notes (collapsed rows as in a chat, each opening its own store and Save; "Add note" makes a new one or picks a shared note; created and deleted to the bin), creator's notes, tags and creator. The note editor (`notes.tsx`, which a note's own page and a chat's notes use too): name; content in the code-editor box, or for a linked slot a banner naming the shared note and how many places an edit changes, with "Open shared note" (its own page, which says how many notes show it) and "Unlink" (or, while it's in the bin, "Restore" and "Unlink"); position, offering the five decided places (start of context, with the definitions, at a depth, start of the tail, end of the tail) with depth and role, and an older or imported place only on a note already there; when it applies (always, on a character's turn, while a character isn't muted, the characters being the chat's cast, or in the card editor the card itself), with a one-line warning when a conditioned note sits above the history; "Lorebook entries can trigger on this note"; for a card's note "On in every chat" and "Private (not exported)"; the agent switch; and in its menu "Save as shared note" and "Delete the note". Saving as or unlinking from a shared note asks for unsaved changes to be saved first, then reopens the editor.
- **Embedded card lorebooks** (U19; `EmbeddedLorebook.tsx`) are a registered `card.editor.sections` contribution (`card.embedded-lorebook`), rendered by the card editor through `SlotOutlet`. A plugin can replace, disable or add sections with the same store. Cards with no book offer Add lorebook entry, creating a valid embedded document. Entries are collapsible rows; only open rows mount their forms, with the first entry and a newly added entry open initially. Book name, description, scan depth, budget and recursive scanning, and entry title, enabled/constant flags, primary and secondary keys, selective logic, case/whole-word matching, content, insertion order, position, depth, role, outlet, probability, entry scan depth, group selection, sticky/cooldown/delay turns, recursion exclusions and budget bypass use the existing card field drafts and revision save. Keys are one per line, so regex keys containing commas survive. Entries use `uid=N` paths; additions have fresh random integer identities and removal uses `EditorStore.removeItem`, recovering drafts inside the deleted entry. Imported extension data and fields outside these controls stay untouched. Undo, revert and rebase of a structural draft recover descendant drafts whose parent disappears, keeping those that still fit; the first Undo restores the field from its own base revision through draft history, including an absent value, so it remains undoable. `test/protocol/draft-regressions.test.ts` and the browser tests cover this recovery and later card saves. Draft reload, field revert, revision restore and card export use the existing mechanisms; no separate lorebook write API or storage path was added. `e2e/embeddedLorebook.spec.ts` covers imported metadata preservation, drafts after reload, revert, deleting an entry with a text draft, adding entries and authoring an absent book, saving an empty book, earlier revision restore and V2 export, at desktop and phone widths in both themes.
- **Standalone lorebooks** use the same form (`LorebookForm.tsx`, shared with the embedded section, rooted at `character_book` there and at the document here). The library editor renders a lorebook through the registered `lorebook.editor.sections` slot (`cardSections.ts`): `lorebook.use` (the "Use in every chat" switch) and `lorebook.entries` (book settings and entries); a plugin can replace, disable or add sections with the same store. Search and replace reaches every entry's title, text, outlet, group and keys through the registered `editorTextFields` contribution `lorebook.entries` (kind `lorebook`). Closed entries show their keys (or "Always included"), "Off" when disabled, and the first two lines of their current text, drafts included; a book with more than one entry has a "Filter entries" box matching titles, keys and text. Edits are the lorebook's own field drafts, saved with Save as a revision; History, variants, rename, update from file and export are the editor's existing ones. No lorebook-specific write operation was added: the generic editor operations validate the edited document as a `LorebookDoc`. `e2e/standaloneLorebook.spec.ts` covers listing, filtering, drafts after reload, save, removing and adding entries, search and replace, export and earlier revision restore at desktop and phone widths in both themes.
- **Preset warnings** render above the prompt document through `preset.editor.warnings` (`presetSections.ts`), with built-in contribution `preset.warnings` registered by the editor feature. Plugins can replace, disable or add warnings with the same draft store and a callback that opens and scrolls to a prompt. The mounted editor host is covered by `endOfRequest.test.ts`.
- **The preset editor** (`PromptDocument.tsx`) is one scrolling document, with every prompt in the order, including off prompts. Each block shows its name, role, position/depth, switch and estimated tokens; its full prompt uses CodeMirror with line markers and no inner height limit. Blocks collapse individually or together. Handles and the desktop outline names reorder by pointer or arrow keys; clicking an outline name jumps to and expands that block. Insert buttons before and between blocks add prompts in place (a card can't replace a prompt added here: `forbid_overrides` on); while the order has no Definitions marker, each offers it too (DESIGN §5.0). There is no separate pool. Slim marker rows show the current chat's estimated content and definition names ("Definitions: Aria, Rook"), or “no chat open” in the library; with the Definitions marker on, the per-field markers say "Filled by Definitions". The Main Prompt and Post-History Instructions blocks say what a card's own prompt does there in the open chat, from `PresetContext.overrides` ("Replaced in this chat by Aria's system prompt" with a link to the card, "Aria has a system prompt; this prompt forbids it", or "A card can't replace it"). The card editor's System prompt and Post-history instructions fields say whether their text is used (`PromptUseNote.tsx`, `GetCardPromptUse`): "Used in place of ⟨preset⟩'s Main Prompt", "Not used: ⟨preset⟩'s Main Prompt can't be replaced", "… is off", or "⟨preset⟩ has no Main Prompt". A lorebook's page has its "Use in every chat" switch (`GlobalLorebook.tsx`). History, tail, note slots and computed cache boundaries appear in the document. Then prefill, utility prompts and regex approval retain their controls; samplers and formatting are model settings (**Model settings**, `@rp/shared/modelSettings.ts`).
- **Preset overlays** (`PresetWorkspace.tsx`, `ui/ColumnOverlay.tsx`) open over the chat's centre column, leaving the mounted chat, its scroll and composer intact. The sticky toolbar keeps the preset’s name, Close preset editor and save status visible after scrolling or jumping to a prompt; closing uses the existing workspace close path and leaves drafts protected. `e2e/presetOverlay.spec.ts` checks the scrolled close control and identity at 1440 and 390 in both themes. The nonmodal Base UI dialog portals into its own column; other panels remain usable. On phones opening it closes the panel sheet. The library's Edit button opens the same editor over the library; direct `/library/$refId` URLs remain supported. A card opens in the same workspace from its chat tab (Full editor), with "Card editor" as the overlay's name and **Back to the chat** in its sticky bar: the chat, its fork and scroll stay mounted under it, and the URL never changes. A workspace shares its `EditorStore` between the overlay and the right panel's Prompt preset tab. That tab has prompt switches, estimates and Edit on each block (opening the overlay at that block), and Save/Discard for the global preset's drafts; Discard asks first when drafts other than the switches' would go too: prompt text edited in the overlay, say, or blocks it reordered (a switch flip keeps the saved order's sequence). Save compares every changed prompt with its draft's base revision using the rich diff; revision browsing uses the existing rich browser.
- **Current-chat annotations** travel in `NextContext.presetContext`, including the Cache event and the client's session projection. The reader transaction projects sent root pieces, their descendant card names, active notes (including empty ones, marking an interval-skipped note as not due) and the request's existing breakpoints (`db/presetContext.ts`); it sends no prompt text. Counts are estimates of expanded piece text, excluding provider framing. Failed assembly yields unavailable context, not a fabricated fill. Draft edits show their own text estimates; marker annotations describe the saved preset's current assembly until a revision is saved.
- **Search and replace** (`SearchDialog.tsx`, `searchCore.ts`) covers every text field of the working document, with case, Unicode whole-word, regex captures and preserve case (upper, lower or title case) options and before → after previews. Patterns run in Unicode mode when they can; one that only a plain regular expression accepts (`\'`, `\-`) runs without it, with ASCII word boundaries for whole words. Presets keep it in the sticky toolbar; on a phone its options fold behind an Options button, the match list is shorter and the editor actions use compact spacing to avoid a spare Rename row, so the bar leaves most of the screen to the document (`e2e/unitGPreset.spec.ts` retains the 400 px toolbar limit at 390 px). Other editors use the dialog. Every match has a clickable field label and a Go to action. The shared dialog calls its host’s reveal callback with the field path and match range; the editor supplies navigation through `searchNavigation.ts`. Prompt and regex sections expand the matched field, and card text, alternate greetings and embedded lorebook fields use the same range selection. A marker-name match focuses and reveals its marker row with the matching text highlighted, since markers have no name input. Lorebook key selection applies once per visit so subsequent typing keeps its new caret position. Field controls focus without editing, reveal closed details, and scroll the match below the measured sticky toolbar in the overlay or page. CodeMirror focuses on the next animation frame so a host can open a closed details section first. Repeated visits reselect the range. `e2e/presetOverlay.spec.ts` covers collapsed-prompt and marker-name navigation at 1440 and 390 in both themes, plus closed utility and regex fields and offscreen ranges in long prompts; `e2e/editor.spec.ts` covers card code-editor and single-line matches. Each search runs in a worker terminated after 2 s, so a catastrophic pattern cannot hang the page; empty matches advance correctly, and at most 500 previews are listed while Replace all still covers all matches. Replacements write through field drafts. If fields change while the worker is running, replacement stops and asks for a fresh search. Feature sections contribute text through the `editorTextFields` registry (`fields.ts`), with override and disable by id; registration changes invalidate open previews. `card.embedded-lorebook` contributes book name and description, entry title, content, outlet, group and primary/secondary keys (`lorebookFields.ts`), including closed entries. Key replacements write their list draft under the entry’s uid, preserving comma-containing regex keys. `test/lorebookFields.test.ts` and `e2e/embeddedLorebook.spec.ts` cover replacements, closed-entry navigation, key range selection, continued typing after navigation and registry overrides.
- **Card long-text fields use CodeMirror 6** (`ui/CodeEditor.tsx`, dynamically imported `CodeEditorView.tsx`), including alternate greetings and module content. Single-line fields remain inputs; presets use the same editor with full-height prompt bodies; notes and message/summary editors also use it; lorebooks and composers retain their existing controls. The reusable editor soft-wraps prose (or a caller-selected monospace font), grows to a bounded height, then scrolls. Its left gutter uses `@codemirror/merge`'s incremental chunks to mark added (+), modified (bar), deleted-here (−), and unchanged (dot) lines against the draft's base revision, including empty final lines created by appended newlines. A change ending at an empty line includes that boundary line only when its base endpoint was inside a line; a base endpoint already at a line start leaves the unchanged suffix unmarked. Every user transaction calls the same `EditorStore` mutation as the textarea did, retaining its immediate browser copy, server drafts and recovery. Controlled echoes leave selection and native field undo intact; external replacements change only the differing span without echoing a new draft, and composition defers replacements until it ends. Every controlled update supersedes the deferred value, including an update matching the current document; only the latest pending replacement is applied, without firing `onChange`. CodeMirror lives in open shadow roots and mounts constructed stylesheets, preserving the strict CSP without inline style elements or a policy exception. Semantic CSS tokens supply fonts, colors, markers and diff highlights in both themes. The editor and diff libraries are lazy chunks, absent from the chat's initial dependency graph.
- **Textarea baseline measured before CodeMirror** (`perf/editor.perf.ts`, part of `npm run test:perf`, load average about 22): a card with a 50 KB description opens in 1.1 s, and typing in it has an input p95 of 48 ms with no frame over 100 ms; a preset with 100 prompts opens in 1.1 s, and toggling, moving and typing have an input p95 of 72 to 104 ms over two runs (a toggle re-renders the 100 rows) with no frame over 100 ms. The budgets are the chat's: 250 ms input p95, 3 frames over 100 ms, 8 s to open.
- `test/codeEditor.test.ts` covers line markers with full and incremental comparisons, including appended empty lines and insertions before existing interior or final blank lines. `e2e/codeEditorComposition.spec.ts` mounts the real editor through an isolated Vite harness and covers successive controlled updates during composition, including a return to the composing document, with no external-change echo.
- `e2e/editor.spec.ts` covers edit and save with the diff, undo, history restore, a card fork with unsaved changes, reordering by drag and keyboard, the regex approval, search and replace with a stopped pattern, and the update resolver; `layout.spec.ts` checks the card editor's layout and `csp.spec.ts` the avatar and the search worker under the CSP.

**Remaining editor limits:** there is no per-card-field agent-edit permission control or avatar-removal control. Per-chat prompt-preset overrides were rejected (DESIGN §6.1); cards use fork and swap, and lorebook selections/switches are chat state (§12), with no separate chat-local lorebook document editor. Card-family comparison is built: its picker compares this card’s saved content with another available member of its family (`features/cardForks/surfacesImpl.tsx`); it does not compare unsaved drafts or arbitrary unrelated cards.

---

## 15. Context assembly

`packages/server/src/assembler/` (DESIGN §5, DATA-MODEL §7.1). It turns a version being generated into a provider-neutral request (Chat Completions' messages, with names, plus ST's sampler values) and a record of where every piece of it came from, for the run inspector (DESIGN §7.4).

**The pipeline** (`pipeline.ts`, PLUGINS §8). Stages are registered by id in a stage registry (§6's generic registry) and run in registration order over one `Assembly`; a plugin overrides a stage by its id, wraps one by `get`ting it first, or disables one. The built-ins (`stages.ts`) register the same way:
- `path`: the version being generated, its slot and the slot before it
- `state`: the events on the path to that slot, folded (§3.8)
- `cast`: participants and the "played by me" set, each card through its layers; the speaker (the version's, else the first character not muted, else none); the generation type ST's presets name (`normal`, `swipe` for a new version of a reply, `impersonate` for a character the user plays)
- `model`: the account's current connection/model pair; summary retry/fallback jobs can explicitly override their request's model
- `preset`: the account's current prompt preset, else the built-in preset named Default
- `notes`: the chat's own notes and the cast cards' that are on, a linked slot with its shared note's head
- `mode`: the chat's role projection and its "only below the history" lorebook setting; a `layout.cards` event on the path is ignored, with one `layout_ignored` issue
- `lorebooks`: attached refs through their current heads, the user's global books the chat didn't turn off, and participant cards’ embedded books; the scan depth history must supply
- `lcm`: the selected summary layer and cursor, when enabled (§16)
- `macros`: the expander for preset and card text
- `scripts`: the preset's prompt regex scripts, compiled (below)
- `history`: the previous recorded start, pinned, and windows of the path, newest first, until the text read, as the regex scripts leave it, would fill the budget on its own
- `resolve`: the path's messages resolved, and given to the macros that read the chat
- `image-tokens`: each resolved image's token estimate for the model (`assembler/imageTokens.ts`; "Image tokens" below)
- `roles`: role projection (DESIGN §5.4). It dispatches to the projection the chat names, from the assembler's own projection registry (`assembler.projections`, `RoleProjection` in `pipeline.ts`: `id`, `label`, `role(message)`); `by_writer`, `alternate` and `perspective` are registered by `registerBuiltinProjections`. `roles.projection` stores an open string (the legacy ids stay valid) and is resolved at use: an id nothing registers (a plugin off or removed) assembles by writer and records a `role_projection_missing` issue, and the chat's settings show the id as "not installed" instead of dropping it. The Roles picker reads the worker registry's current labels and descriptions through `GetCast.roleProjections`; plugin projections are selectable and disabled built-ins disappear. A plugin registers, overrides or disables a projection in a scope like any registry entry.
- `note-gate`: condition gating (on a card's turn, while a card isn't muted), before any injections
- `lore-scan`: prompt regex scripts on resolved templates, reused during composition
- `lore-activation`: resolved scan inputs, keyword matching, issues and activation/skip source tags
- `end-of-request`: prepares the end-of-request renderer (below, "Who writes, and the direction"); `compose` expands it after other text and before prefill, budgets it, and `messages` sends it last
- `compose`: ST's prompt manager and budget, with the tail content and the prefill
- `messages`: squashing and post-processing, with the end-of-request message last, and the one fallback
- `breakpoints` (below), `request`, `images` (the request’s omitted images and rule reasons, §12)

**Image tokens** (DESIGN §10.2a; formulas, counts and calibration in §6). The `image-tokens` stage, after `resolve`, writes an `estimate` on every image of the history read (`RequestImage.estimate`: tokens or null, basis, detail, note, a bound, and `sent: false` for one left out). Images also carry their media's `sha256`, `width` and `height`, read with the history (`media_assets` columns; `describeImage` now reads PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, BMP and AVIF headers at upload, so new images have their size). It reads `AssemblySources.imageTokenFacts` (the stored counts for the model and its calibration samples; pinned in `SourcePins.imageTokenFacts`, so `RebuildContext` estimates as the snapshot did, and an older snapshot by formula alone) and the rules in the assembler's `imageTokens` registry (`Assembler.imageTokens`, passed to stages on `AssemblyRequest.imageTokens` like the macros and projections). Rules are tried newest registration first: a plugin or assembly module adds, overrides or disables one; the stage itself can be replaced like any stage. Initially newest first, images past the model's `maxImages` (four when unknown) and images carrying the fork's `excluded` flag are marked unsent: history is cut from its oldest end, so the newest N read are the newest N sent, as dispatch selects them. Composition re-evaluates the cap in the actual request order with the same `newestImages` helper as dispatch, including when a preset moves Newest message before History. The budget uses those final-order figures and the outer cut admits the complete candidate; an inner chronological cut cannot silently remove a turn whose image the final cap leaves out. A moved-Newest image layout uses an ordered suffix scan because its image costs need not decrease monotonically. Post-processing preserves image estimates when it merges messages.
- `tokensOf` counts a message's sent images: the figure, else the rule's bound, else a reserve of 1,024 for an unknown image (never zero). History chat lines carry their entry's images, so the history cut, the fixed-part check, the target and maximum, `fitSummaryOutput` for summary requests, the LCM prefix sizes, the cache-effect estimate (`prefixChange`, which takes a captured wire message's images from its inputs) and the inspector all count them.
- `GetNextContext` and the published cache estimate carry `imageTokens` (count, tokens as budgeted, how many are unknown, the bases). The client shows it above the composer through the `chat.composer-notes` slot (`composer.image-tokens`, `features/chat/imageTokens.tsx`), and each message's images in the context view.
- A reply fails with a `budget` error if its newest eligible message would be cut entirely; the error names its images, their budgeted token count and the history limit. A summary request likewise fails if any of its declared chunk would be cut, preventing an image from disappearing before summary admission. Images missing an estimate use the unknown reserve rather than zero.
- LCM leaf planning reads owner-scoped attachment hashes/dimensions with the path and estimates them for the summarizer through the same registered rules and stored facts, excluding images taken out in that fork. Its chunk sizes and recent-window trigger count image tokens; planning conservatively counts every image in a candidate chunk, while dispatch and request admission apply the model's last-N cap.
- Draft uploads use the same worker-installed registered formula rules, stored counts and calibration in the reader's preview, respecting the combined history/draft last-N limit. Drafts do not enter the generation snapshot until sent.
- Tests: `test/imageTokenCounts.test.ts` (shared serialization, cross-connection deduplication, persistent cache recheck and timeout/backoff), `e2e/imageTokens.spec.ts` (draft add/remove and inspector at 1440/390, light/dark), `test/imageTokens.test.ts` (each formula against the providers' documented examples, header sizes, the counting endpoints with a fake fetch, calibration), `test/imageTokenBudget.test.ts` (an expensive image moves the history's start; last-N; unknown at the reserve; summary output), `test/imageTokenEstimates.test.ts` (through the server with a mock provider: formula in the snapshot, context view and next context; ledger calibration of an unknown model; one background count with Anthropic's endpoint, used afterwards, never published).

Stages reach data only through `AssemblySources`, so the same pipeline runs over the database (`db/assemblySources.ts`) and over in-memory fixtures (`test/support/assemblySources.ts`, used by the tests and the ST harness). A stage that runs before what it needs throws (`need`): only a misregistered stage can cause it. `AssemblyFailed` (`no_model`, `budget`, `invalid_document`) is the one way a context fails as a whole.

**Final transforms** (`pipeline.ts`, `assembler.ts`). After the stages produce the snapshot, assembly runs the `transforms` registry in registration order. An `AssemblyTransform` receives the source-indexed snapshot and the original `AssemblyRequest`, and returns an effectful snapshot. It uses the same scoped register/override/wrap/disable contract as stages. Job metadata is present on the request before stages execute and on the snapshot before transforms execute. `summary-request` (`lcm/request.ts`) is the registered default for instruction rendering, direct/tool suffixes, output allowance, pinned-prefix application and raw/full request shaping. It does nothing for ordinary reply assembly. Replacing it controls the actual summary request; disabling it leaves the ordinary quiet assembly without those additions. Additional transforms can follow it. There is no summary-specific request-shaping pass after registered assembly; worker checks still enforce owned jobs, declared rendered inputs and the model’s output budget.

**Where it runs.** In a reader worker: `AssembleContext` (`db/assemblyReads.ts`) reads and assembles in one read transaction, so the context sees one snapshot of the database. It reads the path in one fork (`forkId`, else the fork through the slot, §3.8): the versions that fork shows, the events attached to them, and its summaries' freshness. The main thread gets two JSON strings: the snapshot, which it only passes on to be stored, and what generating needs of it (`Generation`: the request, the model, the variable writes, the values template messages drew, and the inspector's display metadata), which it parses. Each reader constructs one scoped `WorkerAssembler` service and shares it across context/rebuild, summary planning/execution and backfill-estimate handlers. Its stages, macros, projections and final transforms share the same registry objects for the reader’s life; trusted request modules (§15, Assembly modules in readers) install scoped registrations into those objects. Handlers do not construct private assemblers. Measured on a synthetic 100,000-message chat (source workers, load 13 to 15):
- with ST's default budget (4,095 tokens): 13 ms
- with one state event in the chat: 220 to 380 ms, all of it the walk to the root every state read made before chains (§3.8). `test/historyPerf.test.ts`'s 100,000-message assembly took 152 ms before chains and 46 ms after, on the same host (load about 1)
- with a 200,000-token preset: 1,662 messages, 250 to 340 ms, and a 2 MB result parsed on the main thread in 5 to 12 ms

The pipeline itself took 5 to 13 ms of that, measured alone on 2,000 messages.

**The sources over the database**, each the acting user's (another user's chat or version is `NotFound`, `test/assemblyStorage.test.ts`):
- **Cards** (DESIGN §4): a `card.binding` revision, else the card’s single upstream head. A card in the bin is left out; one whose document no longer decodes as a card fails the context (`invalid_document`), never silently replaced.
- **Notes:** the notes whose upstream head names the chat, or a participant card or a persona of the cast, as owner, at the revision `note.binding` pins. A card's note with `on_by_default` (an imported Character's Note, which ST always sends, or one the user marked "On in every chat") is on unless the chat turns it off (`note.deactivate`); any other note, the chat's own included, is off until `note.activate` (DESIGN §4.1: not activated in other chats automatically). A linked slot reads its shared note's resolved revision (`sharedHead`: the upstream head for globals, the fork binding for scoped snippets), pinned with the slot for a rebuild; a shared note in the bin pins as none, and the slot sends nothing.
- **The current prompt preset:** the account's `generation.preset`, else the built-in preset named Default. Historical `preset.binding`/`preset.unbind` events remain readable but do not select a preset. A connection lock changes the account selection when switching connections, rather than imposing a persistent assembly override.
- **The model:** what assembly needs (kind, wire API, key, prefill and breakpoint capabilities, a context size some source reported, its model-specific settings), merged by `resolveModel` (§3.12).

**Macros by source** (`macros.ts`, §10's caller contract). Every piece is expanded on its own through `expand()`:
- preset and card text always, card fields as ST's baseChatReplace does (trimmed, without card-field macros); notes count as card text
- a message of the path only when its mode is `template`, against the state before its own events (the cast, `{{user}}` and `{{char}}`, the card fields and the variables as they were there, so a later join, leave or pinned binding doesn't rewrite it) and the resolved text of the messages before it. Until a template's variable writes are recorded (no rollout `var.set` event of its version yet; `frozen_macros` can't tell, since a chat freezes its greetings when it's made), they're kept: they count for the pieces after it, as if at its position, and the generation records them there (below); after that they're state events, and expanding it again writes nothing
- each piece's random and time draws are kept by piece id (`preset:main`, `card:<entity>:description`, `message:<version>`), over a message's stored `frozen_macros`; passing them back builds the same context
- variable writes from preset and card text come back in order as the snapshot's `effects`, and each piece sees the variables the pieces before it left
- a piece that reaches a macro limit is left out and reported, not the whole turn
- messages the history didn't read still count for the macros that number messages (`{{lastMessageId}}`), as placeholders

**The one assembly path** (`st.ts`, `stages.ts`; DESIGN §1.2, §5.0). Every chat is assembled the same way, whatever its number of characters: the preset's order says where the cards go (below), and what ST 1.19 would send for one character, persona, preset and chat, with the join wrapper empty and lorebook entries by position, is what's sent here, with the deliberate differences below. There's no hidden mode. ST behaviour that depends on what the chat observably holds stays: with more than one character, names are prefixed by ST's group rule, `new_group_chat_prompt` replaces `new_chat_prompt`, and the user-played speaker's card has no prompt overrides; every persona of the cast is read. The group nudge and impersonation prompt are end-of-request texts of the preset (§9 "Conditioned prompts"; "Who writes, and the direction" below), whatever the cast size. History uses the same stepped cuts everywhere. `st.ts` follows ST's functions one by one (named in its comments):
- the prompt collection: the order's enabled prompts whose condition holds and whose triggers allow the generation type (§9 "Conditioned prompts"), with a disabled `main` kept empty; the end-of-request texts are never among them. A missing `newestMessage` marker in an older revision is resolved immediately after history. Composition budgets the complete history with its injections before moving the newest admitted history message and following depth-zero injections into that marker's slot. Adjacent history/newest slots preserve imported ST ordering; moving newest after the tail deliberately changes it
- markers filled where the order has them, and appended where it hasn't
