- the speaker's card prompt override and post-history instructions in place of `main` and `jailbreak`, unless the prompt forbids it or is off (below)
- in-chat prompts and notes injected by depth, then order, then role
- the budget: all consumers count toward the thresholds below. With `pin_examples` on, whole example blocks are admitted before history; off, history takes priority and examples use the remaining room. Admitted examples are retained until a history cut or configuration change, so a held start does not re-admit examples and rewrite the prefix. With `strip_examples` on, neither card nor lorebook example dialogues are sent, even with pinning on; direct uses of example-text macros remain text. `collapse_newlines` follows ST’s `baseChatReplace`: repeated newlines in expanded character fields and lorebook examples become one, then carriage returns are removed. Preset prompts and saved history keep their blank lines; persona descriptions are card fields and follow the switch too. Lore entries forced below history remain ordinary lore text, rather than example dialogues. The source pieces record the transformed text for the inspector.
- squashing, post-processing, names behavior, impersonation and swipes

The request maps to Chat Completions per provider kind as ST maps its sources (`wire.ts`): ST's custom source for OpenAI-compatible connections, OpenRouter's extra samplers, DeepSeek's non-zero `top_p`, a seed only when set, and ST's rules for models that refuse some samplers (OpenAI's o-series, GPT-5 and GPT-6 Astra take `max_completion_tokens`; Claude's newest take no samplers, and its limited ones temperature or `top_p`).

**The fidelity gate** (`npm run test:st`, §7). A synthetic lorebook adds four captures across both cards and the default/full presets: keyword, AND ALL, NOT ANY, constant, two depth roles, author-note top/bottom and example top/bottom entries. ST must actually send the keyword entry, so equal empty results cannot pass. Two synthetic cards: one with a prompt override, post-history instructions, two example blocks and a Character's Note in the user's role; one with none of these and no personality. Each has its chat and the fixture persona, and is captured under ST's fresh settings, the three fixture presets, the poison preset, the `full` preset with each of ST's four post-processing modes, ST's impersonate and swipe on two presets, a Summarize extension summary after the main prompt, before it, and in-chat at the Character's Note's depth and role (saved on the chat's second message, with the extension's settings, and read as ST reads it when a chat opens; the harness checks ST sent it), and an author's note (set in the chat's metadata, read the same way): in-chat at the Character's Note's depth and role, after the main prompt, before it with the summary there too, and in-chat every second message of the user's, which one chat reaches with the message sent and the other doesn't (the harness checks ST sent it in one and not the other); an empty send with the full preset counts its filler toward interval 2, and notes after main as user and before main as assistant retain those roles on both cards. The gate also covers a summary template writing a variable read by the author's note, interval-2 impersonation, and skipped section and depth-zero notes under squashing and impersonation. Our side imports the card through our importer, and the settings ST had in force through our preset importer, with the card join wrapper set empty (ST's single-character output) and lorebook entries by position (`lorebook.below_history` off). The persona's placement is imported as ST folders import it (`buildPersona`), and ST's page is set to the same position, depth and role. The gate's persona cases: the description at the top and at the bottom of the author's note, in-chat, after the main prompt as the user and before it as the assistant, with ST's default note when the chat has none, with the lore at the note's top and bottom, with the note switched off or not due (nothing sent), and the persona at depth and not sent. The author's note is parsed and mapped through the real chat/default-settings importer. Empty chat and default notes cover depth 0 as user, after main as user, before main as assistant, frequency 0 and frequency 99, with personas at both sides and lore. An active nonempty note edited to frequency 0 also suppresses both attachments. The harness checks that ST sent the description (or not) as each case says. The first chat has a reply that the `full` preset's prompt-only regex script strips, and the harness approves the presets' scripts on both sides. Results (2026-10-13): 142 of 142 requests equal, apart from the documented differences (62 before the persona cases, which add 36; empty-note and active-zero regressions add 44); checked against breakage, squashing with a blank line instead of ST's newline made 14 of the 26 differ, and putting the author's note after the Character's Note and sending a note on every turn whatever its interval each made one of the author's note's requests differ. Since notes dropped insertion frequency (DESIGN §4.1), the interval and frequency-0 cases are documented differences, and an `allowWIScan` case keys a lorebook entry only on the author's note's text, which ST must trigger. Results (2026-10-27, unit P1-2): 140 of 144 requests equal apart from the documented differences; the other 4 (an impersonation's last-turn filler and its prompt order, and the legacy preset's `[Stay in character.]` after the group nudge) come from unit P1-1 and appear on the merge before P1-2 too; that run predates the one assembly path too. Results (2026-10-28, fb-gate, master plus fb-rp, fb-notes, fb-sum): 144 of 144 requests equal apart from the documented differences. Before the structural matchers, 6 differed: the closing user turn on impersonation, the nudge shifting later messages (legacy preset), and the speaking card's override and post-history instruction on an impersonation (owner: the persona has none). The other sections (fixtures, macros, preset round trips, preview overlap) were equal apart from the macro deviations. Results (2026-10-30, w1, the end-of-request instruction): 168 of 168 requests equal apart from the documented differences, the other sections the same; before the gate took the end-of-request texts off a message ST's "single" post-processing had named (`Tobin: […]`), and before the impersonation-override matcher covered the preset's own post-history prompt left at the end once ST's replacement is taken out, 3 differed.

Reference captures disable only PromptManager’s automatic `tryGenerate()` preview call before page initialization. In ST 1.19.0, previews and real sends mutate the same author-note buffer while awaiting lore activation; overlap can append lore twice. The real generation path and strict request comparator are unchanged. A deterministic regression runs the actual ST reset/await/append sequence: its unprotected control must duplicate both adjacent lore entries, and the protected capture must send each once. A unit scheduling test covers the same interleaving; a changed pinned preview call site fails capture rather than silently weakening isolation.

**Deliberate differences from ST.** The gate lists each documented difference it sees apart, without failing:
- "Stream": we always stream; ST sends the preset's `stream_openai`. A person watches the reply (PROVIDERS §7.2).
- "End-of-request instruction" (DESIGN §2.2, owner 2026-10-29): we send one user message last, the speaker's turn text (the group nudge whenever the model writes as a character, one or many; the impersonation prompt when it writes as the persona) and the direction; ST sends the nudge only in groups, at its prompt position, and the impersonation prompt at its marker, last. The gate takes our texts off the end of our request (a message of its own, or the end of the user message a merging post-processing mode joined it to) and the same impersonation text off the end of ST's, before diffing, so later messages aren't reported as shifted (`dropEndOfRequest` in `test/st/assembly.ts`).
- "Impersonation override": on an impersonation turn the speaking card's prompt override and post-history instructions apply (owner, §2: impersonating the persona, none); ST applies the chat character's. The gate documents the line where the character's override text is missing from our system prompt, and takes the character's post-history instruction out of ST's request when we send none, or, where ST's replaces the preset's own post-history prompt, documents the line.
- "`{{char}}` in groups" (DESIGN §5.3): in preset text `{{char}}` is every character in the chat but the persona, comma-joined, whoever speaks; ST uses the current speaker in a group. The gate's chats have one character, where both are its name, so it's listed and not compared.
- "Name wrappers" and "one-character mode": ST sends a lone character's fields bare and wraps them only when it joins a group's. Here there is never only one character (`{{user}}` is a cast card, DESIGN §5.0), so that mode is removed: every cast card's definitions, the persona's included, are wrapped `<Name>…</Name>`, in the per-field markers and at the Definitions marker. The gate sets our join wrapper empty (ST's single-character output), so the wrappers are documented here and not compared.
- "Summaries as blocks": summaries are separate blocks placed like the history, usually just before it, so a new summary never invalidates the earlier ones (PHASE1-DECISIONS §2); ST sends one monolithic summary at its position. The gate's summary cases import ST's summary as one note at ST's position and compare equal; summaries made here aren't compared.
- "n": no multi-swipe requests; ST sends a preset's `n` above 1. DESIGN §3.1: "There's no dedicated 'generate ×N' action".
- "send_if_empty": expanded like all preset text when it fills the user's turn; ST sends it as written before an impersonation. After a reply with an empty composer, ST sends it as a new user message that it stores in the chat; here it's in the context only.
- "Macros expand once": ST expands a card field, then the marker prompt holding it again (`preparePrompt`), and extension prompts again when it joins them (`getExtensionPrompt`), so text a macro produced, or an escaped `\{\{`, expands the second time. Here a macro's output is never expanded again (§10).
- "Chat messages verbatim": ST runs every message through its macros when it builds the prompt and when the user sends one; here typed and generated text stays as written unless the message is a template (DESIGN §5.6: "Text the user types is not macro-expanded by default", "Model output is not macro-substituted by default").
- "History cuts": once the maximum is crossed, history drops to the target instead of ST’s moving maximum cut. The assembly gate adds two synthetic overflow captures and requires fewer retained history turns than ST; existing below-maximum cases still compare equal.
- "Token counts": ST counts the budget with the model's tokenizer; here a token is four characters plus three per message, plus each sent image's estimate ("Image tokens" below), so which messages fit at the margin can differ. The budget is the preset's context size, within a context size some source reported for the model, less its response length.
- "Fixed prompts over budget": ST shows an error and sends what it had built before the overflow; here the generation fails, saying the preset's prompts don't fit (`budget`).
- "Prefill": sent to any model whose prefill capability is `assistant`, and otherwise dropped and reported (DESIGN §1.2); ST sends it only for its Claude source and ignores it elsewhere. A prefill that's sent comes out of the budget; ST doesn't count it, so it can send more than the preset's context size.
- "Post-processing": the preset's `postprocess` always applies; in ST it's a connection setting, which a preset changes only when it's bound to the connection.
- "Reasoning and stops": ST's `reasoning_effort` and `verbosity` for the OpenAI models it lists aren't sent, nor its custom stop strings and logit-bias presets.
- "Card overrides": the speaker's card's, in every chat (ST's group generation takes the speaker's too). ST expands a card's prompt override twice, the second time with `{{original}}`; here it's expanded once, in ST's order (before the preset's prompts), and its first `{{original}}` then becomes the prompt it replaces.
- "Regex scripts": a script's replacement is expanded before the match is put into it, so text it captures from a message isn't expanded (ST expands after, per match: a random macro in it draws once here). Their pattern, replacement and trim strings are expanded before the path is read, since the scripts decide how far back it's read: macros in them that read the chat (`{{lastMessage}}`) find it empty. A case-insensitive pattern matches both cases of ASCII letters only, and `u` is dropped where nothing in the pattern needs it. Scripts whose pattern expands macros escaped (`substituteRegex` 2), or that need backtracking, are left out and reported (below).
- "Several notes at one depth": joined in the order above, by note id within each group; ST joins its extension prompts in key order. The summary and the author's note come first, as ST's `1_memory` and `2_floating_prompt` sort before `DEPTH_PROMPT`.
- "Author's note": a note goes every turn (DESIGN §4.1: no insertion frequency), so ST's author's note at frequency 2 or more, which ST skips on some turns, differs on those turns; a frequency-0 note imports off, and what rides on it is still sent where it sits (owner question 6), where ST sends nothing. Empty chat notes and default settings are imported, off, with their position, depth and role; only missing settings use ST's built-in defaults for what rides on the note (below). ST's per-character author's note is a card note at the top or bottom of the author's note (§13), joined there rather than replacing or prefixing ST's floating prompt text.
- "Summaries": the summary's template is expanded once, with the summary in place of `{{summary}}`; ST expands it, then the whole prompt again, which only shows when a macro in it draws or writes. An in-prompt summary keeps its imported role (system when absent), as other section notes do (§13). `{{summary}}` is explicit (DESIGN §8 [D], owner 2026-10-28): it reads the chat's switched-on summary notes (kind `imported_summary`) on the path that are due by their condition (a summary note's condition is checked here as the note gate checks it later), in note order, joined with blank lines, and is empty when none is on; it never reads the LCM layer, whose summaries are separate messages before history (§16). A switched-off note sends nothing anywhere. ST leaves the macro as written when the extension is off. What it fills in is recorded on the snapshot (`summaryFill`: the preset prompts holding it, notes or none, their count and tokens) and projected into `PresetContext.summary`, which the preset editor's badge and the Notes tab read.
- U20 text/context switches are compared against ST 1.19 in all eight combinations, with multiline card fields/examples, plus pinned and unpinned cases over the context maximum and card fields read through preset macros with collapse on and off. The registered field macros receive the preset's newline switch through the macro context, so `{{persona}}`, `{{mesExamples}}`, greetings and other field macros follow the same expand/collapse/remove-carriage-returns order as field markers; surrounding prompt text and the unexpanded character version retain their newlines. `ST_DIFF_ONLY=assembly ST_DIFF_TEXT_SWITCHES=1 npm run test:st` runs that subset. The overflow cases compare the fixed prefix and independently assert example presence on both sides; different history cuts remain the documented stepped-history difference. Unit regressions live in `presetTextSwitches.test.ts` and the shared macro tests; `e2e/presetTextSwitches.spec.ts` covers draft/revision persistence and 1440/390 light/dark views.
- Not built, so not compared: regex scripts other than prompt-only ones (ST rewrites stored messages with them as they're sent or received). Continue and notes/card modules are implemented separately; their current behavior is documented above.

**Card definitions** (DESIGN §5.0, §5.2; `stages.ts` `composeStage`, `layouts.ts`), filled the way the preset says, never per chat:
- **Per-field markers** (`charDescription`, `charPersonality`, `scenario`, and `{{description}}`, `{{personality}}`, `{{scenario}}` in preset text) are filled as ST's group join fills them (`getGroupCharacterCardsLazy`): each character's trimmed field, expanded with `{{char}}` bound to that character, is wrapped in the preset's `utility_prompts.card_join_prefix` and `card_join_suffix` (absent: `<{{char}}>\n` and `\n</{{char}}>`; `{{char}}` that character, `<FIELDNAME>` `Description`, `Personality` or `Scenario`), empty fields are skipped, and the parts are joined with newlines in cast order, muted members included (DESIGN §3.5). `personality_format` and `scenario_format` apply to the joined text, as the macros read it. A card's notes placed with the definitions (`after_main_prompt`, and older notes' `section: card`) go at the top of their card's description part, inside its wrapper. The macros read the joined fields without notes, already expanded, so their macros never expand again (`CardFields.joined`). Template messages read the first character's fields, as before. ST imports get the default wrapper; the fidelity gate sets it empty, which is ST's single-character output.
- **`personaDescription`:** each persona's description, with its notes placed with the definitions at the top, wrapped like the characters' parts (`<Tobin>…</Tobin>` by default) for the one selected persona (DESIGN §5.0: there is never only one character, `{{user}}` is a cast card; ST sends one persona bare, a documented difference the gate's empty wrapper hides).
- **The Definitions marker** (`charDefinitions`, `@rp/shared/presetOrder.ts`, `layouts.ts`): each character whole (its notes placed with the definitions, description, personality, scenario, joined with blank lines), then each persona (its notes so placed, its description), each wrapped in the preset's join prefix and suffix like the per-field parts (`<Mira>…</Mira>` by default, `<FIELDNAME>` `Definitions`), joined with blank lines; a card with nothing to send is left out. It's never added to an existing order; the preset editor inserts it. When the order sends it, the per-field markers and `personaDescription` stay empty.
- **Examples** keep their handling: each character's parsed into example chats, names prefixed with more than one character.
- **Card prompt overrides** (DESIGN §5.0): the speaker's card's `system_prompt` and `post_history_instructions` replace `main` and `jailbreak`, as ST's group generation takes them, on impersonation turns too (owner 2026-10-28; ST passes the character's prompts for every generation type): a card the user plays applies its own, and a persona without them has none. An empty override does nothing, a prompt that forbids overrides or is off is kept, and the first `{{original}}` becomes the replaced text. Each decision is recorded in the snapshot's `cardOverrides` (replaced, forbidden, disabled, missing), which the preset editor shows at the prompt through `PresetContext.overrides` ("Replaced in this chat by Aria's system prompt", "Aria has a system prompt; this prompt forbids it", "A card can't replace it"). The card editor's prompt fields say whether their text is used in the preset chats use now (`GetCardPromptUse`, a reader op reading the preset's `main`/`jailbreak` flags, no assembly). The built-in Default preset forbids overrides on `main` and `jailbreak` (`forbidCardOverrides`), and prompts the preset editor inserts forbid them; imported ST presets keep their file's value, and the import report lists the prompts a card can replace (`preset.overrides_allowed`).
- **Card layout is never per chat:** `layout.cards` events still parse (old events), but assembly ignores them and says so once per context. The card-filling code stays split by card (`cardBlock`, the per-card parts), so a later global card-swap mode can put one card's part at the tail.
- **Prompt-name controls** (DESIGN §5.3; backlog U09): This chat → Prompt names is the registered `settings.macro-bindings` contribution to `chatSettingSections`. It shows the next prompt’s `{{user}}` and `{{char}}` names, using in-chat model names, and whether each follows the cast/personas or is pinned. Pin preserves the current value or an explicit override (including empty text); Unpin resumes the live cast/persona set. `GetCast` returns `macroBindings` and resolved `macroNames`; `ChangeCast(MacroBinding)` records `macro.binding` or `macro.unbind` through the existing ownership-checked writer operation, at the viewed fork and leaf, publishing `State`. Unbind removes just that map entry. Pins are independent of the turn’s speaker; card-local expansion rules remain the same. The control explains scope and the cache rebuild. After a successful save, the session rereads the current cast while its fork and leaf are still displayed, rather than applying the save’s potentially older snapshot. Cast reads reject superseded results and results for a moved or disposed view; a failed save leaves in-flight reads valid. `client/test/session.test.ts` covers navigation, overlapping pin responses and failed saves. Tests cover the reducer, assembly with three speakers, nickname resolution, fork isolation and the browser controls across reload/persona changes (`shared/test/state.test.ts`, `server/test/assembler.test.ts`, `server/test/chatSetup.test.ts`, `client/e2e/backlogBindingsCutoff.spec.ts`).
- **Stable bindings** (DESIGN §5.3): in preset text `{{char}}` is every character of the cast except the single selected persona (muted ones included) and `{{user}}` the single selected persona, unless `macro.binding` pins them; for one character and one persona these are ST's. A card's own text binds `{{char}}` to that card, a persona's `{{user}}` to that persona.
- **No tail directive:** the group nudge and the impersonation prompt are end-of-request texts, with `{{speaker}}` naming who writes (below, "Who writes, and the direction"); nothing fills the `impersonate` marker.
- **Disabling the `notes` stage (M2):** raw `stages.disable("notes")` is safe: the stages after it read a missing `notes` as none (no notes, no author's-note placement, no off-note), so the context is that of a chat without notes (`test/assembler.test.ts`). Other stages stay required; only optional features have this baseline.
- **`roles.projection`:** `by_writer` (ST's: messages on the user's side are `user`, the side taken from the speaker when it's a card, else from the slot's first version); `alternate` (by depth: even depths, the greeting's, are `assistant`, so roles never change as the chat grows; the end-of-request message still ends the request on a user turn, below); `perspective` (the speaker's own messages are `assistant`). A message is named by its recorded speaker, also one who has since left the cast (an earlier persona, say: `resolveStage` reads those cards into `departed`), else by its side's first card.
- **The LCM layer** (§16) is one system message per summary before the history in every chat.

**Notes** (DESIGN §4.1, §5.2; §13 for the imported ones). Every note that's on, and due this turn, goes where its placement says (the start of the context and the start and end of the tail are placed relative to the preset's sections in `composeStage`, §15 "Newest message"):
- **In the chat** at its depth and role, as ST's extension prompts go; a `tail` note at depth 0, DESIGN §4.1's "tail/D0" [P].
- **Beside the main prompt,** before or after it, one after another as ST's `injectToMain` puts its summary and then its author's note: each "before" goes in at the start in turn, so the later one ends up first. An in-chat main takes them as injections beside it, the same way. Section notes keep their role, defaulting to system for documents without one.
- **With the definitions**, for a card's note (`after_main_prompt`, or older notes' `section: card`; owner, 2026-10-28): at the top of its card's part of the `charDescription` marker (or the `personaDescription` one for a persona), inside the card's wrapper, or at the top of its card's part at the Definitions marker. A chat's note "with the definitions" goes after the main prompt (above); a chat's `section: card` note has no card to go with and is left out. A conditioned card note there is allowed (the editor warns): due or skipped, its piece is volatile (`markVolatile`), so the card's part, and the message holding it, is never in the cached prefix; skipped, it sends nothing. The preset editor lists these notes in the "With definitions" slot (`presetContext.ts`).
- **At the top or bottom of the author's note** (`author_note_top`, `author_note_bottom`) joins the author's note's text with newlines, as ST adds a persona description (`addPersonaDescriptionExtensionPrompt`) and World Info's author's-note entries (`getWorldInfoPrompt`): `persona, note, lore-top, author's note, lore-bottom, note, persona`. The result goes where the author's note goes. It rides on that note: when the author's note isn't due this turn (its condition doesn't hold), nothing of it is sent, as on ST's skipped turns; when it's switched off, only its own text goes, and what rides on it is still sent where it sits, joined around an empty line (owner question 6; ST sends nothing of a frequency-0 note, a documented difference). A chat without an author's note has ST's default one: empty, in-chat at depth 4 as the system, every message, so what rides on it is sent there. This holds for the lore at the top or bottom too.
- **Nowhere** for `none`.

Their order is ST's for its extension prompts (`1_memory`, `2_floating_prompt`, `DEPTH_PROMPT`, `PERSONA_DESCRIPTION`): the chat's summary, its author's note (`isAuthorNote` in `@rp/shared/docs/note.ts`: the chat's note the last `author_note.set` names, none after one naming null (`ChatState.authorNote` `false`), and only without any such event one of kind `author_note`; the chat's Notes tab marks it "author's note" and sets it, `test/authorNote.test.ts` in shared), its other notes [P], then the characters' notes, then the personas', each group by id. Notes at one depth and role are joined with newlines in that order. A card's note expands with `{{char}}` bound to its card, the chat's notes and a persona's with the chat's bindings.

**A persona's notes** are ordinary card notes (DESIGN §4.1: no hidden note kinds). The notes of every persona of the cast are read with the characters'. The persona's description is always sent where the persona goes (`personaDescription`, the Definitions marker); a note's kind or title never takes it away (`persona_description`, still decoded in old revisions, is ignored; migration 0050, §14.4). An ST import with the persona placed elsewhere leaves the card's description empty and puts the text in a note at that place (§13), so it is sent once. The gate compares the positions against ST (§15).

**ST's Character's Note** for macros and lorebook matching (`{{charDepthPrompt}}`, an entry's "Character's note" match) is `characterNoteText` (`types.ts`): the first character's notes that are on (for the lorebook, due) and placed in the chat, at a depth or after the newest message, as ST's note is, joined with newlines in id order. Only those visible settings decide it; before, it was the card's oldest note not made from a module and not of kind `character_author_note`.

**A condition** (DESIGN §4.1) makes a note due only on a card's turn (the speaker of the message being generated, a swipe's or continuation's included, and the user's own card when they write as it, owner question 8) or while a card isn't muted (P1-1's mute, `presence.set`, at the leaf, `isMuted`); a card that isn't in the cast at the leaf holds neither. A note whose condition doesn't hold isn't due: no text and no macro effects, and nothing rides on it if it's the author's note. The inspector's note activation carries the condition in words ("on Aria's turn") with whether it was due. **Cache:** a conditioned note is volatile at its placement: one at or below the history never touches the cached prefix, and one placed above it (allowed, owner question 7; the editor warns) keeps its potential boundary there on every turn, due or not, so the cached prefix ends before it. Conservatively, each conditioned note is a potential variable writer: any note or prompt expanded after it that reads variables is volatile at its actual placement, including on skipped turns and when the reader expands to empty; this tracks a single expansion-order flag and variable reads, not a dependency graph. Empty cache markers do not decide the last message's role for `send_if_empty`; their volatility propagates before they are removed ahead of squashing and post-processing. An older note's `interval` is ignored: it goes every turn. Tested in `test/notes.test.ts` and `test/assembler.test.ts`, over the database and on branches in `test/assemblyStorage.test.ts`, and the cache in `test/steppedHistory.test.ts` and `test/warmthSources.test.ts`.

**The lorebook scan switch** (DESIGN §4.1). Each due note with `scan_lore` (not an imported summary) has its expanded text added to the lorebook scan as ST's `addInject` adds its extension prompts: whole, whatever its placement and the scan depth (`loreActivationStage`, `injections`). A card's note is expanded as it's sent (`{{char}}` its card, without card fields). An entry whose primary key is in a scanned note's own text names the note in its reason (`why.notes`), which the request inspector shows.

**Imported summaries** (DESIGN §8.1, §13). A chat's `imported_summary` note, while the chat has it on (`note.activate`, as the import sets it when ST sent it), goes where ST's Summarize extension put it. Its text is the newest of its revisions saved on a message of the context's path, up to the leaf (DATA-MODEL §15, invariant 5): a branch from before the latest summary sends the one saved by then, and one from before the first sends none (a revision with no source message applies wherever its binding does; the template and placement are the bound revision's). The chat note list and editor use the same path selection, showing empty content before the first summary while retaining the bound settings. Saving a correction creates a revision without the imported `source_slot_id`; its branch-local binding supplies applicability, even before the first imported summary (`test/importedSummaryEdit.test.ts`). It reads the revisions back along their first parents, at most 1,000, and which of their messages are on the path, both of which never change, so a rebuild reads the same. It goes out as its template with the summary in place of `{{summary}}` (or `Summary: ` and the summary, without one), expanded with the chat's macros, in-chat at its depth and role, or beside the main prompt (before or after it, in its message group, so squashing merges them as ST's does; an in-chat main takes it as an injection beside it). Expansion follows dependency order independently of placement: main, then the summary template, then the author's note, the other chat notes and card notes. Each sees preceding variable writes. The hidden messages it covers stay out of the context, like every hidden message. `{{summary}}` reads it anywhere while it is on. With summarization on it is placed by its own switch too, beside the summaries; enabling summarization switches it off in the same write (§16 *Enabling and backfill*), and nothing suppresses it out of sight. Tested in `test/assembler.test.ts`, over the database in `test/assemblyStorage.test.ts`, on an imported chat's branches in `test/stImport.test.ts`, and against ST by the gate.

**Who writes, and the direction** (DESIGN §2.2, owner 2026-10-27; the end-of-request instruction, owner 2026-10-29, provisional), in every chat:
- **Conditions** are evaluated in `collectionOf`, for in-chat prompts too, beside the prompt's ST triggers (§9): `as_user` when the speaker is in the played-by-me set, `as_character` when it's any other card, `with_direction` when the version's direction isn't blank. A summary request sends only `always` prompts [owner question 11]. The inspector's piece source names the condition that admitted a conditioned prompt ("Preset prompt “No impersonation” (sent when writing as a character)"). Conditions stay for the order's prompts, such as anti-impersonation lines.
- **The end-of-request message** (`end-of-request` stage, `@rp/shared/endOfRequest.ts`) is one user message, always the request's last, after everything else: post-history instructions, other prompts after the history, the notes at the end of the tail, the lorebook's block below the history. It holds the speaker's turn text, `impersonationPrompt` ("when your card speaks") when the speaker is in the played-by-me set, else `groupNudge` ("when a character speaks"), then, when the version's direction isn't blank, a newline and `directionPrompt` ("when you give a direction"). Neither conditions nor ST's triggers apply to the three; their stored role is ignored. Each is its own piece, with its preset source, so the inspector names each ("End of request: when a character speaks", `sourceDescriptors.ts`). A text that's empty, or expands to nothing, is left out. The registered stage installs `renderEndOfRequest`; `compose` expands it after ordinary prompts and notes and before prefill, so variable reads see earlier writes, end writes cannot affect the prefix, and prefill can read end writes. `compose` reserves its tokens in the budget; `messages` appends it after squashing and post-processing, so it remains one separate, verbatim user message in every mode, including merge and single.
- **With a prefill** (Continue, or the preset's prefill on a model that takes one), the message is the last of `messages`, so it goes right before the partial assistant message the wire appends (`withPrefill` in `wire.ts`), which stays last. **A summary request** sends none: the summary instruction takes its place as the final user message (§16 "Summary requests"), with no direction.
- **The one fallback:** when there's no end-of-request message (both texts empty, or the stage disabled), no prefill and no summary, and the final request's last message is the assistant's, a user message `[Write {{speaker}}'s next message.]` (source `assembler`, "end of request fallback: who writes next", volatile) goes last. It's decided on the final request, after post-processing, so an assistant prompt or note at the tail is caught too. The budget reserves it whenever there's no end-of-request message. It's no issue on the reply: only the request inspector shows it (contexts before assembler version 30 recorded a `last_turn_filled` issue, which `toVersion` never shows). The closing user turn after the last chat message and the default direction prompt with its `direction_prompt_missing` issue are gone (assembler version 34).
- **`{{speaker}}`** is the speaker's in-chat name and **`{{direction}}`** the direction, verbatim: a macro's output is never expanded again (§10), so a `{{random}}` typed in a direction stays literal. Outside a generation, and in a template message of the chat, both stay as written. A piece whose text names either is volatile (`macros.ts`), a conditioned prompt after the history is volatile, and the end-of-request message is volatile, so none of them ever sits inside the cached prefix.
- **The reference-harness delta** (its "Satellite block and final user turn" and "Cache control"): reference-harness always ends on one user turn carrying its volatile satellite, so everything that changes per turn follows its cache-stable timeline. Here the layout is the preset's (DESIGN §5.0), and the end-of-request message plays reference-harness's final user turn: per-turn text is volatile, after the history breakpoint, and the request ends on it. A conditioned prompt above the history (an anti-impersonation line in Main marked `as_character`) does change the prefix on impersonation turns: the owner's decided trade, which the inspector shows.
- **The preset editor** (`PromptDocument.tsx`) has a **Condition** select and the **Generation types** checkboxes on each prompt of the order (each shown as a badge when it limits the prompt). The three texts are in a fixed **End of request** section below the order (`EndOfRequest.tsx`, registered as `preset.end-of-request` in the `preset.editor.sections` slot, `presetSections.ts`): "When a character speaks", "When your card speaks" and "When you give a direction (added after the turn text)", each with a line saying when it's sent, and nothing to drag, delete, switch off or condition. The warnings at the top (`PresetWarnings`, computed by `presetWarnings` in `shared/presetConditions.ts`) are a direction text without `{{direction}}`, which would lose the direction, with a link to the text; and each switched-on prompt of the order whose condition and generation types never meet (§9, `gatesNeverMeet`), named, with both gates in words, that it's off in an ST export, and **Go to the prompt**, which expands and scrolls to it (`PresetEditor` hands `PromptDocument` a `goTo` request). Tests: `shared/test/presetWarnings.test.ts`, client `test/endOfRequest.test.ts`, `server/test/presetFormat.test.ts` (export and reimport), `e2e/rpLoop.spec.ts` (setting a condition the prompt's types never meet, the warning, the link, clearing it). It subscribes to that field’s current text draft, so removing or restoring the macro updates the warning before a revision save. The fixed sections use the toolbar’s measured scroll margin, so the warning’s link reveals the field below the sticky toolbar. The impersonation notice (DESIGN §2.2) counts only prompts of the order marked for writing as `{{user}}` (an `as_user` condition, or ST triggers of only `impersonate`); the end-of-request text "when your card speaks" doesn't, so a preset without such a prompt shows the notice once.

**Cache breakpoints** (`breakpoints.ts`, DESIGN §5.5), placed structurally, as deltas from reference-harness's scheme (its `src/agent/cache-breakpoints.ts`, ARCHITECTURE "Cache control"):
- **Every message knows what it's made of** (`Stability` in `st.ts`): the preset's and cards' prompts ahead of the chat (`prefix`), lorebook entries pinned above the history and whatever follows the first place one can go, until the history (`pinned`: stable until the history's start moves, below), the chat's own messages (`history`), and what moves or changes every turn (`volatile`): in-chat prompts and notes, which keep their depth as the chat grows, the tail, the user turn ST fills in, a message a depth-bounded regex script will change as it gets deeper (one that applies now and has a maximum depth, or one that matches and hasn't reached its minimum yet), and one a script was skipped for. Squashing and post-processing merge it with the text. reference-harness finds its boundaries by searching the wire text for `<system>` and `<conversation_summary`, and flags that as a weakness itself.
- **reference-harness's (a), the leading system item:** here `prefix`, the last message of the leading run of prefix messages, which ends at the first pinned lore, the summary layer or the history. It's what survives a move of the history's start: the preset's prompts, the card definitions and card overrides wherever the preset's order puts them before `chatHistory`, and notes placed there. With "only below the history" on, no lore is above the history, so it reaches the summary layer or the history. When the provider takes them as its system parameter, its own marker there covers them; with pinned lore, Anthropic's system parameter ends with the (a) message (`wire.ts`), so the pinned lore after it is sent as messages and keeps its own marker.
- **reference-harness's (b), the conversation summary:** the last summary message (§16), after stable history. Prefix and layer keep separate message boundaries through squashing and post-processing.
- **Pinned lore** (a delta: reference-harness has no lorebooks): a marker after the last pinned message before the history, when a slot is free.
- **reference-harness's (c), the last timeline item the next session reproduces verbatim:** here `history`, the last message before the first volatile one. The deepest in-chat prompt or note is where the next request starts to differ, since it moves down as messages are added; with none, it's the chat's last message. **Priority** (most important first, as a provider with fewer free markers keeps the first): while the history's start holds, (c), then (a), then (b), then the pinned marker, so with two markers (a) beats (b); after the start moved (a cut, a restore, LCM's cursor), (a) comes first, since the history after it is new anyway. With an `explicit` model a fifth boundary can't be marked (Anthropic's four, one taken by pi-ai's own): the snapshot records a `cache_breakpoints` issue naming what isn't marked, rather than dropping it silently.
- **reference-harness's (d) and (e), every request's end and the previous request's:** pi-ai already marks the last message, and Anthropic reads back about 20 blocks before a marker.
- **No token floor:** reference-harness's 1,024-token minimum is Bedrock's; Anthropic ignores a marker on a shorter prefix.
- **Off unless the model's breakpoints are `explicit`,** as reference-harness's are off unless configured: the snapshot always records them, and the provider request sends them only then (§6 "Explicit cache breakpoints").

**Stepped history** (`stages.ts`, DESIGN §5.5, owner 2026-10-13). The maximum is the current model’s context size, capped by its reported window. Its optional `settings.context_target` is a total token count; absent, it defaults to 80% of the maximum [P]. Context and response length are edited on the model, and folder-imported prompt presets never change them. Response reserve, prefill, fixed prompts, admitted examples, notes and summaries in every placement, and the stable lorebook tail allowance all count toward the same total. Hidden messages spend nothing. Budget checks use the larger estimate before and after squashing/post-processing, counting names and placeholders those transforms introduce while retaining ST’s conservative pre-transform accounting [P].

The start is a slot and its path depth, recorded as `historyStart` in every new context. Below the maximum, it stays at the previous start. Crossing the maximum removes whole oldest messages until the total fits the target. A first context that fits the maximum retains the full history; with no prior start, an overflow cuts to the target. If fixed content alone exceeds the target, that content is the minimum achievable total [P]; exceeding the maximum fails assembly. The admitted example count is part of the cut state and source pins; it is reconsidered only at a cut or a configuration change (preset, model, cast, notes, the lorebooks read and the chat's lorebook setting: `cutState.ts` `configurationOf`). Admission and cuts use a composition with all note reservations present, including skipped intervals, then send only the actual due text. Re-admission recomputes the target floor before cutting.

With LCM on, recent messages kept as written have their own pair (DESIGN §8; §16 *Defaults*): the maximum and target above become the fixed part plus the recent maximum and target, in tokens. Both pairs are first scaled down by one factor when their maxima don't fit the model's context beside the fixed part and the reply, keeping their proportions, and the recent pair is then fitted to the history allowance left after the layer reserve and admitted examples (`fitPair`); the total limit always wins and no reply fails because of them. A summary request (`summarizing`) is bounded only by the window, since it must render its whole chunk. The recent pair cuts only messages that can still be summarized: when its cut would turn fresh failed leaf ranges into markers, the start moves back to the earliest such range's first message whose history fits the allowance before the pair capped it, so those ranges stay raw while the window fits (§16). The fitted pairs are recorded as `lcm.recentBudget` and `lcm.layerBudget`, with the figures the settings panel shows (`lcm.measure`: the context, the fixed part, the reply reserve, the current recent and layer tokens, and whether a pair was scaled). With LCM off nothing here changes and the ST differential gate is byte-identical. Assembler version 25 pins this, the explicit `{{summary}}`, the summary note placed by its own switch, and the summary request without the preset.

The provider cache identity hashes the retained history in the final reusable prefix: a cut changes that prefix, while unchanged retained turns preserve its earlier checkpoints between cuts. A newly appended endpoint can still be cold and stagger even when its shorter prefix is warm.

The start never moves back on ordinary sends. Restoration requires a raised maximum or target, a different preset/model identity, or a durable reduction before the recorded start; the entire earlier suffix must then fit the target. Changes confined to retained messages do not unlock restoration. This prevents a skipped interval note from restoring dropped history only for the next due note to cut it again. The same slot-based boundary can later be supplied by LCM coverage: its proposed cursor is the slot immediately before verbatim history (`spec/LCM.md` §2.1).

Notes and formatted summaries are never shortened to fit a context budget. Stable expansions count at their actual size. The cut state pins each note’s largest observed size and one shared allowance for unused capacity: at most 25% of the available input window or 1,024 tokens, further limited by the space left after full fixed content [P]. There is no per-note quarter-window charge. Notes allocate unused capacity in placement order; every candidate counts at least the full actual composition and at most that cost plus the shared allowance, including role framing, merging and post-processing. Optional capacity cannot make a fixed context fail. Skipped notes never expand or execute macro writes; literal skipped text can seed its size observation, while a first unseen macro expansion is measured when due.

When an expansion exceeds its pinned size observation, its full text updates that observation, example admission and the target floor are recalculated, and history is cut again to the target. This is an explicit note-budget change, recorded as `historyMove: budget` when the maximum itself was not reached; the cache estimate explains it. A smaller or skipped expansion retains the observation, so the next occurrence of the same large note does not manufacture another budget change. Ordinary sends still cannot move the start backward. Observations reset on configuration changes; pinned source inputs reproduce them exactly on replay. Only full actual fixed content exceeding the maximum fails assembly, with an error naming full notes, fixed context and the response reserve. The lore tail allowance remains independent of activation.

`StartRollout` extracts the start and generation depth into three rollout columns (migration 0021). `rollouts_history(chat_id, history_depth DESC, id DESC)` supplies candidates; a single materialized parent walk finds the nearest ancestor generation whose start remains on the path. An empty candidate set exits without walking. Sibling generations and abandoned swipes never restart the walk: the 10,001-slot worker regression reads exactly 10,000 ancestry edges with 0, 100 or 500 unrelated candidates, about 26–31 ms in isolated samples (formerly over five million reads at 500). The test checks the index plan and measured work, independently of host timing. At 100,000 messages, assembly measured 71.3 ms without a prior start and 62.6 ms with the indexed start (five-run medians).

**Inserted messages** (spec/MESSAGE-INSERT.md §3.2, unit 4). In a fork that places insertions, the sources read its display path (`display` in `sqlSources`, `displayThrough` in `db/displayPath.ts`), and everything the assembler calls a depth is a display position: a history entry's (`HistoryEntry.depth`; an inserted one also has `inserted`), an event's, the target's (`Target.depth`), the history start's and the LCM cursor's. Without insertions a position is the depth and every read is the structural one, so chats without insertions assemble as before, and the ST differential gate is unchanged. The target is placed on the path by the sources (`placeTarget`): a version at its position, a next reply after the leaf, or after the display end when the leaf is the fork's tip (an inserted message after the tip comes before an appended reply). When the message right before the target isn't its parent (an inserted message sits between them), `Target.historyLeaf` names it, and the stages read the history, its events, the history start and revision, the message count and the summary layer from it (`historyLeafOf`); `parentSlotId` and the snapshot's `leafSlotId` stay structural. `history(leaf, limit, after)` reads the last `limit` positions up to the leaf, after `after`'s, through `displaySlots` (the window's pieces driving one range of `slots_chain` each), with the same columns as the structural read: an inserted message's images, their per-fork exclusion (`image_exclude` on its original version) and their estimates are read like any message's. `older` is the slot at the position before the window. The macros that number messages count positions (the omitted count is the first entry's position less the cursor's). `historyStart` translates the recorded start (`previousStartIn` in `db/historyStart.ts`): the candidate walk stays structural, and the start's depth becomes its position on the display path through the leaf (a start at the target: its rollout's message); `rollouts.history_start_depth` is recorded as the start slot's depth (`generationWrites.ts`), since the snapshot's is a position. `historyRevision` counts the fork's reductions at positions before the boundary (`displayHistoryRevision`: the depth index bounds the changes read, since a position is never below its slot's depth, and each is placed among the pieces), so deleting or hiding an inserted message before the start is a reduction there; an insertion writes no `history_changes` row. So an insertion before the start leaves the start, the revision and the cached prefix as they were, and one after it cuts nothing and changes the prefix from the inserted message on. The warmth estimate names it ("Message N was inserted.", when a message the next request reads, marked `inserted`, comes before the first message that moved), and the inspector numbers messages by the positions the snapshot pinned. Captured requests and their replays are unchanged. Not yet: an inserted message's own rollout isn't a candidate for later replies' history start (the walk is structural; a skipped candidate costs a fresh cut). LCM (§16 "Inserted messages") gives the layer's cursor, summaries' and failed ranges' `from` and `to` as display positions in a fork that shows insertions, so the stages compare positions with positions: `historyOmitted`, the retained-history minimum and the omitted ranges, the failure markers and the image omission locations. A summary request's `summaryBefore` and a condensation's cursor are positions too, and since `history` reads strictly after `after`'s position, a cursor on an inserted message, which isn't a structural ancestor of the leaf, fences the history. `NextContext.summarizedThrough` and `historyStart.depth` are positions, which the message list compares with each entry's `position` (§16 "Client data"). `test/lcmInsertRequests.test.ts` runs real summary requests through the summarizer and the mock provider: inside a range, with the cursor on an inserted message (and one inserted below it, raw), at a seam, above the first message, condensations over and across insertions, and a failed range holding an inserted message. Each request renders its whole chunk or parts in display order and completes, and the next reply sends the summaries, then the raw history after them in display order. The insertion-path target also pins `displayPositions`, so macro message arrays retain the positions of summarized history and deleted gaps; absent on paths without insertions and older snapshots, it preserves their numbering and replay keys. A next-context preview at a null structural tip still reads the fork’s above-first insertions. `test/insertionAssemblyReview.test.ts` covers that empty-tip preview, regeneration after an insertion, bounded event inheritance at X, raw history immediately after a summary cursor and a byte-identical synthetic request captured from master’s assembler version 38. `test/steppedHistory.test.ts` ("stepped history with inserted messages", over the database), `test/forkStateEvents.test.ts`, `test/inspector.test.ts` and `test/imageExclusion.test.ts` cover it.

`history_changes` holds sparse reductions keyed by slot, fork and sequence (`fork_id`, `''` for every fork). Earlier entries stay readable at descendant lineage bounds after further parent reductions. A lineage compaction copies the inherited per-slot totals into the new fork. Local deletion marks do not also increment a global counter; explicit global purges do. Worker-side triggers count deletions and hiding; content and selection writers compare JavaScript UTF-16 lengths in the same transaction to count shortening, including emoji and embedded NULs. Ordinary appends do not enter the index. `historyRevision` sums only changes on the requested path before the boundary, with an indexed empty-set exit. The cut state stores the budget, configuration digest, admitted example count and reduction revision. `SourcePins.historyStart` and `historyRevisions` pin the reads, including null/zero, so replay ignores later changes. Worker tests replay every recorded generation after interval sends, branch changes, pre-boundary reductions and raised budgets; unit tests compare complete prefixes across ST, concat and swap-tail layouts and four post-processing modes.

**Note slots.** Notes support explicit `context_start`, `tail_start` and `tail_end` sections as well as existing card sections, before/after-main placement and depth injections. The tail starts after history and an adjacent newest-message marker, or directly after history when newest was moved later. Several notes in one slot keep their order (rank, then id). Tail-end notes precede generation control directives; a newest-message marker moved after the tail follows those directives. Legacy `tail` remains ST depth zero, preserving imported behavior. The editor lists active sent notes at those positions, including their actual depths.

**The snapshot** (`types.ts`, DATA-MODEL §7.1, invariant 6) holds:
- the assembler semantics version (`ASSEMBLER_VERSION`, currently 39; absent means 0)
- what it was built from: chat, slot, version, the leaf, the clock and time zone, type, speaker, mode, the preset's ref, revision and scope, each card's ref and revision, the model
- the request and its resulting history start and movement reason
- every piece with its source and the messages it went into (a card block's fields included)
- the enabled preset prompts their condition or their ST triggers left out this turn (`skippedPrompts`, by preset source with the condition, or with the triggers it is sent for), for the inspector's "not sent" list; absent in older snapshots and when none were
- the issues the user should see: a prefill dropped, a piece left out, history or examples cut by the budget
- the values drawn per piece, and the variable writes

- what the sources read, pinned (`SourcePins`): the state events by id, the path's versions with their amend counts (and the rest of what was read of them, but their text), and each card and note read by revision, keyed by what it asked for. Revisions and state events never change, and an amended version keeps its earlier texts (`version_history`), so the pins hold everything else the context depended on.

`RebuildContext` checks the recorded assembler version first [P: the assembler code is an explicit input]. The integer constant is bumped whenever assembly output for the same inputs changes; version 1 was the first bump; version 2 fixes note expansion dependencies and skipped-note composition; version 3 moves cache boundaries for variable readers after intermittent notes; version 4 activates and places lorebooks; version 5 bounds lazy previews, reserves stable tail capacity, resolves primary sources, pins path counts and fixes ST example ordering/empty wrappers; version 6 makes frozen macro work replay identically, pins legacy import filenames and caps the tail reservation by eligible stored content; version 7 records historical note activation and interval decisions on source pieces; version 8 pins stepped history state and counts every reserved consumer toward both thresholds; version 9 pins durable restoration inputs and example admission; version 10 preserves full note text, shares unused note capacity and pins observed expansion sizes; version 11 adds the LCM layer and cursor; version 12 adds frozen summary requests; version 13 fixes summary placement and framing; version 14 pins triggering reply prefixes and integrates LCM with stepped history and historical cache lifetimes; version 15 loads the persona's notes, places notes at the author's note's top and bottom and with a card's definition, and sends a persona placed by its note instead of by the persona-description marker; version 16 preserves persona card-section notes in our layouts and treats an activated interval-zero author's note as off for attachments; version 17 preserves stepped raw-history starts with LCM and omits zero-content lineage from rendered summary prose; version 18 expands the summarization instruction’s `{{words}}` with the job’s target; version 19 budgets and pins failed-range markers after raw history overflows; version 22 uses CCv3 nickname fallback for the model’s card names throughout assembly; version 23 takes generation settings from the model and global selections rather than the prompt preset; version 24 separates the newest-message marker, adds explicit note slots, and records root pieces’ prompt slots for editor annotations; version 25 keeps several notes in one slot in their order and bounds the default response length by the context size in use and, for a schema continuation, by the model's output limit less the source prefix; the RP loop's version 26 sent preset prompts by their conditions with `{{speaker}}` and `{{direction}}`, replaced the tail directive and the verbatim system direction with the imported group nudge, impersonation and direction prompts, and closed a request that would end on the model's side with a user turn; the assembly unit's version 26 removed ST-compatible mode for the one assembly path (per-field joins, the Definitions marker, the speaker's card overrides, global books, lorebook placement by the chat's setting, pinned lore and the breakpoint priority); version 27 combines both units' version 26 changes on one path (conditioned prompts with the per-field joins, the Definitions marker, the card overrides and lorebook placement); the summarization unit's version 26 added the summarization pairs, the explicit `{{summary}}`, summary notes placed by their switch alone, and summary requests without the preset; **version 28 combines it with version 27** (the pairs and the explicit `{{summary}}` on the one assembly path, where the layer always takes the `<summaries>` block and ST mode's layer placement is gone); the notes unit's version 26 sent notes by their conditions instead of intervals, added the lorebook scan switch, `on_by_default` in place of the first-note rule, linked shared notes and what rides on a switched-off author's note; **version 29 combines it with version 28** (notes' conditions, links and `on_by_default` on the one assembly path, where the imported summary note is a chat note switched on like any other and `{{summary}}` reads only one that is on and due, and "with the definitions" stays after the main prompt, owner question 2, whatever unit 3's Definitions marker does with the cards). Version 30 is the RP loop of PHASE1-DECISIONS §2: the closing user turn right after the last chat message, and only without a user-role message after it; ST triggers as a filter beside conditions; the speaking card's prompts on impersonation turns; departed speakers naming their messages; version 30 also, from the notes fix batch, wraps the persona's part like every cast card's and puts each card at the Definitions marker in that wrapper, places a card's note "with the definitions" inside its card's part (volatile when conditioned), never leaves a persona's description out for a note's kind or title, and reads ST's Character's Note from a card's in-chat notes that are on; version 30 also, from the summarization fix batch, sends each summary as its own message before the history, `{{summary}}` reading only the summary notes (DESIGN §8, owner 2026-10-28), summary requests without notes unless the chat includes them, and the summary length in words. Version 31 makes Main a permanent slot, adding it first and off when an older preset lacks it, so notes beside that empty slot are sent. Version 32 resolves chat-scoped shared-note/snippet links through the viewed fork’s revision binding. Version 34 enforces one selected persona and scalar `{{user}}` expansion, retaining historical requests from earlier versions without replaying them through the new fold.

Version 38 adds image token budgeting, explicit oversized-newest/chunk failures and image content/detail cache digests. Version 39 assembles a fork's display path by position (§15 "Inserted messages"); chats without insertions assemble byte for byte as in version 38. Version 35 combines the end-of-request instruction with version 34’s single-persona bindings: the turn and direction remain one separate final user message after post-processing; summary instructions also use the final user message with or without the preset; the empty-text fallback checks the actual final role, including system prompts. Raw summary cache markers and estimates cover continuity only. The built-in preset and new prompt presets use the balanced end-of-request defaults. End-text macros expand after other text and before prefill, preventing end writes from changing the prefix.

Version 36 makes the explicit `author_note.set` choice override an imported author-note kind and treats an explicit null as no author's note, so old requests keep their recorded assembly rather than replaying through the changed precedence.

Version 37 adds the registered `images` stage: an image the user took out of the context in a fork, or one a rule leaves out, is left out of the request with its reason recorded.

An older snapshot returns `EarlierAssemblerVersion` with its version, the current version and the original snapshot intact as `storedSnapshot`, without attempting replay or claiming missing chat data. The stored request remains available internally; the public inspector requires a sanitized final capture. A current-version snapshot returns `Rebuilt` with `snapshot` (callers must narrow the tag to access either field) and builds again from its pins, its inputs (the model as resolved, the preset's revision), its clock and the values drawn, rather than from the chat as it is now: after the user amends a message, a card or the preset moves on, or a state event is added, it still gives the same snapshot (`test/assemblyStorage.test.ts`). A card, note or preset in the bin still rebuilds, since its revisions are kept and never change; it fails, with the reason, only when something pinned was deleted for good. Only the chat's owner can rebuild it: every pinned read is the owner's. A context whose prompt regex scripts ran out of time can come back different where the deadline fell; lore activation’s emergency result is pinned.

**Regex scripts** (`regex.ts`, §9). Once the user approved the preset's scripts (`regex_approved`), its prompt-only ones run on the chat as ST runs them when it builds a prompt: on the user's or the model's side, within their depths, in order, before names are added. A pattern comes from a file, and `(a+)+$` takes exponential time on some inputs with a backtracking engine, so every script runs on V8's linear-time engine: the `l` flag, with `--enable-experimental-regexp-engine` set when the module loads, for the whole process (V8 calls flags set after startup unsupported; Node 24.15 honors it in the main thread and in workers). That engine takes no `i` or `u`, so a case-insensitive pattern is rewritten with both cases of each ASCII letter, in classes and ranges too (`caseless`, tested against V8's own `i` on sample patterns), and `u` is dropped unless the pattern uses `\p{…}` or `\u{…}`. What the engine refuses (backreferences, lookaheads, nested counted repeats) is left out and reported, never run. The linear engine's time still grows with the pattern times the text, and no count of that predicts it (a review measured a 902-character nested pattern over 43,000 characters at over 8 s), so the scripts run with a deadline: each batch of messages is a `vm` script with a timeout, which V8 enforces by stopping whatever runs, a regex included, in the main thread or a worker. Matches are found with `exec` in a loop rather than `String.prototype.replace`: V8 13.6's `replace` with a function loops forever, where no timeout reaches, on an empty match of a global linear-engine pattern with 32 or more groups. A context gets 200 ms in all; past that the rest of its messages go without scripts, and the issue says so. Every character the scripts make, replaced or copied, is charged to 8 million per context, so neither a long replacement nor a script copying long messages can grow without bound; a script that runs out, or throws, is skipped for that message and reported, never a failed assembly. A message a script was skipped for, for time or output, is volatile for the cache breakpoints (below): what ran out depends on the messages read before it, newest first. Each context also allows at most 100 scripts and patterns of 2,000 characters after the rewrite. A context built again can differ from its snapshot only where the deadline fell, which a busy host moves: the issue is recorded either way. The rewrite only ever matches less than `i` would: non-ASCII letters, letters in escapes, and ranges between cases match as written. Without `u`, a character class or `.` sees an emoji as two characters. V8's fallback to the linear engine after too much backtracking was tried first: it skips every pattern with an `i` or `u` flag, and a review measured `(a+)+$` with `i` taking 6.7 s on 26 characters.

**Lorebook activation** (`loreStages.ts`, `lorebook.ts`). Registered stages run inside the assembler’s reader transaction. Books come from `lorebook.attach`/`detach` on the selected path, the user's global books (below) the chat didn't turn off (`lorebook.global` with `on: false`; one also attached is read once), and the cast’s embedded `character_book`s, personas included. Attached books use the ref head; its id, entity and revision are pinned, including an empty read. A card’s bound primary attachment replaces its embedded book; the embedded book is the fallback when the primary is unavailable, unless the chat turned it off (`lorebook.embedded` with `on: false`, which an ST import writes where ST never sent it, §13, and the chat settings' "<name>'s own lorebook" switch writes through `ChangeCast`'s `CardLorebook`). With no such event it's on. Earlier imports without a binding resolve `extensions.world` against the retained import filename (`entity_refs.source_path`, basename without `.json`), never the mutable document name or label. Variants inherit the upstream import identity. The resolved filename is pinned with each book so replay does not consult mutable ref metadata. No content-based deduplication is used. The association comes from pinned state/card/book revisions, and embedded fallbacks use the card’s pinned revision. Rebuilds read those revisions even after edits, detaches or binning. Semantics version is **7**; earlier snapshots are retained without running this assembler.

ST folder imports attach chat-bound, primary character and extra character books, and mark ST's `globalSelect` books global (§13); persona-bound book selection has no representation. **Global books** are a per-account list of the user's lorebook refs in `user_settings` (`lorebooks.global`), read and written through reader and writer ops (`GetGlobalLorebooks`, `SetGlobalLorebook`, which checks the ref is a live library lorebook of the user's, in one transaction); the global refs read are pinned in `SourcePins.globalLorebooks`. **The Lorebooks tab** (client `features/chat/LorebooksTab.tsx`) lists the characters' books (`CardLorebook`), the books added to this chat (`Lorebook`, with Add from the library and "Use in every chat") and the global books (`GlobalLorebook`, per chat), each with its switch, and at the top the "Only below the history" switch (`LoreBelowHistory`) with a popover saying why and how. Every switch is a state event written at the displayed leaf through `ChangeCast`; a lorebook's library page marks it global. Cross-user probes cover the new events (`stateKeys.test.ts`) and ops (`isolation.test.ts`).

The scan uses eligible path messages newest first, with speaker names and approved prompt regex transformations. Hidden, failed and empty messages do not enter it; swipes already end their path before the replaced reply. History loading supplies the greatest requested scan depth independently of the request budget (at most 1,000 messages). Message-count delays use a separate count of eligible messages on the full path, pinned independently of that bounded window; edits, hidden messages and branches cannot change it on rebuild. Templates are resolved against their position as usual. Description opt-ins read the first character and persona, including the first character’s active Character’s Note. Notes have no retained `scan` flag, so general scannable extension injections are left out. Resolved templates run their prompt scripts before lore scanning; composition reuses the result.

[P] Defaults follow a fresh ST install: depth 2, case-insensitive, whole words, recursion on, and a lore allowance of 25% of the assembler’s available context budget; the estimator counts four characters per token. Book depth, recursion and absolute budget overrides apply as the matcher defines below. ST global scan preferences and insertion strategy have no runtime representation. Probability has its own deterministic RNG hashed from the pinned target, time, book/card revisions and read message identities/text; it consumes no macro RNG. Keys and descriptions use read-only macro previews; content is previewed lazily only after trigger, delay, key, secondary, group and probability gates, and an already-exhausted budget. All previews share a deterministic 4,000,000-unit work allowance, charged before allocating repeat output or parsing/hash-reading input. Exhaustion leaves candidate content out with an issue. Logical handler work declared by a macro (`MacroSpec.work`, e.g. dice count for `roll`) is charged from its arguments before choosing a fresh or frozen value, so preview-limit outcomes replay exactly without rerolling. Draws are frozen by book/uid/field, but variable writes do not persist or affect other pieces. This avoids skipped entries writing chat state; it differs from ST’s content macros. If the emergency regex deadline fires, the partial matcher result and its content-preview draws/issues are retained in the snapshot and reused by RebuildContext, so the timeout does not change activation on replay.

**With "only below the history" off** (`lorebook.below_history` with `on: false`; absent is on, DESIGN §1.3), entries go where their positions say, as ST places them: at `worldInfoBefore`/`worldInfoAfter` with the preset’s `wi_format`, at depth with their role, around the active author’s note, and before/after example dialogues. Blocks and `em_bottom` reverse descending admissions, including equal-order ties; `em_top` keeps descending admissions because ST prepends those examples. Empty joined lore skips `wi_format` while retaining activation provenance. Outlets have no consumer and produce an assembly issue. Author-note entries are unplaced when that note is off. World Info regex postprocessing and `placement_override` are not implemented.

**Pins.** With the setting off, entries placed above `chatHistory` in the preset's order (`before_char`/`after_char` at markers before it, `em_top`/`em_bottom` when `dialogueExamples` is before it) are recorded in the cut state as `pinnedLore` (book, revision, uid). The next generation on the path loads them when its configuration digest is unchanged and keeps them activated and placed (activation `why: pinned`), whether or not they match again; a new match above the history is added at once and pinned. When the history's start moves (a cut, a restore, LCM's cursor: the start's depth or the movement changes), entries kept only by their pin are dropped (their piece records `pin released`) and what matched this turn is pinned in their place; the cut chosen with them stays valid, as the context only shrinks. A changed configuration (a book switched, an entry edited, the preset, the setting) loads no pins. Entries after the history are never pinned and are evaluated every turn, as in ST; author's-note entries ride on that note and aren't pinned, so an author's note outside the chat is `volatile` whenever an enabled entry could ride on it. From the first place pinned lore can go, the prefix's messages are `pinned` (an empty boundary keeps that even on a turn with no entry there). The pins live in the snapshot's `historyStart.state`, which `SourcePins.historyStart` pins, so `RebuildContext` replays them exactly (`test/lorePins.test.ts`).

**Only below the history** (on, the default): **all** activated entries, constants included, whatever their position (depth entries too), go in one system block at the start of the tail: after `chatHistory`, and after `newestMessage` when that is right after it (G's `tail_start` slot), before the tail's notes, in descending admission order with stable input ties. That block is volatile and follows every cache breakpoint. History and examples reserve the smaller of 25% of available context and the estimated joined size of eligible stored content (enabled constants and entries with primary keys), including one lore message’s overhead. Empty or ineligible books reserve zero. This calculation never expands content or depends on this turn’s matches or draws, so the allowance is identical whether any entry activates or not. Macro expansion beyond that stored-text allowance is subject to the same overflow cut. An entry that would exceed it is skipped with a budget issue, even with `ignore_budget`; it cannot borrow from history. Thus activation changes neither retained history/examples nor the cached prefix. With the setting off, ST's budgeting applies. Constants stay there because probability, groups and budgets can make them change too. Retention until a compaction boundary remains deferred with LCM; activation is evaluated each assembly.

Measured alone on this host (2026-10-12), the real worker assembled a synthetic 1,000-message path with 500 entries in 101.3 ms cold and 41.9–66.2 ms warm (median 44.4 ms); `uptime`: 46 days, 13:35, load averages 24.48, 26.21, 21.71. The probe is in `assemblyStorage.test.ts`. The review’s 887 KB, 1,000-entry unmatched macro book used about 1.0 MB additional heap in 54.8 ms (previously 203 MB); the preallocation regression also verifies that a repeat exceeding the shared allowance never allocates its output.

Every activated and skipped entry has a `lorebook` source tag naming book/revision/uid, status, recursion pass and matched primary/secondary keys or skip reason. Skipped pieces have no text or request-message indices. Matcher issues (construct and suggested regex fix, unsupported controls, work/recursion limits) and exhausted token allowances become `lorebook_issue`s in the inspector.

The pure matcher takes identified books, messages newest first, descriptions, scannable injections, settings, a replayable random source and a token counter. The caller resolves names, keys and descriptions, supplies the full path count separately, and optionally supplies a lazy content-preview callback for candidate admission. The scan window is independent of final request budgeting. Results identify the book, matched keys, recursion pass, skipped entries and issues. The initial pass is numbered 0. The [ST 1.19.0 reference](spec/ST-WORLD-INFO.md) has 166 numbered compatibility tests. Additional synthetic tests cover safety limits and review findings.

Enabled constants need no keys. Other enabled entries need a primary match. Selective entries then apply one of four secondary conditions: any match, every match, no match, or not every match. Trigger and message-count gates apply before matching. Plain keys use literal string search. Whole-word mode requires that neither neighbor be an ASCII letter, digit or underscore, as ST does. Keys containing whitespace use substring matching. Case sensitivity defaults to false and whole-word matching defaults to true. Only ST's `/pattern/flags` syntax invokes regex matching; `use_regex` is ignored.

Scan depth comes from the entry, then the book, then settings, then defaults to 2. Messages are trimmed and capped at 1,000. The buffer preserves ST's sentinels and interior empty slots, but skips trailing empty strings. Selected descriptions and scannable injections follow the messages. Only requested message depths are prepared. Each depth's chat text is built once. Completed buffers and their lowercase forms are reused for entries with the same depth and description flags within a pass. ASCII whole-word keys use a word index built once per buffer and case mode. Case-sensitive regexes such as `/\bcat\b/` share that index when they are not sticky. Exact non-sticky patterns such as `/(?:cat)/` use substring search when their entire rewritten body contains only ASCII letters, digits or underscores. Their results, and other regex results, are cached per buffer. Duplicate key slots still contribute separately to group scores. Unscored constants need no buffer.

Admitted content can activate further entries on later passes. Recursion defaults to enabled and honors book overrides and the exclude/prevent flags. Delayed recursion levels are pass thresholds; they never force recursion when it is disabled. Recursion defaults to 16 passes and cannot exceed 64. Probability uses the supplied random source and ST's inclusive comparison, so probability 0 passes an exact-zero draw. A failed draw is not retried.

A group admits at most one entry. Scores count matching key slots, including empty slots. Positive secondary logic adds secondary matches whenever primary slots exist, even if none of those primary slots matched.
