Planning splits around that completed coverage independently of the selectable prefix cursor, so a failed earlier chunk cannot cause repeated paid summaries of later chunks. Claims order interactive, idle, then background by age, admit two processing jobs per user, and serialize a chat's L1 jobs in path order. Attempts count at claim. Migration `0024_summary_attempts` adds a monotonically increasing claim epoch, rollout binding and clipping cap. Release and startup reset refund attempts without refunding epochs, fencing late callbacks. The scheduler records each acknowledged claim in a live-attempt registry before forking its task and removes it only after task cleanup. Before claiming again, owner-scoped `ReconcileSummaryJobs` refunds processing rows absent from that registry and closes their rollouts. Claiming and reconciliation run serially in the single instance-locked scheduler; a lost worker acknowledgement is recovered after respawn without resetting still-running tasks. Job-specific operations require the acting owner; reset is internal and has no RPC. Failure and release can clean up an owning binned chat, so deletion during a request cannot strand a processing job. Completion compares inputs with the frozen request before the atomic summary write. Concurrent edits may make the result stale but cannot change its pins. Failures close rollouts, retry twice, then leave a failed job; original messages remain available. Terminal failures become failed summary rows (below). Each provider call has a ledger row, rolled up into its summary rollout.

Job reads use the range's last slot when storage has no planning leaf. Planning, reconciliation and claim failures, including RPC schema defects, are isolated to that operation and logged; they do not stop the scheduler. Failed reconciliation skips that user's claims until the next poll. A committed claim whose acknowledgement cannot be decoded is recovered by the existing reconciliation fence. Interruptions still stop work normally. `lcmPool.test.ts` exercises the nullable leaf, each scheduler boundary, lost acknowledgement recovery and a job defect followed by a successful retry.

**Summary requests.** Registered assembler stages run in `quiet` mode, with summarizer model/preset bindings falling back to narrator bindings and the path cut at the chunk end. Earlier coverage supplies continuity; exactly the chunk's effective inputs supply material. Assembly rejects trimmed chunks. Individual oversized messages are clipped at 1.5 times the planned chunk size with a marker. Direct output is the default. The instruction resolves the chat's `tail`, the account default's, then the built-in (`BUILTIN_SUMMARY_INSTRUCTION`, the owner's Sum2 request made neutral: pause the roleplay, summarize the story so far, start where an existing summary stopped, keep language, voice, names, events, relationships and unresolved threads, at most `{{words}}` words). `{{words}}`, and a condensation's "at most N words", are the summary length in tokens as words, about 0.75 a token (`summaryWords`: 600 tokens ask for 450 words), since models follow word counts better. The chat's notes are sent only when `withNotes` resolves on (off by default): the notes stage then gates none, otherwise it passes no notes at all, since the current notes may tell of events after the chunk. Only reply text becomes the summary. Prefill, the end-of-request message (§15 "Who writes, and the direction") and the one-turn direction are suppressed: the instruction takes the end-of-request place, the request's last message, and no fallback turn is added. Output is capped at target times 2.5 and reduced when needed to fit the preserved prefix and whole chunk; clean length stops are clipped at sentence boundaries and saved as truncated. After retries, a nonempty partial response from the final transport-failed attempt can also be saved as truncated, after output checks. Refused or aborted responses are never salvaged, and drafts from earlier attempts are not reused across changed input pins.

The instruction is always one final user-role message (DESIGN §2.2), for both preset modes and both direct/tool styles; `midChatSystem` does not change that role. Its inspector piece is `lcm:instruction`. Requests retain prefix and layer cache markers and remove the history marker. Migration `0025_summary_prefix` pins the triggering reply’s assembled prefix at enqueue, by its explicit version id even if a selection changes before coalesced planning runs; startup and overflow planning fall back to the closest selected reply on the path. Summary requests reuse its frozen macro values, selected example messages, source pieces and mapping decisions when model and preset identities agree. The output allowance is applied after prefix selection. Different model or preset bindings assemble independently and are treated as cold when the actual prefix differs. Current snapshots retain that prefix alongside quiet mode, frozen tail, style, clipping cap and version pins for exact replay. LCM-off ST assembly remains under the unchanged differential oracle.

*Without the preset* (`withPreset: false`, ST's Raw; `rawSummary` in `lcm/request.ts`). The request is built from the same assembly, so the chunk is exactly the one the full-preset path renders and clips: the earlier coverage as continuity, one user message per summary (`<summary level from to>`); then a level-1 job's messages in their roles, or a condensation's parts as one block; then one final user message carrying the instruction (its instruction names "the last N summaries" without "above"). Nothing of the preset, cards or notes is sent; prefill is dropped. It shares no prefix with the chat: one breakpoint after the continuity (`summary`), or no breakpoint when there is none, lets consecutive requests read the summaries back: the next chunk's request repeats those messages byte for byte and adds the new summary after them (a single continuity message would change with every summary, and provider caches match whole messages). Its inspector pieces keep their indices: the summaries and messages point at their new messages, the rest at none, and `lcm:instruction` names the instruction. The job snapshot records `withPreset: false`, and `RebuildContext` replays either mode through the same registered `summary-request` transform. Fresh jobs supply resolved instruction settings, target and condensation range before assembly; snapshots freeze the original transform inputs in `summaryAssembly` (job, instruction parameters and input samplers). Rebuilds replay those inputs before transforms and apply the same fixed context-budget cap afterward, so plugins can derive instructions and samplers without applying their own changes twice. Earlier snapshots without that envelope retain their frozen tail and sampler replay and their original shape. The backfill estimate counts the instruction as uncached input on every request. Output checks, refusal judging, truncation and `OpenLlmRequest`'s reservation are unchanged.

`summaryAssembly.test.ts` runs real generation and rebuild handlers in a reader worker with a synthetic scoped replacement of `summary-request`, for both preset modes. It checks job metadata in stages before transforms and pinned-prefix control, derives its output from the original instruction and samplers, and checks the outgoing generation request, exact snapshot rebuild (including source indexes and breakpoints), disablement and restoration. A scoped observer also exercises every assembler consumer: ordinary context, cache warmth, summary planning and assembly, backfill estimates, settings measurements and rebuilds. Existing summary settings, prefix, condensation and fork tests retain their behavioral assertions.

**Execution registries** (`execution.ts`, `chat/replyOutput.ts`, `lcm/strategies.ts`). `serverWith` provides and exposes a scoped `ExecutionRegistry` with four registries: reply orchestration (`reply`), reply output strategies (`reply`), summary execution strategies (`direct`, `tool`), and summary tool factories (`summary_tool`). Each built-in registers through the same scoped mechanism as plugins: replacing an ID changes execution, disabling it fails the generation/job with an unavailable error before a provider request, and scope exit restores the previous entry. The generator and summarizer capture the registry instance, then resolve entries for each generation/job attempt. Standalone service construction without this service builds the same defaults in its enclosing scope.

- A reply output factory creates a decoder per generation and optionally names its forced reply tool. The built-in emits text/reasoning and preserves tool-schema prefill validation and incremental prefix stripping. Rollout acquisition/finalization, provider attribution and version persistence remain in the generator and generation services.
- A summary strategy creates per-attempt state from the owned job and frozen snapshot, including chat/user and leaf position, chooses tool definitions, reads streaming text and interprets completed assistant turns. It returns candidate text and tool feedback; the summarizer retains provider execution, the eight-call bound, output checks, empty-output rejection, clipping, retry/salvage and fenced completion/failure writes. `direct` keeps streamed partial text for salvage and accepts final text. `tool` resolves `summary_tool` from the tool factory registry, using that factory's definition, draft reader and executor together. Draft state belongs to an attempt, never a registry entry.
- Strategies can supply an output-instruction function; tool factories can supply usage instructions. The built-in tool strategy uses its selected factory's instructions, or generic guidance to follow the offered tool's definition. A replacement tool is never told to call the old built-in name or schema. The summarizer captures strategies and a read-only view of tool factories together before assembly, and passes their instruction strings to `AssembleSummary`. Only data crosses the worker boundary. The registered summary transform applies them before budgeting and freezing the request; frozen transform inputs also drive exact rebuilds after the plugin scope closes. Built-in instruction bytes are unchanged. Direct worker callers omitting this optional catalog retain the built-in suffixes from `lcm/instructions.ts`.
- Reply orchestration (`chat/replyStrategy.ts`) owns the existing single provider turn, streaming preference, waiting-cache notification and output decoding. It receives the generation request (including fork), assembled generation, rollout, providers and per-generation decoder. Replacing `replyStrategies` changes the actual generated stream; rollout opening/closing, storage and admission stay in `ContextGenerator`.
- Worker policies are registries on `Assembler`: `leafPlanners` (`leaf`) groups raw messages into chunks, `maintenancePlanners` (`maintenance`) chooses repairs/condensations, `summaryInstructions` (`leaf`, `condensation`) renders base instructions, and `summaryStyles` (`settings`) selects the output strategy. Defaults preserve the existing midpoint/target, chunk/fork boundaries, maintenance algorithm and instruction text. Reader queries gather owned inputs and keep fixed limits; the writer still validates/enqueues/commits. Policy code never runs in a writer transaction. Missing policies fail with `AssemblyFailed` before provider execution.
- Trusted `AssemblyModules` install these worker policies using the same per-request scoped snapshot as stages, projections and transforms, in source and bundled workers. Planning and summary assembly install modules around the whole reader operation; nested assembly recognizes the same assembler and avoids installing a wrapper twice. Scope exit restores defaults, and subsequent requests/respawns receive the current module snapshot.
- Summary style is an open nonempty string (bounded to 200 characters); absent still selects `direct`. A plugin can persist another ID in `lcm.settings` and register its execution strategy, or replace the worker selector. The summarizer pins its strategy/tool view before assembly and supplies available output instructions; a missing selected strategy fails clearly before calling a provider. Rendered base instructions are frozen in `summaryAssembly.instruction.base`, so exact request rebuild remains possible after the instruction plugin unloads. Older snapshots without this field retain their previous rendering path.
- `Commands` (`commands.ts`) exposes scoped command descriptors (ID, description, input/output schemas and executor). The built-ins `history.search`, `history.rename` and `message.insert` (§3.8 `InsertMessage`) register the existing preview, rename and insertion workflows, and those RPC handlers dispatch through the registry. Commands resolve `CurrentUser` at execution and use the existing owned worker operations, atomic application, limits and invalidation. `makeCommand` validates input/output; typed RPC dispatch also validates the expected output and reports a disabled command as unavailable. Additional commands can register without changing these RPCs. This is a trusted command seam for a future agent runtime; no generic remote command endpoint, tool-offer UI or plugin loader is added.
- The client `features/chat/replyPolicy.ts` registry registers `round-robin`; `planReply` dispatches through it for both the composer's preview and submitted reply speaker/flag. Disabling it posts messages with `reply: false`, including queued sends. An explicit one-turn reply choice still works. The built-in registers the existing unnamed-reply fallback for chats without characters; plugins may supply their own or decline an automatic reply. Muting and guidance retain their existing behavior. As with other client registries, plugins register before rendering; registration changes do not themselves trigger React renders. Client registrations use undo disposers, which can be tied to a plugin scope.
- `planningRegistry.test.ts` exercises planning overrides in source/bundled readers, maintenance suppression/restoration, registered leaf/condensation instructions and replay after unload, selected and persisted additional styles, reply orchestration and history command RPC dispatch. `executionRegistry.test.ts` exercises scoped replacements through persisted generations and durable summary jobs, unavailable failures and restoration, both summary request modes, actual replacement tool calls and exact request rebuilds. `summaryStrategies.test.ts` covers replaced tool definitions/executors, unknown tool rejection, disable/restore and isolated drafts; `client/test/replyPolicy.test.ts` and `session.test.ts` cover scoped choice replacement, disable/restore and immediate/queued sends without an unwanted unnamed generation.

The selectable tool style exposes only `summary_tool`: create, view, unique replacement, line insertion and finalize. Edits are atomic and bounded by the output ceiling. Errors return feedback for correction. Only a nonempty created draft becomes the summary; the loop accepts explicit finalization or a clean ending with a draft, as reference-harness does, and is bounded to eight provider calls. Tools have no filesystem, code-execution or chat-mutation access. Provider metadata rejects hard refusals; the `SummaryChecks` registry holds the refusal judge (below). No refusal text heuristics are used.

**Refusals and failed summaries** (slice 4; `decision/RefusalJudge.ts`, `lcm/OutputChecks.ts`, `db/lcmFailures.ts`). There are exactly two kinds of refusal. A hard refusal is the provider's own signal: a refusal stop reason or a category on it, which the provider layer already classifies as a `ProviderError` with reason `refused`. A soft refusal is decided only by a judge: a decision model (DESIGN §7.6) chosen as `judge` in the user's generation defaults, which reads the output and answers one word, `REFUSED` or `OK` (a request with purpose `decision` in the ledger, temperature 0). Nothing else looks at an output: no phrase list, pattern or length rule. With no judge chosen, outputs are unjudged and only hard refusals exist. The judge sees the output and, for a summary, the instruction it answered, never the chunk. `RefusalJudge` registers itself in the `SummaryChecks` registry; `serverWith` wires it, so the summarizer holds no judge of its own.

For a summary, a check that refuses or cannot decide (the judge's request failed, timed out after 60 s, or its answer was not one of the two words) fails the attempt: the output is not saved and not kept as a draft, so no `generated` revision ever holds a refused output. A failed attempt retries up to `max_retries`; then, if the user set `generation.lcm.fallback`, the job moves once to that model (`model_json` over the chat's summarizer binding, `fallback_used`) for one more attempt; then it ends. A transport failure on the last attempt can still salvage a partial summary as before, after the checks. Ending writes, in the same transaction as the job's failure, a summary row with status `failed`: its inputs (or children), an empty revision, and its error as JSON (`class`, `message`, and for refusals `kind` hard or soft and the provider's category). A failed row is never selected and never replaces messages that fit. Its range is held from the planner while its inputs are unchanged (`failedHold`); an edit or swipe inside the range makes it stale and the next planning pass tries again. A failed row is never a child. Retry and Retry with… are rerun requests (`SetSummaryRerun`, with an optional model; below): the click records a row and nothing more, and the next reply's planning makes the job. The failed row stays, and the retry is a new row.

For a chat version, a finished model reply is judged in the background when a judge is chosen. A refusal verdict sets `versions.mark` to `refused` (migration `0027_refusals`) and publishes the version; the text stays, and Regenerate (with an optional `model`, Retry with…) is the retry. A judge that fails marks nothing. A hard refusal remains a failed version with class `refused`. The client shows a "refused" badge and, on a refused or provider-refused reply, a "Retry with…" model picker. The judge and the fallback summarizer are chosen in the chat settings dialog (user-wide, beside the summaries switch). The model test drives the real summarizer against a scripted provider and a fake judge, and plants hard and soft refusals; it asserts that no refusal is saved, no failed row is ever selected, and that a deliberately removed judge check is caught.

**Condensation, absorption and stale summaries** (slice 5; `lcm/maintenance.ts` is the pure planner, `db/lcmMaintain.ts` loads its input, `PlanSummaries` and `Summarizer` use it). It follows reference-harness's fanout condensation and budget-driven eager condensation, and adds what edits under summaries need. Knobs in `lcm.settings`: `fanout` (default 5) and `condenseTarget` (800 tokens). All of it considers only the summaries whose chain ends on the path being planned.

- *Ordinary condensation.* The pool at a level is the fresh, selectable summaries (not superseded, not in a batch with live jobs) that no valid summary lists as a part and no live job has claimed; for one range only the best (fresh, higher level, newer) counts. Adjacent pool members form runs; a run is cut into groups of `fanout` from its oldest end, leftovers wait, a gap or a failed range splits a run, and a group whose range a live job covers, a standing failure holds, or a fresh summary of that level or above already covers is skipped. A hand-written summary is a part like any other. A condensation is a level n+1 job whose ordered parts are stored on the job (`EnqueueCondensations` rechecks them: the chat's, below the level, each following the one before, the last on the path; it skips a range a live job of that level covers).
- *Budget.* When the layer's rendered cost passes its maximum (the chat's summary block pair, fitted to the context, in the snapshot's `lcm.layerBudget`), or stays above the target after a budget job finished (job column `eager`, migration `0028_condensation`), and nothing else is live or ordinary, at most one budget job is planned. Oldest run first, never the run holding the newest summary: absorb the run into the generated fresh parent beside it, the left one first (the parent's own parts, then the oldest run members that fit twice `fanout` parts; or the newest members then the parts, for a right parent), else make the next level from the oldest `min(run, fanout)` of a run of at least two below the highest level. Either must be worth twice the condensation target. `CompleteSummary` supersedes the old parent and the run members it absorbed; the parent's original parts stay as they were.
- *The request.* A condensation is input-addressed: the layer before its first part (selected as for a level-1 job) followed by exactly its parts at their selected revisions, cut after the last, with no history and a tail naming them ("Condense the last 3 summaries above (messages 1 to 6) ..."). The rendered tail of the layer must be the declared parts and revisions or the job fails before the request; the snapshot records the parts and completion compares them with the job's. Refusal checks, retries, the fallback and failed rows apply as for level 1; a failed condensation leaves its parts selected and holds its range.
- *Re-summarizing.* A stale summary is *used* when it is in the layer or has a descendant in it. Planning for a reply (never an edit, swipe, startup or a finished job) writes a stale used level-1 summary again over the same chain with the current inputs, once per summary: unless a live job covers its range, a standing failure holds it, a fresh summary of its range and level or above exists, or nothing in its range has content. A stale used condensation is written again, by a reply's pass or by a finished job's, over the best fresh summary of each of its parts' ranges, once every part has one; stale parts below it are written again first, so a cascade is one job per affected summary. The stale rows stay; a swipe back makes them fresh again. A summary whose selected revision is not `generated` (the user's, or imported) is never replaced: it and anything built over it are `flagged` in the plan (`SummaryPlan.flagged`), and its text stays in the context marked stale. Migration `0031_lcm_reply_maintenance` persists an episode per reply path: signatures of effective inputs and recursively selected summary revisions, plus the explicit requests and their generated ancestors. It is saved before enqueue, and covers the entire set beyond the four-job cap. Completion passes continue that frozen set, including remaining L1 refreshes, but cannot repair an input or revision edited after the authorizing reply. New fresh generated results may join ordinary condensation. Startup continues persisted episodes as well as pending jobs.
- *Rerun requests.* A row in `summary_reruns` (the panel's Re-summarize and Retry; migration `0029_rerun_models` adds the model for Retry with…) means "redo this with the next reply". Only a reply's pass authorizes it; completion can consume the requests frozen in that episode, while later requests wait for another reply. A request on a live summary, fresh or stale, hand-written or not, becomes a new summary over the same chain (level 1) or over the best fresh summary of each part (condensation); the old one stays. A generated source yields to its replacement, including through generated ancestors; a hand-written source stays selected and is marked stale when its inputs changed, with a replacement available in Revisions. The panel disables child reruns beneath a selected manual ancestor, since that protected context would stay unchanged; an existing request remains cancelable. A request on a failed summary, or on a job that gave up, retries its range (its chain, or its recorded parts), bypassing the standing failure. The job takes the request's model over the chat's bindings. The enqueue withdraws the request in the same transaction, when it makes the answering job; an unrelated overlapping job leaves the request pending; requests for summaries not on the path being planned wait. The planner never starts a generation from the click, and no panel action does.
- *Bounded planning.* A plan asks the writer for at most four jobs of each kind, and `EnqueueSummaries` works from the chunks and their neighbours, not the chat's length (bounded chain reads, a coverage check through `summary_inputs_slot`, a depth-overlap check against live jobs, a bounded search for the pinned reply), so a 100,000-message chat's first plan is a write of tens of milliseconds and the rest of the backfill follows as jobs finish. The reply that triggered a plan must lie within 5,000 slots above the leaf (a walk up by their depth difference, a few steps in practice). With no model to summarize with (no `summarizer` or narrator binding and no default, or one that is gone) nothing is planned and `SummaryPlan.blocked` is `no_model`, for the UI to show.
- *Tests.* `lcmMaintenance.test.ts` runs the planner over hand-built summaries; `lcmCondense.test.ts` runs condensation, refusal of one, absorption, the cost bound (many edits under summaries, then one pass writes each affected summary once, and a second plans nothing), flagging, and the pool reacting to a reply and not to an edit; `lcmScale.test.ts` keeps the enqueue write of a 100,000-message chat under 300 ms and checks the no-model state. The model test runs real condensations with a fanout of two, edits, swipes, deletes and hand-written summaries, and a cascade (reply pass, jobs, completion passes) per `maintain` step and at the end for four paths; the oracle, written apart from the SQL, asserts at quiescence that every stale summary a context holds is hand-written, built over one, empty, or held by a standing failure, that no job re-summarizes a fresh summary, that each affected summary is written again at most once per reply, and that a finished job starts no level-1 re-summarization. Disabling the re-summarizing makes it fail.

**Idle work, batches and overflow.** Reply completion coalesces planning for 250 ms. Noninteractive jobs are submitted for idle admission. Claiming compares the pinned prefix with the last request that reached the provider, including connection/model identity and TTL, and demotes cold jobs before ordering the queue. Starting the rollout rechecks the fully assembled prefix to cover expiry or identity changes during assembly; interactive escalation is preserved. This also applies after restart. Startup resumes queued work and plans enabled active paths. "When it runs" is the chat's `when`, else the account default's (`spendAhead` its old name): with the next reply, speculative noninteractive claims are held, including restarted jobs; right after a reply, they run while the cache is warm. The claim reads the chat's newest chat-wide `lcm.settings` in its query; `PlanSummaries` reports it as `spendAhead`. Explicit reruns and retries have durable `explicit_requested` admission and run with the next reply even with spending ahead off; the permission follows generated ancestor repairs. A matching pending automatic job takes the request's admission, source pins and model in the enqueue transaction, without a duplicate job; a running job leaves the request for the frozen episode's next pass. An overflowing send plans/escalates the covering job and waits with one 120-second deadline. Stopping the send stops only its wait. No covering job or an expired deadline proceeds with explicit omitted ranges. A failed job never displaces originals that fit. Ordinary assembly withholds batch members until all jobs terminate; overflow can use completed members immediately. Summary requests use completed predecessors as continuity.

**Enabling and backfill** (slice 6; `db/lcmEnableReads.ts`, `db/lcmEnableWrites.ts`, `db/lcmImported.ts`, `db/lcmBackfill.ts`, `lcm/backfill.ts`, `api/lcm.ts`, `EnableLcmDialog.tsx`). Everything here follows spec/LCM.md §2.7 and §2.11. Enabling reads and writes; only the next reply, or the one click "Summarize now", plans work.

- **The offer** (`GetLcmEnableOffer`, from the reader's `EstimateBackfill`). `importedSummary` finds the chat's `imported_summary` note, takes its text on the path shown (`noteAt`: the newest revision saved on a message of the path) and its `covers`, and accepts it only if the run lies on that path, starts at the chat's first message and holds no merge or generating slot, and the chat has no summaries yet. It reads counts of the run, never its text. The dialog then offers *keep the imported summary as the first level over messages 1–N* or *summarize from scratch*; a chat without such a summary has the estimate only. The same dialog opens from the switch in the chat settings and from the "Older messages beyond the context aren't summarized" notice, which a chat with LCM off shows once its context has dropped messages. A chat with no messages enables directly.
- **The write.** Enable and Continue carry the figures displayed to the user. The server recomputes the choice's estimate and rejects any increase in billed tokens or cost (or a different cost currency) with `LcmEstimateChanged` and the new figures. The dialog reloads the offer and requires another click; Continue reloads the remaining estimate. A successful write stores the seen allowance, never a higher replacement. Adoption is prepared from the reader's owner-scoped `ReadLcmImported` in `StageLcmEnable` transactions of at most 500 slots: gather the primary-parent chain and selected versions, create an unsealed imported L1, then copy its inputs in order. The input trigger checks the indexed final ordinal and predecessor instead of recounting every input; sealing counts once. `EnableLcm` publishes the run, seals the summary, writes the switch and records its backfill in one short transaction. Context and message reads apply the run's visibility overlay only after publication; the fork-owned `hide_range` override publishes the visibility change, and later individual visibility edits win by sequence. Scratch uses the same staged unhide without a summary. Incomplete or abandoned runs are invisible; a later attempt cancels the old staging run. Originals and the imported note's text remain intact; the imported summary notes switched on at the leaf (`summaryNotesOn` in the offer, `summaryNoteOn` in `LcmEnableOffer`) are switched off by `EnableLcm` in the same transaction, with ordinary `note.deactivate` events at the leaf, for both choices (Q4); the dialog says so, and the user can switch the note back on in the Notes tab, after which it is sent beside the summaries. Enabling spends nothing.

- **The estimate** (`lcm/backfill.ts`, pure). From the planner's rules: recent messages end under the midpoint of their fitted pair, a pass plans at most four chunks of `leafInput` tokens (plus half a message) from what lies before the target, and condenses the runs of `fanout` summaries of a level the layer holds when it plans (lowest level first, at most four), as `planMaintenance` does; its chunks run first, then its condensations, and passes go on until neither is left. It simulates the layer job by job, each summary with the `<summary>` tags and message it's sent in (`layerTokens`). A summary request sends the prefix, the layer and its chunk; a condensation sends the prefix and the layer up to its last part. Without the preset the instruction takes the prefix's place at the start (the cached part is the instruction and the summaries) and nothing follows the chunk. With a cache lifetime (`ModelInput.cacheTtlS`) a request reads back the prefix and the summaries it shares with the request before from the start, and sends the rest, its chunk and the instruction (80 tokens) in full; the first request pays for the prefix and the layer. A price with cache writes charges that part as writes. Without a cache lifetime everything is sent in full. Output is `leafTarget` per summary and `condenseTarget` per condensation. The reader measures the path (tokens and messages after the summaries' cursor, as the planner costs them), assembles a context as if LCM were on for the window, the layer maximum and the prefix tokens (the request's messages that are neither history nor summaries, nor sent only for a reply: conditioned prompts and the user turn that ends a reply's request, which a summary request doesn't send), and reads the model's recent output speed from the ledger for the duration. The simulation retains each planned request. The main thread runs `Providers.estimateCost` separately for each request and sums the results, including a judge request for every generated summary when a judge is chosen. Flat fees and input-size tiers therefore price each request independently. Unavailable or mixed-currency costs remain unpriced. The forecast assumes ordinary output and cache behavior; retries, cold cache and longer output can hold work sooner. `blocked` says no model is chosen to summarize with, or the window leaves no room for two chunks. Worked example (`lcmBackfillEstimate.test.ts`): a 750k-token story, 6k prefix, 4,000-token chunks comes to about 190 summaries and 45 condensations, 0.95M tokens sent in full, 2.4M read from cache and 0.15M returned.
- **The backfill is a `jobs` row** (kind `lcm-backfill`, status `processing` while live, `complete` when over; migration `0030_lcm_backfill` indexes it by chat). Its payload holds the choice, the time the user approved, the approved billed tokens and money, the cap, and the flags `armed`, `paused`, `capped`, `drive`. It holds no progress: what is left is estimated again from the chat when the panel shows it. It survives restarts.
  - *Lazily.* It is `armed` until a reply's planning or "Summarize now" begins it; until then neither startup nor a finished job plans it (`PlanSummaries` returns nothing for an armed backfill unless the reason is a reply), so enabling a 100,000-message chat spends nothing. After that the existing planner does the work in its bounded batches (four chunks, then the completion pass continues), at the idle or background class, so the long job is a series of short writes.
  - *Summarize now* (`SetLcmBackfill`) sets `drive` and plans once like a finished job would. A driven backfill's jobs run although the user's `spendAhead` is off (claims check `drive`), since the click was the user's call. Pausing sets `paused`; Continue clears `paused` and `capped` and plans again.
  - *The cap.* Every provider attempt is admitted atomically by `OpenLlmRequest` before dispatch, after payload transforms. A reservation bounds prompt tokens conservatively by wire UTF-8 bytes plus framing (images also reserve the known context limit) and output by the wire maximum. The built-in cost bound takes the highest applicable input/cache and output rates across tiers, without assuming cache hits; custom formulas supply `upperBound`, and a priced request without a bound is held. Admission compares completed spend plus all in-flight reservations plus the new bound against the approved caps. A request that does not fit is never dispatched and marks the backfill capped. This boundary also covers concurrent leaf/condensation work, provider retries, fallback models, judge retries, every additional tool turn, reply-authorized continuations after recovery, and explicit reruns even with spending ahead off. Closing the ledger row reconciles to reported usage; interrupted or unreported work retains its reservation because it may have been billed. Reservations persist in migration `0032_lcm_request_budget`. The panel counts decisions attributed to summary/condensation rollouts, excluding narrator decisions on the same chat. A held job is released without consuming a job attempt. Claims remain blocked while paused or capped. Sends recheck held state on every poll, so a pause or cap reached during an existing wait releases the send. Continue revalidates the displayed remaining figures before approving its next allowance.

  - *Done.* A planning pass that finds nothing to plan and no live job ends the backfill (`SetBackfill finish`), so the cap stops applying to the chat's ordinary summarizing.
- **Operations.** Writer: `StageLcmEnable`, `EnableLcm`, `SetBackfill` (pause, continue, begin, drive, finish). Reader: `ReadLcmImported`, `EstimateBackfill`, `GetBackfill`. RPCs: `GetLcmEnableOffer`, `EnableLcm`, `GetLcmBackfill`, `SetLcmBackfill`; each acts as the caller and reaches the chat through its owner, with cross-user probes in the isolation matrix (RPCs, and their operations as storage probes). `PlanSummaries` also returns `liveJobs` and the backfill's `armed`, `held`, `drive` and `settled` flags.
- **Summary panel.** A *Backfill* section while a backfill is live: its state (waiting for the next reply, in progress, paused, stopped at the limit), what is left with its estimate, what has been spent against the limit, and Summarize now, Pause and Continue. The summary the panel shows from ST carries the "Imported from SillyTavern" badge.
- **Tests.** `lcmBackfillEstimate.test.ts` checks the simulation, the 128-slot chunk bound, and worked example; `lcmBackfill.test.ts` exercises quiet enable, conservation, estimates, pause, Continue and driven work through synthetic HTTP imports. Its accuracy comparison uses an explicitly generous allowance to finish despite conservative reservations; the cap assertions forbid overspend. Review reproductions are regular tests: `lcmBackfillReview.test.ts` covers cold cache/maximum output and costly refusal-judge attempts; `lcmBackfillFormula.test.ts` covers per-request flat fees, provider retries and extra tool turns; `lcmBackfillTransitions.test.ts` covers concurrent leaf/condensation work, changed-price approval and both pause/cap transitions during a real send; `lcmBackfillContinuation.test.ts` exercises explicit imported-summary reruns and their recovered fifth bounded part against the same reservation gate; `lcmBudgetLedger.test.ts` checks atomic in-flight admission, reconciliation, crash exposure and narrator isolation. `lcmAdoptionScale.test.ts` HTTP-imports 100,000 synthetic messages with 99,999 hidden, verifies staging stays invisible, retains every input and makes no provider calls, and bounds every adoption write at 300 ms. `e2e/lcmEnable.spec.ts` covers the browser import, offer, choices, panel and quiet enabling. New reader/writer operations have cross-user isolation probes.

- **Imported instruction.** The Summarize extension’s prompt is the user’s default leaf-summary instruction when no default was chosen (§13). Chat `lcm.settings.tail` takes precedence, then `generation.lcm.tail`, then the built-in instruction (a new chat copies the account's when it is set). `{{words}}` expands to the job’s target; other text stays literal. Settings → Chats exposes the default instruction with an explicit Save and an empty value restores the built-in default. `lcmImportPrompt.test.ts` imports a synthetic ST folder through HTTP and asserts the instruction at the mock provider, retained settings, chat overrides and edits surviving re-import. Backfill covers the path shown; other branches get theirs when visited and enabled.

**Images.** The common provider boundary caps contexts and final wire payloads at the last `maxImages` images, including summary and tool requests. This model setting defaults to four when unknown. Limiting preserves inspector source markers. Version image storage, reply transport and the composer/viewer are implemented in §12 (U15/U16). Summary requests (U22) use the selected versions' image references already pinned by assembly; `lcmPath` also tracks image-only versions as real inputs and budgets their `[Image]` marker. Its image metadata, which planning's image estimates read, leaves out the images the fork took out, looked up as assembly looks them up (`image_exclude` on the shown version's original); `test/lcmImageExclusion.test.ts` checks the metadata and each fork's summary request in two forks, the second no longer skipped as already summarized. Immediately before dispatch, the summarizer resolves only admitted images with the job owner's media read and asynchronous file I/O, using the same helper as replies. Both direct and tool styles, with and without the preset, send the range's images; tool follow-ups retain the hydrated initial payload. Stored snapshots keep references, never image bytes. A text-only model refuses images without calling a caption model. `test/lcmImages.test.ts` checks all four style/preset combinations against a mock HTTP provider, including an image-only input, an image outside the range, last-N selection, tool follow-up turns and byte-free snapshots.

**Images in freshness** (`db/lcmImages.ts`). An image taken out of a fork's context, by the user's toggle or by a rule, must not reach that fork through a summary written in another fork where it was sent. A level-1 summary records what it was written with in `summaries.images_json`: `{limit, sent, withheld}`, the media ids its request carried in order, the image limit they were cut to, and the inputs' images its fork had taken out (`withheld`: those none of whose places among the inputs was left in). `CompleteSummary` takes them from the frozen request (`requestImages`: the newest `maxImages` of the request's images, as `hydrateMessageImages` sends them, and the taken-out images of the history read pinned with it), so a toggle during the request can't change what is recorded. A completion without a frozen request, a failed row and an adopted imported summary record what their fork sends for the range, uncut (`shownImages`; the enable run gathers the imported range's images into `lcm_enable_runs.images_json`). The column is NULL when the inputs' versions have no image attached. `readSummary` then counts an L1 whose inputs match as fresh only when `imagesMatch`: the fork sends every image the summary carried (its images for those inputs, less the ones it took out, `image_exclude` looked up as assembly does), and none of those it sends under the recorded limit is one the summary withheld. Without a plugin's own image rule this is exactly "the fork sends the images it was written with". A plugin stage or transform that drops images from a summary request, or adds one of its own (only the inputs' own images, less the withheld ones, are recorded as sent), leaves the summary fresh where it was written, so no rule can make one be planned again on every reply. So a summary written where an image was sent is stale in a fork that took it out, a summary written without an image is stale where it is sent again, and putting an image back makes the fork's old summary stale and another fork's matching one fresh again. Because every freshness reader goes through `readSummary`, the layer walk, completed coverage, failed holds, the planner's re-summarizing and `EnqueueSummaries`'s "already summarized" check all treat such a summary as not covering the fork: its enqueue over the same inputs makes its own job, and a stale used summary is rewritten on the fork's next reply by the existing rule. Condensations carry no images and are stale when any part is. The maintenance episode's L1 signature includes each slot's sent images, so a completion pass can't repair an image change made after the authorizing reply. Cost: a NULL column reads nothing more; otherwise one indexed probe (`fork_overrides_chat` is on `(chat_id, kind)`) finds whether the chat has any `image_exclude` row, and only then is the image list read. Override rows are never deleted short of the chat, so with none, every fork sends what each summary was written with. A no-image flow's provider requests are byte-identical to before. The model's image limit is part of what was recorded, so changing the summarizer model's `maxImages` doesn't make summaries stale, as changing the model doesn't. Freshness knows only the built-in exclusion: a plugin rule that leaves an image out in one fork and not another isn't seen, so a summary written where the plugin sent it can be used where it doesn't. `test/lcmSummaryImages.test.ts` runs real summary requests through the workers and the mock provider: two forks sharing inputs with an image taken out in one each get their own fresh summary, the first fork's summary request and next reply never carry the image while the other's request does; putting it back; a summary rewritten on the fork's next reply; the limit rule; a plugin stage sending only each message's first image, and one adding an image from outside the inputs, whose summaries stay fresh and unplanned where they were written; an image taken out of one message but sent with another; and a chat without images recording nothing. `test/lcmImportedSummary.test.ts` checks an adopted summary's images and its staleness where one is taken out.

**Job oracles.** The graph model includes enqueue, claim/run, failure and release, checking batch visibility and rendered version pins. Queue tests cover duplicates, serial order, claim fencing, drain refunds, concurrent edits, oversized messages and replay. Real HTTP mocks exercise both styles and compare their cached prefix through the layer with the reply, including idle class and ledger attribution. An after-commit/before-acknowledgement writer-crash test verifies recovery at a new epoch, a refunded attempt and at most one concurrent provider call. A failed-gap sequence bounds paid requests across repeated replies, and a TTL-expired job is demoted across restart. Static examples at the output-budget boundary and random macros are byte-compared at the mock provider for both styles. New owned operations have isolation probes in both directions. Review mutations removing queue ownership, substituting the job's owner during assembly, and selecting batches early are all detected.

**Per-fork summaries** (fork independence unit 7). `EditSummary` and `SelectSummaryRevision` write overrides, never the summary's shared selection. Absorption records a `summary_superseded` override instead of globally superseding material another fork may use. `SetSummaryRerun` creates an immutable request in `summary_reruns`, referenced by a `summary_rerun` override; cancelling or consuming it writes a local withdrawal. A fork made while a request stands keeps its request after the parent consumes or cancels it. Revision lists keep all historical texts available, and mark the viewed fork's selection. Summary drafts and their browser copies are keyed by the viewed fork, captured when the editor opens; unnamed legacy API calls retain the unscoped draft key. Freshness compares effective inputs in that fork, including replacements: `summaries_done` preserves a coordinated summary/history replacement while an ordinary replacement or a later amend makes coverage stale. `completedCoverage` uses this same freshness test. Coordinated replacements require the changeset’s preserved input identity and amend count; a later rename cannot conceal an earlier amend or message-only edit. Parent and child summaries replaced together must also share the application call, not just a reused group. Integration tests cover a coordinated replacement preserving a sibling’s summary text, revision and freshness, and replacement of a fork’s own edited summary followed by an amend. Completed coverage and failed holds also require the whole summary chain to be on the planned path; sharing its first slot does not cover a divergent sibling. The enqueue duplicate guard checks the summary endpoint against the planning leaf through chain segments before treating overlapping work as redundant. The inspector's exact historical request stays frozen; its changed-since marks compare summaries in the generating rollout's fork. `forkSummaries.test.ts` covers edits, choices, bounded inheritance, requests and consumption, freshness, same-tip coverage, separate queue claims, restart positions and wrong-chat fork rejection. `forkSummaryJobs.test.ts` checks context and summary-request text in both forks at the same tip, distinct instructions and continuity, job completion, rollout provenance, and planning and enqueue admission past completed or failed summaries on divergent sibling paths against synthetic fixtures and a synthetic provider. The desktop and phone inspector browser tests also switch the current fork, edit its summary, and check that historical text and changed-since marks retain the generating fork. The independent graph oracle (`test/protocol/lcm.model.test.ts`) snapshots revision selections and absorption at fork creation and evaluates queued work in its own fork; selecting a summary’s own generated revision clears a redirect without preferring it over newer fresh coverage. Browser fixtures use fork-scoped drafts and active rerun reads.

**Summary search navigation.** The registered History edits panel opens a matched summary in the registered Summaries panel and focuses its level/range row, including summaries outside the selected layer. Previous/Next uses the hit's kind to choose summary opening or message navigation. The search stays mounted while switching tabs, retaining its query. `e2e/qaAttribution.spec.ts` covers result clicks, focus and Next at desktop and phone widths.

**Summary UI** (slice 7; `@rp/shared/lcm.ts`, `api/lcm.ts`, `db/lcmUiReads.ts`, `db/lcmUiWrites.ts`, `client/src/features/chat/`). Everything here reads or edits; nothing starts a generation.

- **Reads.** `GetLcmState(chat, leaf)` returns the chat's LCM switch, the selected layer in path order (with text), the failed summaries and the pending, running and failed jobs on that path, and the cursor depth; with LCM off it is empty. `GetLcmCoverage(chat, leaf, fromDepth, toDepth)` returns every sealed, unsuperseded summary on the path overlapping a depth range, whatever its level and whether or not the layer uses it, without text, capped at 600; the rail asks for the loaded window only. `ListSummaryRevisions` lists a summary's revisions and completed linked replacements. Successful fresh coverage resolves old failures in state and coverage reads; the rows remain in storage and can still be read directly. The divider counts each failing level/range once, and the panel avoids showing a terminal attempt twice. Message numbers (`from`, `to`) are one-based path depths, as in the inspector. Each is owner-scoped through the chat and probed in the isolation matrix.
- **Writes.** The summary text edit is the existing `EditSummary` and `SelectSummaryRevision`. The draft behind an edit is the existing `summary_edit` draft, now reachable through `GetSummaryDraft`, `SaveSummaryDraft`, `SummaryDraftHistory`, `RestoreSummaryDraft` and `DiscardSummaryDraft` (thin handlers over the writer's draft operations, keyed by the summary and viewed fork). `SetLcmEnabled` writes a new fork-scoped `lcm.settings` event keeping the other settings. `SetSummaryRerun` records or withdraws a request, in `summary_reruns` (migration `0026`), naming a summary or a job that gave up; a trigger keeps it in its own chat and rows cascade with the chat or target. **A request is only stored state**: the click never plans, enqueues or calls a provider. Planning consumes it (see *Rerun requests* under condensation and stale summaries).
- **Events.** The `Lcm` chat event means "read `GetLcmState` again". It is published when a summary is edited, a revision selected, a request set, the switch moved, and by the summarizer when it enqueues, claims or finishes a job (it takes `ChatHub`, so the server wires `ChatHub` last). The client also reads the layer again after every `Cache` event, which follows any edit, swipe, delete or hide, and on a leaf change, a reconnect or a `State` event. Every completed state read advances the LCM event version, even when the selected layer is unchanged, so focused rail summaries refresh their metadata, text and rerun/cancel state.
- **Boundary divider** (`LcmBoundary.tsx`). With LCM on, the message list holds one extra row before the first uncovered message: "Older messages: N summaries, ~X tokens" with stale, failed, to-redo, queued and running ("Summarizing messages 40–52…") badges. With nothing summarized yet it sits at the top. It is shown only when the loaded window contains the boundary. It opens the panel. In originals mode the list's rows are the entries plus this row; the virtualizer keys it as `lcm-boundary`, so the scroll anchoring of §12 is unchanged (the perf harness checks 0 px anchor error across it).
- **Timeline mode** (`TimelineToggle.tsx`, `TimelineSummary.tsx`, `ChatPage.tsx`). The divider and Summaries tab both offer “Summaries in chat”, saved per account (`timeline`, default `originals`). Originals mode retains the full paged message timeline. Summaries mode substitutes the selected `GetLcmState.layer` in path order for covered messages, labels each summary with its level and message range, and retains raw messages after the cursor. The layer's prefix is visible even when its originals are outside the loaded window, without fetching those originals. Each summary expands inline: higher levels read their direct children with `GetSummary` and restore the pinned revision text from `ListSummaryRevisions` if a child was edited, level 1 reads original messages with `ReadPath` in pages of 100 and offers earlier pages inside its range. Expanded originals use the regular message component and actions. Open expansions refresh their loaded pages on the session’s path revision (edits, selected versions, visibility changes and reconnects), including originals outside the main message window, retaining earlier pages already loaded. A message link, including a summary-panel range link, automatically expands the covering summary hierarchy, reads the target’s 100-message page directly, and highlights the original; earlier and later pages remain available inside its range, also after a refresh (which reads backwards from the last loaded original, so the next later page starts after it). A page response is dropped if the expansion was read again meanwhile, by a refresh or another link. The list centers the linked original each time a new article for it mounts (the uncovered original can render before the layer replaces it), and doesn't hold at the end until the user scrolls, taps or types: a collapsed summary can fit the viewport, and holding there would follow its expansion to the bottom. Expansion is local to the viewer, starts no generation, and leaves the composer draft intact. Nested summaries retain stale badges. The virtualizer measures the replacement rows and maps coverage ranges to them. `e2e/chatTimelineReview.spec.ts` covers saved edits, off-window updates with retained pages, and direct and nested links, including summary-panel range navigation, a refreshed linked page's later pages, and a delayed page response after another link.
- **Summary panel** (`SummaryPanel.tsx`, the side panel's Summaries tab, opened from the divider, the rail or the header; with summaries off it offers to turn them on). Work in progress and failed summaries first, then the layer in path order. A row shows level, range (a button that opens the window at its first message, as a message link does; the panel stays open beside the chat, a phone's sheet closes), size, and state badges: stale (an input changed), failed, edited, imported from SillyTavern, cut short, "re-summarizes with the next reply". A level above 1 expands to the summaries it condenses. Actions: Edit, Revisions (use any), Re-summarize (a request, with Cancel), and Retry for a failed job or summary. **Edit** is a text box backed by a server draft (`summaryDraft.ts`): saved 600 ms after typing, the draft shown again after a reload or in another tab, undo through its history and “Earlier draft text” for every retained state, Save revision writes a revision of origin `user` and selects it and ends the draft, Cancel sends the text to the recovered texts. The draft names the id, row version and session it last saw, as every field does (§3.10). As in library fields, every change also leaves a copy in this browser (§3.10 invariant 4, `backup.ts` kind `summary`, the `SaveSummaryDraft` payload as typed), replacing only this edit's copy not on its way to the server; a save's success deletes the copy it sent and re-files what was typed since on the new base, and a failure keeps it. Closing the edit (or the panel) saves what's left without dropping the copy, and opening the edit replays the summary's copies with their bases, showing what didn't reach the server in the box with a note. As for message editors (§12), the base is kept apart from the draft last read: text shown from a refused copy keeps that copy's draft, row version, session and revision until the server takes it, and replays and saves run one at a time. Save revision drops only the copy of the text it saved; text typed meanwhile keeps the edit open, its copy re-filed on the revision it was typed from. Cancel refuses while the server doesn't have the text. `e2e/lcm.spec.ts` covers both. Editing a summary leaves it as written even when its messages change; the choice to replace it is the user's.
- **Per-summary provenance.** `summary.generation-details` registers a Details control through `summaryPreviews`, including rows read from coverage. The owner-scoped reader operation and RPC `GetSummaryDetails` join the stored summary to its job, original rollout and ordered ledger requests. Details show the historical model, job attempt count, start/finish/duration, per-request uncached input, cache read/write and output counts, usage source and cost/currency. Missing values are labelled Unknown. Editing a summary leaves this original-generation provenance intact. A captured request adds Open request in inspector, opening the registered `/chats/$chatId/summaries/$summaryId/request` route; summary rollouts have no message version. That page displays the sanitized stored request verbatim in sent order, with historical source labels, and links back to the generating fork and summary. No request is rebuilt or provider called. The reader explicitly projects the capture and ledger DTOs, excluding raw usage, transport mapping and message digests. `test/lcmUi.test.ts` covers stored/unknown values, retained provenance after edits and private-field exclusion; the isolation matrix probes the RPC and worker op; `e2e/summaryDetails.spec.ts` exercises Details and captured/uncaptured requests at both widths.
- **Which summary shows.** A user-written or imported selection stays in context after covered edits and queued refreshes, until an explicit revision/replacement choice. Generated summaries prefer freshness, then higher level; after an edit under a generated level-2 summary, fresh level-1 coverage can stand in while its repair waits for an authorized reply episode.
- **Marks** (`Message.tsx`). A message covered by the layer shows "Summarized" with an icon and an accent left edge; a message that fell out of the context stays faded with a dashed border and its own icon; a hidden message keeps the ghost and is dimmed, and never shows as summarized. Icons have accessible names. They read the layer's cursor from `GetLcmState` when it has loaded, otherwise the cache estimate's.
- **Coverage rail** (`CoverageRail.tsx`). A "Coverage rail" switch in the Summaries tab (shown with LCM on), saved in the account's display preferences (`coverageRail`); the old browser-local key is no longer read. One lane per level outward from the messages, a bar per summary positioned from the virtualizer's measurements: solid when the layer uses it, light when a higher level condenses it, hatched when stale, red when failed, pulsing when running, dashed when queued. A click opens the panel at that summary. The rows shift right by the rail's width.
- **Setting.** The side panel's This chat tab has a Summaries section (`ThisChatTab.tsx`, `LcmFields.tsx`): the "Summarize older messages" switch throughout the viewed fork (on for new chats, off for imported ones, and nothing runs until the next reply; turning it on for a chat with messages opens the enable dialog, *Enabling and backfill*); "Send with": the full preset or the instruction and messages only; the instruction in the code editor with explicit Save and "Use account default"; "Recent messages kept as written" and "Summary block", each a target and a maximum in tokens with the current figure ("now 18,250") and a note when the model made them smaller; "When it runs"; "Include notes in summary requests", a switch under "Send with"; and, folded under Advanced, chunk size (empty is automatic) and summary length, saying the instruction asks for it in words and what `{{words}}` is now. Each field is one `ChangeLcmSettings` write, made when a number is left or Enter is pressed. Under each: "Follows the account default", "Same as the account default" (a copy unchanged), or the chat's own value with the default's and "Reset to account default". A maximum lowered to its target or below is written with the target lowered too (`loweredTarget`: the same share of the maximum, in hundreds, below it), and the pair says "Target lowered to N to stay below the maximum." while the target is that. Fit problems are under the pair or field that causes them, in `text-danger`; a refused write's reason is under its field. Settings → Chats → Summaries (`ChatDefaults.tsx`) has the same fields for the account default for new chats, with "New chats summarize older messages", beside the refusal judge, the fallback and the default instruction. The Notes tab's summary note says "Your preset includes this through {{summary}}." when an enabled prompt of the preset uses it (or that it sends nothing while the note is off), and, while the chat summarizes and the note is on and sent, "Also sent beside the summaries."; the preset editor and the chat's Prompt preset tab badge each prompt holding `{{summary}}` with what it fills in ("Summary (from ST) note (~X tokens)", or "Nothing, no summary note is on").
- **Settings tests.** `test/lcmSettings.test.ts`: resolution (chat → account → built-in), a new chat's copy and a later default change, `ChangeLcmSettings` keeping other fields and null falling back, refusals, `spendAhead` read as `when`; a chunk that doesn't fit the band planning nothing without failing; planning at the midpoint and keeping the target, the pairs in the snapshot, settling back under the maximum; scaled pairs with the fit sentences and a reply that still assembles; "when it runs" both ways with the chat's setting over the account's; requests without the preset at the mock provider (instruction first, no preset, continuity, breakpoints, exact rebuild), with the preset unchanged, the instruction's length in words, and the estimate of both. `test/lcmSummaryNotes.test.ts`: summary requests without the chat's notes unless `withNotes`, and a `{{summary}}` prompt reading a conditioned summary note kept out of the cached prefix. `test/lcmPlacement.test.ts`: each summary its own message before the history, a new one after the earlier ones without changing them, and `{{summary}}` reading only the note. `test/lcmDefaultsMigration.test.ts`: migration 0051 on synthetic chats (never changed, switched off, changed once and back, a half-changed pair, a size from the account default, a chat from before). `packages/shared/test/lcmSettings.test.ts`: `loweredTarget`, `summaryWords` and the built-ins. `test/lcmImportedSummary.test.ts` imports synthetic folders: injected (on, placed, sent once), only through `{{summary}}` (on, unplaced, sent once by the macro, empty when switched off), the extension disabled (off, sent nowhere), enabling switching the note off for both choices, imported chats' copies and re-import keeping a chat's settings. `writeRealisticStFolder(root, { summary })` writes the realistic folder with the summary injected (default), sent only through the selected preset's `{{summary}}`, or with the extension disabled, all counts unchanged. `e2e/lcmSettings.spec.ts` (desktop and phone): every field saved and read back after a reload, reset, misfits, a maximum lowered past its target, the notes switch, Advanced, Settings → Chats defaults copied into a new chat, the Notes tab's line, the badge, the enable dialog switching the note off, and the note switched back on saying it's also sent beside the summaries.
- **Context view.** Summary sources already read "Summary, level N, messages A–B, revision R", with stale and changed-since marks (§15); a browser test pins it.
- **Tests.** `test/lcmUi.test.ts` (real server): the layer, coverage windows, revisions, drafts, requests that start no job, jobs on other branches, the switch keeping its other settings, the table's triggers, and the `Lcm` event; isolation probes for every new RPC. `e2e/lcm.spec.ts` (Chromium, desktop and phone, summaries written by `e2e/support/summaries.ts` straight into the running server's database): divider position, marks, panel, children, jump, edit through a draft across a reload, revisions, stale and re-summarize with no job or provider request, failed work and retry, the rail, the switch and the context view. The perf harness has a 20,000-message chat with 1,125 level-1 summaries under three more levels (the layer is nine level-4 summaries): opening at the boundary, with the rail on, then forty pages scrolled up across it (frame p95 17 to 33 ms, no long frames over 100 ms, 0 px anchor error at a load average near 12). The fixture chats stand for imported ones with LCM off, and the user default `spendAhead` is off: with LCM on, a 100,000-message chat made the first reply plan its whole backfill in one `EnqueueSummaries` transaction that held the writer for many minutes, and the 20,000-message chat's startup plan held it for 31 s (found by this scenario; the planner's limits are for slices 4–5 and the backfill for slice 6). `GetLcmState` costs about 0.65 s on that chat, like `GetNextContext`, from validating the layer's lineage.

The review regressions are regular tests in `lcmReview.test.ts`, `lcmReviewScale.test.ts`, the client failure-count test and `e2e/lcm.spec.ts`: six layout/layer multi-send prefix comparisons, manual-selection races and explicit replacement choices, bounded 100,000-slot hidden/deleted/empty prefixes and restore lineage, stale batches across restart and later edits, condensed-child reruns with spending ahead off, explicit refusal retries, resolved failure marks, and rail request/cancel/text invalidation.
